## A

### [ability](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ability_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈbɪləti/` · **US:** `/əˈbɪləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abi/abili/ability__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abi/abili/ability__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - People with the disease may lose their ability to communicate.
  - This program has the ability to adapt to its user.
  - Students must demonstrate the ability to understand simple texts.
  - She has an uncanny ability to predict what consumers will want.
  - A gentle form of exercise will increase your ability to relax.

### [able](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/able_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɪbl/` · **US:** `/ˈeɪbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abl/able_/able__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abl/able_/able__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You must be able to speak French for this job.
  - A viral illness left her barely able to walk.
  - We're still able to get visas to come and go from Thailand.
  - I didn't feel able to disagree with him.
  - Will you be able to come?
  - Are you really willing and able to do what is necessary?
  - They don't even seem able to see what's good about their ideas.
  - These families are less able to afford a balanced, healthy diet.

### [abroad](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/abroad) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈbrɔːd/` · **US:** `/əˈbrɔːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/abr/abroa/abroad__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/abr/abroa/abroad__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to go/travel/live/study abroad
  - She worked abroad for a year.
  - imports of cheap food from abroad
  - He was famous, both at home and abroad (= in his own country and in other countries).
  - For a holiday abroad you need a valid passport.

### [accept](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/accept) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əkˈsept/` · **US:** `/əkˈsept/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/accep/accept__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/accep/accept__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He asked me to marry him and I accepted.
  - accept something Please accept our sincere apologies.
  - It was pouring with rain so I accepted his offer of a lift.
  - I am unfortunately unable to accept your kind invitation.
  - The board of directors accepted our proposal.
  - She's decided not to accept the job.
  - Please accept this small token of my appreciation.
  - accept something from somebody He is charged with accepting bribes from a firm of suppliers.
  - accept something for something She said she'd accept $15 for it.

### [accident](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/accident) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæksɪdənt/` · **US:** `/ˈæksɪdənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/accid/accident__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/accid/accident__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a car/road/traffic accident
  - in an accident He was killed in an accident.
  - One in seven accidents is caused by sleepy drivers.
  - The accident happened at 3 p.m.
  - to have an accident
  - a serious/minor accident
  - a fatal accident (= in which somebody is killed)
  - Every year in the United Kingdom more than 4 000 people die in accidents in the home.
  - a climbing/riding accident
  - Take out accident insurance before you go on your trip.
  - I didn't mean to break it—it was an accident.
  - Their boat sank in a freak accident.
  - His son died in a tragic cycling accident.
  - There are a number of accident blackspots (= places where lots of accidents happen) on that road.
  - We don't know the cause of the accident.
  - He was badly injured in a motorcycle accident.

### [according to](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/according-to) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/əˈkɔːdɪŋ tu/` · **US:** `/əˈkɔːrdɪŋ tu/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/acc/accor/according_to_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/acc/accor/according_to_1_us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - According to Mick, it's a great movie.
  - You've been absent six times according to our records.

### [achieve](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/achieve) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtʃiːv/` · **US:** `/əˈtʃiːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ach/achie/achieve__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ach/achie/achieve__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He had finally achieved success.
  - They have both achieved great results.
  - She has worked hard to achieve her goal.
  - to achieve an objective/aim
  - They could not achieve their target of less than 3% inflation.
  - The firm has achieved the status of undisputed market leader.
  - achieve something through (doing) something Course objectives were achieved through a range of activities.

### [act](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/act_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ækt/` · **US:** `/ækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/act/act__/act__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/act/act__/act__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - act to do something It is vital that we act to stop the destruction of the rainforests.
  - + adv./prep. By acting quickly doctors saved her life.
  - The government was criticized for failing to act decisively.
  - Initial reports indicated he acted alone.
  - He claims he acted in self-defence.
  - We acted in good faith (= believing that we were doing the right thing).
  - They are acting out of self-interest.
  - She was not acting for personal gain.

### [active](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/active_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæktɪv/` · **US:** `/ˈæktɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/act/activ/active__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/act/activ/active__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Staying physically active in later years can also keep you feeling younger.
  - Before our modern age, people had a more physical and active lifestyle.

### [actually](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/actually) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæktʃuəli/` · **US:** `/ˈæktʃuəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/act/actua/actually__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/act/actua/actually__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What did she actually say?
  - It's not actually raining now.
  - The book was never actually published.
  - I'm glad we were actually able to get the message across.
  - That's the only reason I'm actually going.
  - There are lots of people there who can actually help you.
  - I didn't want to say anything without actually reading the letter first.

### [adult](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/adult_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈdʌlt/` · **US:** `/ˈædʌlt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adu/adult/adult__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adu/adult/adult__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - preparing young people for adult life
  - He was a healthy young adult male.
  - the adult population
  - adult monkeys
  - She spent her entire adult life caring for others.

### [advantage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advantage_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ədˈvɑːntɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ədˈvæntɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/advan/advantage__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/advan/advantage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a distinct/significant/huge advantage
  - It gives you an unfair advantage (= something that benefits you, but not your opponents).
  - She had the advantage of a good education.
  - You will be at an advantage (= have an advantage) in the interview if you have thought about the questions in advance.
  - advantage over somebody The company was able to gain a competitive advantage over its rivals by reducing costs.
  - Being tall gave him an advantage over the other players.

### [adventure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/adventure) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ədˈventʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ədˈventʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/adven/adventure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/adven/adventure__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - her adventures travelling in Africa
  - adventure stories
  - Four members of our staff have embarked on the adventure of a lifetime.
  - They fly around in their rocket ship and go on exciting adventures.
  - When you're a child, life is one big adventure.
  - Popper described science as the greatest adventure in the world.

### [advertise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advertise) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈædvətaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈædvərtaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/adver/advertise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/adver/advertise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If you want to attract customers you need to advertise.
  - to advertise on TV/online/on social media
  - advertise something to advertise a product/service
  - a glossy brochure advertising holidays in the Maldives
  - How to Advertise a Business for Free on the Internet
  - I bought the camera and case as advertised in the catalogue.
  - advertise something as something The cruise was advertised as the ‘journey of a lifetime’.

### [advertisement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advertisement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ədˈvɜːtɪsmənt/` · **US:** `/ˌædvərˈtaɪzmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/adver/advertisement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/adver/advertisement__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a newspaper/television advertisement
  - an online advertisement
  - You can place an advertisement on a classifieds website.
  - advertisement for something Television and radio refused to carry advertisements for the album.

### [advertising](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/advertising) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈædvətaɪzɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈædvərtaɪzɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/adv/adver/advertising__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/adv/adver/advertising__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Cigarette advertising has been banned.
  - radio/TV/online advertising
  - Paid advertising on social media is an assured way to reach out to customers.
  - The company's print and digital advertising rose compared with the same period a year ago.
  - a career in advertising
  - Val works for an advertising agency (= a company that designs advertisements).
  - A good advertising campaign will increase our sales.
  - She is a successful advertising executive.
  - We were very happy with our advertising strategy last year.

### [affect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/affect) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈfekt/` · **US:** `/əˈfekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aff/affec/affect__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aff/affec/affect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - How will these changes affect us?
  - The article deals with issues affecting the lives of children.
  - Thousands of people have been adversely affected (= affected in a negative way) by the decision.
  - The quality and health of the soil directly affects the quality and health of the plants.
  - Your opinion will not affect my decision.
  - The south of the country was worst affected by the drought.

### [after](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/after_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːftə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈæftər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aft/after/after__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aft/after/after__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That was in 1996. Soon after, I heard that he'd died.
  - I could come next week, or the week after.
  - And they all lived happily ever after.

### [after](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/after_2) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːftə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈæftər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aft/after/after__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aft/after/after__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll call you after I've spoken to them.
  - Several years after they'd split up they met again by chance in Paris.

### [against](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/against) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/əˈɡeɪnst/` · **US:** `/əˈɡeɪnst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aga/again/against__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aga/again/against__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the fight against terrorism
  - We're playing against the league champions next week.
  - We were rowing against the current.
  - That's against the law.
  - She was forced to marry against her will.
  - Are you for or against the death penalty?
  - She is against seeing (= does not want to see) him.
  - I'd advise you against doing that.

### [ah](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ah_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/ɑː/` · **US:** `/ɑː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ah_/ah__g/ah__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ah_/ah__u/ah__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Ah, there you are!
  - Ah, this coffee is good.
  - Ah well, better luck next time.
  - Ah, but that may not be true.

### [airline](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/airline) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeəlaɪn/` · **US:** `/ˈerlaɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/air/airli/airline__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/air/airli/airline__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an airline pilot/passenger
  - a low-cost/budget/no-frills/discount airline
  - international/major airlines
  - He needed to buy an airline ticket immediately.
  - The company is looking to expand into the commercial airline industry.

### [alive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaɪv/` · **US:** `/əˈlaɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ali/alive/alive__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ali/alive/alive__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Is your mother still alive?
  - Doctors kept the baby alive for six weeks.
  - She had to steal food just to stay alive.
  - He was buried alive in the earthquake.
  - The police are desperate to catch this man dead or alive.
  - We don't know whether he's alive or dead.

### [all](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/all_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːl/` · **US:** `/ɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/all__/all__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/all__/all__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was dressed all in white.
  - He lives all alone.
  - The coffee went all over my skirt.

### [all right](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/all-right_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːl ˈraɪt/` · **US:** `/ɔːl ˈraɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/all_r/all_right_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/all_r/all_right_1_us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Is the coffee all right?
  - Are you getting along all right in your new job?
  - ‘They're off to Spain next week.’ ‘It's all right for some, isn't it?’ (= some people are lucky)
  - ‘How was your trip?’ ‘All right.’

### [all right](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/all-right_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːl ˈraɪt/` · **US:** `/ɔːl ˈraɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/all_r/all_right_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/all_r/all_right_1_us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Is the coffee all right?
  - Are you getting along all right in your new job?
  - ‘They're off to Spain next week.’ ‘It's all right for some, isn't it?’ (= some people are lucky)
  - ‘How was your trip?’ ‘All right.’

### [all right](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/all-right_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːl ˈraɪt/` · **US:** `/ɔːl ˈraɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/all_r/all_right_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/all_r/all_right_1_us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We've got to get up early, all right?

### [allow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/allow) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlaʊ/` · **US:** `/əˈlaʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/all/allow/allow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/all/allow/allow__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - allow somebody/something to do something His parents won't allow him to stay out late.
  - He allowed his mind to wander.
  - The law allowed companies to dismiss workers without any reason.
  - be allowed to do something He is not allowed to stay out late.
  - Students are only allowed to use the equipment under supervision.
  - This research must be allowed to continue.
  - allow yourself to do something He refused to allow himself to be kept quiet.
  - She won't allow herself to be dictated to.
  - be allowed Eating is not allowed in the classrooms.
  - He received the maximum sentence allowed by law.
  - allow something We do not allow eating in the classrooms.

### [almost](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/almost) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːlməʊst/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːlməʊst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alm/almos/almost__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alm/almos/almost__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I like almost all of them.
  - They'll eat almost anything.
  - Almost no one (= hardly anyone) believed him.
  - The castle is almost entirely surrounded by water.
  - It's a mistake they almost always make.
  - The painting is almost certainly a forgery.
  - Her handwriting is almost impossible to read.
  - The team faces almost certain defeat in today's match.
  - The workforce has almost doubled in the past ten years.
  - I almost forgot your birthday.
  - Housing has become almost as big a problem as education.
  - The story seems almost too strange to be true.

### [alone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alone) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈləʊn/` · **US:** `/əˈləʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alo/alone/alone__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alo/alone/alone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - not alone in Tom is not alone in finding Rick hard to work with.
  - be alone with somebody/something She did not want to be alone with him.
  - He was afraid of being alone with his thoughts.
  - He lives alone.
  - She was sitting all alone in the hall.
  - Finally the two of us were alone together.
  - I don't like going out alone at night.

### [alone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alone) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈləʊn/` · **US:** `/əˈləʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alo/alone/alone__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alo/alone/alone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - not alone in Tom is not alone in finding Rick hard to work with.
  - be alone with somebody/something She did not want to be alone with him.
  - He was afraid of being alone with his thoughts.
  - He lives alone.
  - She was sitting all alone in the hall.
  - Finally the two of us were alone together.
  - I don't like going out alone at night.

### [along](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/along_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlɒŋ/` · **US:** `/əˈlɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alo/along/along__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alo/along/along__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was just walking along singing to myself.
  - He pointed out various landmarks as we drove along.

### [along](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/along_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/əˈlɒŋ/` · **US:** `/əˈlɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alo/along/along__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alo/along/along__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They walked slowly along the road.
  - I looked along the shelves for the book I needed.

### [already](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/already) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːlˈredi/` · **US:** `/ɔːlˈredi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alr/alrea/already__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alr/alrea/already__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Lunch?’ ‘No thanks, I've already eaten.’
  - We got there early but Mike had already left.
  - By 2015, filming of the sequel was already under way.
  - Much of what he said I knew already.

### [alternative](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/alternative_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːlˈtɜːnətɪv/` · **US:** `/ɔːlˈtɜːrnətɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alt/alter/alternative__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alt/alter/alternative__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The car is too expensive so we're trying to find a cheaper alternative.
  - There is a vegetarian alternative on the menu every day.
  - We had no alternative but to fire Gibson.
  - to offer/provide an alternative
  - alternative to something Does this offer a viable alternative to the existing system?
  - as an alternative to something Fruit juice is provided as an alternative to alcoholic drinks.
  - alternative for somebody This treatment is the only alternative for some patients.

### [although](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/although) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/ɔːlˈðəʊ/` · **US:** `/ɔːlˈðəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/alt/altho/although__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/alt/altho/although__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Although the sun was shining, it wasn't very warm.
  - Although small, the kitchen is well designed.

### [among](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/among) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/əˈmʌŋ/` · **US:** `/əˈmʌŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/amo/among/among__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/amo/among/among__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a house among the trees
  - They strolled among the crowds.
  - I found the letter amongst his papers.
  - It's OK, you're among friends now.

### [amount](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/amount_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈmaʊnt/` · **US:** `/əˈmaʊnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/amo/amoun/amount__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/amo/amoun/amount__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was amazed at the amount he could eat.
  - Mix the colours in equal amounts.
  - amount of something an amount of money/information/work/time
  - A certain amount of time has already been spent on the project.
  - The server is designed to store huge amounts of data.
  - Try to reduce the amount of energy and water you use at home.
  - Faculty should increase the amount and quality of feedback they provide.

### [ancient](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ancient) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeɪnʃənt/` · **US:** `/ˈeɪnʃənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/anc/ancie/ancient__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/anc/ancie/ancient__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Archaeologists are excavating the ruined temples of this ancient civilization.
  - ancient Greece/Egypt/Rome
  - the ancient city of Petra

### [ankle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ankle) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæŋkl/` · **US:** `/ˈæŋkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ank/ankle/ankle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ank/ankle/ankle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to sprain/break/twist your ankle
  - She suffered a serious ankle injury in training.
  - to have a broken/twisted/fractured/sprained ankle
  - ankle boots (= that cover the ankle)
  - We found ourselves ankle-deep in water (= the water came up to our ankles).
  - My ankles have swollen.
  - The injury required surgery on his right ankle.
  - He will wear an electronic ankle bracelet, which allows police to track him.

### [any](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/any_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeni/` · **US:** `/ˈeni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/any/any__/any__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/any/any__/any__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He wasn't any good at French.
  - I can't run any faster.
  - Is your father feeling any better?
  - I don't want any more.
  - If you don't tell them, nobody will be any the wiser (= they will not find out about it).

### [any more](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/any-more) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌeni ˈmɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ˌeni ˈmɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/any/any_m/any_more_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/any/any_m/any_more_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She doesn't live here any more.
  - Why doesn't he speak to me any more?
  - Now she won't have to go out to work any more.

### [anybody](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/anybody) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈenibɒdi/` · **US:** `/ˈenibʌdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/any/anybo/anybody__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/any/anybo/anybody__us_4_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Is there anybody who can help me?
  - Anybody can use the pool—you don't need to be a member.
  - She wasn't anybody before she got that job.

### [anyway](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/anyway) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeniweɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈeniweɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/any/anywa/anyway__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/any/anywa/anyway__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's too expensive and anyway the colour doesn't suit you.
  - We're not perfect, but it doesn't really matter anyway.
  - It's too late now, anyway.

### [anywhere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/anywhere_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeniweə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈeniwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/any/anywh/anywhere__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/any/anywh/anywhere__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I can't see it anywhere.
  - Did you go anywhere interesting?
  - Many of these animals are not found anywhere else.
  - He's never been anywhere outside Britain.

### [anywhere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/anywhere_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈeniweə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈeniwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/any/anywh/anywhere__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/any/anywh/anywhere__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I don't have anywhere to stay.
  - Do you know anywhere I can get my bike repaired?

### [app](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/app) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/æp/` · **US:** `/æp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/app__/app__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/app__/app__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You first need to install the app on your device.
  - to download/launch an app
  - to build/develop an app
  - Have you got the dictionary app on your phone?
  - Gaming today is all about mobile app downloads.
  - app developers
  - The app is available on/in the app store.

### [appear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appear) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/əˈpɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appea/appear__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appea/appear__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Three days later a rash appeared.
  - Symptoms usually start appearing within two to three days.
  - + adv./prep. A cat suddenly appeared out of nowhere.
  - Smoke appeared on the horizon.
  - A bus appeared around the corner.
  - Posters for the gig appeared all over town.
  - New shoots are just appearing at the base of the plant.

### [appearance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/appearance) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈpɪərəns/` · **US:** `/əˈpɪrəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/appea/appearance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/appea/appearance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the physical/outward/external appearance of something
  - She had never been greatly concerned about her appearance.
  - The dog was similar in general appearance to a spaniel.
  - He gave every appearance of (= seemed very much to be) enjoying himself.
  - Judging by appearances can be misleading.
  - To all appearances (= as far as people could tell) he was dead.

### [apply](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/apply) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈplaɪ/` · **US:** `/əˈplaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/app/apply/apply__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/app/apply/apply__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You should apply in person/by letter.
  - apply for something I have decided to apply for this new job.
  - They have successfully applied for political asylum.
  - to apply for a visa/passport/grant/licence
  - The developers applied for planning permission to build over 200 houses.
  - apply to somebody/something to apply to a company/university
  - apply to somebody/something for something They may apply to the court for the decision to be reversed.
  - apply to do something He has applied to join the army.

### [architect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/architect_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːkɪtekt/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːrkɪtekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arc/archi/architect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arc/archi/architect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The house was designed by architect Louis Kahn.
  - She's a London-based architect and interior designer.
  - In 1721 he was named chief architect of the cathedral.

### [architecture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/architecture) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːkɪtektʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːrkɪtektʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arc/archi/architecture__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arc/archi/architecture__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to study architecture
  - She's a professor of architecture at the University of Oregon.
  - an architecture student/critic

### [argue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/argue) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːɡjuː/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːrɡjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arg/argue/argue__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arg/argue/argue__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My brothers are always arguing.
  - He's offering to pay so who am I to argue?
  - argue with somebody about/over something We're always arguing with each other about money.
  - argue about something I know it was a stupid thing to argue about.
  - argue over something All families seem to argue over money.
  - argue with somebody I don't want to argue with you—just do it!

### [argument](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/argument) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːɡjumənt/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːrɡjumənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arg/argum/argument__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arg/argum/argument__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to win/lose an argument
  - After some heated argument a decision was finally taken.
  - argument with somebody She got into an argument with the teacher.
  - argument about/over something He was killed during a violent argument over money.
  - argument with somebody about/over something We had an argument with the waiter about the bill.
  - argument between A and B Jack was always the one who settled arguments between us.

### [army](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/army) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɑːmi/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːrmi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arm/army_/army__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arm/army_/army__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The two opposing armies faced each other across the battlefield.
  - He commanded the army of occupation after the war.

### [arrange](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arrange) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈreɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/əˈreɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arr/arran/arrange__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arr/arran/arrange__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - arrange something The party was arranged quickly.
  - Can I arrange an appointment for Monday?
  - The meeting was provisionally arranged for 9 October.
  - She arranged a loan with the bank.
  - We met at six, as arranged.
  - arrange how, where, etc… We've still got to arrange how to get to the airport.
  - arrange to do something Have you arranged to meet him?
  - arrange that… I've arranged that we can borrow their car.
  - arrange for something to do something We arranged for a car to collect us from the airport.
  - arrange for something The president arranged for a meeting in his office.
  - arrange with somebody (about something) I've arranged with the neighbours about feeding the cat while we are away.

### [arrangement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/arrangement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈreɪndʒmənt/` · **US:** `/əˈreɪndʒmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/arr/arran/arrangement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/arr/arran/arrangement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Travel and accommodation arrangements have been finalized.
  - arrangement for something I'll make arrangements for you to be met at the airport.

### [as](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/as_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/æz/` · **US:** `/æz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/as_/as__g/as__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/as_/as__u/as__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You're as tall as your father.
  - He was as white as a sheet.
  - She doesn't play as well as her sister.
  - I haven't known him as long as you (= as you have known him).
  - He doesn't earn as much as me.
  - He doesn't earn as much as I do.
  - It's not as hard as I thought.
  - Run as fast as you can.
  - We'd like it as soon as possible.

### [as](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/as_3) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/æz/` · **US:** `/æz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/as_/as__g/as__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/as_/as__u/as__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He sat watching her as she got ready.
  - As she grew older she gained in confidence.

### [asleep](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/asleep) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsliːp/` · **US:** `/əˈsliːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/asl/aslee/asleep__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/asl/aslee/asleep__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I waited until they were all fast asleep (= sleeping deeply).
  - The baby was sound asleep (= sleeping deeply) upstairs.
  - He was so exhausted that he fell asleep at his desk.
  - My mother fell asleep at the wheel (= while driving) and crashed into a tree.
  - She was still half asleep (= not fully awake) when she arrived at work.
  - The police found him asleep in a garage.

### [assistant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assistant_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsɪstənt/` · **US:** `/əˈsɪstənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assis/assistant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assis/assistant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the assistant manager/director/commissioner
  - She is an assistant professor of psychology at Ohio State University.
  - Assistant Chief Constable Owen
  - Assistant Attorney General William Weld
  - a former assistant Secretary of State
  - an assistant coach/editor

### [assistant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/assistant_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈsɪstənt/` · **US:** `/əˈsɪstənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ass/assis/assistant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ass/assis/assistant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My assistant will now demonstrate the machine in action.
  - a senior research assistant
  - She works as a care assistant in an old people's home.
  - I work part-time as a classroom assistant in my local primary school.
  - He was working as a special assistant to the president.

### [athlete](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/athlete) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈæθliːt/` · **US:** `/ˈæθliːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ath/athle/athlete__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ath/athle/athlete__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Olympic athletes
  - Depression is thought to be common among elite athletes.
  - a training facility for amateur and professional athletes
  - Her company is designing part of the Olympic athletes' village.

### [attack](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attack_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtæk/` · **US:** `/əˈtæk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attac/attack__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attac/attack__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Five people were killed in the attacks that took place last night.
  - attack on/upon somebody/something the recent series of deadly terrorist attacks on European cities
  - a violent attack upon a police officer
  - attack against somebody vicious attacks against defenceless people
  - attack by somebody/something A child is recovering in hospital after a serious attack by a stray dog.

### [attack](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attack_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtæk/` · **US:** `/əˈtæk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attac/attack__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attac/attack__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Most dogs will not attack unless provoked.
  - attack somebody/something Terrorists attacked several targets across the city.
  - A woman was brutally attacked by a gang of youths.
  - attack somebody with something One of the men viciously attacked officers with a home-made weapon.
  - The man attacked him with a knife.

### [attend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtend/` · **US:** `/əˈtend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/atten/attend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/atten/attend__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We'd like as many people as possible to attend.
  - attend something The meeting was attended by 90% of shareholders.
  - to attend a wedding/funeral/service/ceremony
  - Everyone is welcome to attend the free event.
  - All members are requested to attend this meeting.
  - 5,000 people from around the world were invited to attend the conference.

### [attention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attention_2) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtenʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈtenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/atten/attention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/atten/attention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Attention, please! Passengers for flight KL412 are requested to go to gate 21 immediately.

### [attention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attention_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtenʃn/` · **US:** `/əˈtenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/atten/attention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/atten/attention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the report’s attention to detail
  - He turned his attention back to the road again.
  - His attention turned to other matters.
  - Public attention is focused on the issue of plastic pollution.
  - Please pay close attention (= listen carefully) to what I am saying.
  - Don't pay any attention to what they say (= don't think that it is important).
  - She doesn't pay her children much attention.
  - She tried to attract the waiter's attention.
  - The event has attracted a lot of media attention.
  - The exhibition has received special attention from the media.
  - The problem has been getting little attention in the press.
  - I tried not to draw attention to (= make people notice) the weak points in my argument.
  - An article in the newspaper caught my attention.
  - I couldn't give the programme my undivided attention.
  - As the youngest child, she was always the centre of attention.
  - Small children have a very short attention span.
  - (formal) These issues should be brought to the attention of the public.
  - (formal) It has come to my attention (= I have been informed) that…
  - (formal) He called (their) attention to the fact that many files were missing.
  - (formal) Can I have your attention please?

### [attractive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/attractive) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈtræktɪv/` · **US:** `/əˈtræktɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/att/attra/attractive__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/att/attra/attractive__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an attractive woman
  - I like John but I don't find him attractive physically.
  - I find men look more attractive as they age.

### [audience](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/audience) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːdiəns/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːdiəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aud/audie/audience__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aud/audie/audience__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The audience was/were clapping for 10 minutes.
  - The museum is trying to attract a wider audience.
  - The debate was televised in front of a live audience.
  - an audience of 10 000
  - Audience members voted on their favorite presentations.
  - We encourage audience participation during our show.

### [author](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/author_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːθə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːθər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/aut/autho/author__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/aut/autho/author__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who is your favourite author?
  - He is the author of three books on art.
  - best-selling author Paul Theroux
  - the lead author of the report
  - the author of a novel/study/article
  - Who's the author?
  - According to report author Matt Blaze, the technique requires no special skill.
  - Really quick author's note: This is the last chapter of the story.
  - The film is based on a novel written by a female author.
  - The report's authors point to a recent study of American college students.

### [available](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/available) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈveɪləbl/` · **US:** `/əˈveɪləbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ava/avail/available__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ava/avail/available__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - available resources/data
  - Most available evidence suggests that home-schooled children are getting a good education.
  - Further details are available on request.
  - We'll send you a copy as soon as it becomes available.
  - We have plenty of office space available.
  - The document is now commercially available in electronic form.
  - readily/widely/freely/publicly available
  - available from somebody/something Tickets are currently available at reduced prices from the festival office.
  - available for something This data is still available for download on the company's website.
  - available to somebody When will the information be made available to the public?

### [average](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/average_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈævərɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈævərɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ave/avera/average__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ave/avera/average__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The average age of participants was 52 years.
  - an average rate/cost/price
  - an average annual income/wage/salary
  - Average daily summer temperatures are about 20°C .
  - Average earnings are around £20 000 per annum.
  - The school has an average class size of 24 students.
  - at an average speed of 100 miles per hour

### [average](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/average_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈævərɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈævərɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/ave/avera/average__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/ave/avera/average__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The average of 4, 5 and 9 is 6.
  - an average of something Parents spend an average of $220 a year on toys.
  - Prices rose by an average of 10 percent over the decade.
  - If I get an A on this essay, that will bring my average (= average mark/grade) up to a B+.
  - Class sizes in the school are below the national average.
  - We will deliver growth in the economy that is higher than the OECD average this year.
  - They had higher mortality rates from accidents compared with the national average.
  - Children here score well above the city average on math and reading tests.

### [avoid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/avoid) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əˈvɔɪd/` · **US:** `/əˈvɔɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/avo/avoid/avoid__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/avo/avoid/avoid__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - avoid something to avoid conflict/confrontation
  - We must find a way to avoid similar problems in future.
  - They narrowly avoided defeat.
  - The name was changed to avoid confusion with another firm.
  - The accident could have been avoided.
  - Efforts are being made to avoid war at all costs.
  - avoid doing something They built a wall to avoid soil being washed away.

### [award](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/award_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/əˈwɔːd/` · **US:** `/əˈwɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/awa/award/award__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/awa/award/award__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was nominated for the best actor award.
  - the UK's most prestigious literary award
  - the Housing Design Award
  - The mayor will present the awards.
  - award for something to win/accept an award for something
  - He received numerous awards for excellence in teaching.
  - She was recently honoured with a lifetime achievement award.
  - The winners of the awards are picked by the network's viewers.
  - an awards ceremony

### [awful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/awful_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːfl/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/a/awf/awful/awful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/a/awf/awful/awful__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That's an awful colour.
  - ‘They didn't even offer to pay.’ ‘Oh that's awful.’
  - I feel awful about forgetting her birthday.
  - to look/feel awful (= to look/feel ill)
  - There's an awful smell in here.
  - The awful thing is, it was my fault.
  - It sounds awful to say it, but the war was exciting.
  - The weather conditions were just awful.
  - It's awful, isn't it?
  - The weather last summer was awful.


## B

### [back](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/back_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/bæk/` · **US:** `/bæk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bac/back_/back__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bac/back_/back__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We were sitting in the back row
  - the back garden (= behind the house)
  - a back room (= one at the back of a building)
  - My phone's in my back pocket.
  - back teeth
  - the back page of a newspaper
  - We drove along miles of twisty back roads (= away from the main roads).

### [background](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/background) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbækɡraʊnd/` · **US:** `/ˈbækɡraʊnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bac/backg/background__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bac/backg/background__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - somebody's ethnic/genetic/cultural background
  - somebody's family/educational background
  - The job would suit someone with a business background.
  - We come from very different backgrounds but we get on well.
  - background in something She has a background in journalism.
  - In the future, we will require background checks on airport employees.

### [badly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/badly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbædli/` · **US:** `/ˈbædli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bad/badly/badly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bad/badly/badly__us_2.mp3)

### [bar](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bar_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɑː(r)/` · **US:** `/bɑːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bar/bar__/bar__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bar/bar__/bar__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We arranged to meet in a bar called the Flamingo.
  - The area is full of restaurants and bars.
  - The bar staff are very friendly.
  - It's the island’s only licensed bar (= one that is allowed to sell alcoholic drinks).
  - (British English) I found David in the bar of the Red Lion (= a room in a pub where drinks are served).

### [baseball](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/baseball) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbeɪsbɔːl/` · **US:** `/ˈbeɪsbɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bas/baseb/baseball__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bas/baseb/baseball__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [based](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/based) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/beɪst/` · **US:** `/beɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bas/based/based__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bas/based/based__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - Their research was based largely on anecdotal evidence.

### [basketball](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/basketball) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɑːskɪtbɔːl/` · **US:** `/ˈbæskɪtbɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bas/baske/basketball__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bas/baske/basketball__us_2.mp3)

### [bean](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bean_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/biːn/` · **US:** `/biːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bea/bean_/bean__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bea/bean_/bean__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/beə(r)/` · **US:** `/ber/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bea/bear_/bear__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bea/bear_/bear__us_1.mp3)

### [beat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/beat_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/biːt/` · **US:** `/biːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bea/beat_/beat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bea/beat_/beat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - beat somebody at something He beat me at chess.
  - beat somebody She was narrowly beaten by the German in the final.
  - I beat her by just three points.
  - Their recent wins have proved they're still the ones to beat (= the most difficult team to beat).

### [beef](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/beef_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/biːf/` · **US:** `/biːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bee/beef_/beef__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bee/beef_/beef__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - minced/ground beef
  - a joint/fillet/cut of beef
  - a beef farmer/producer

### [before](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/before_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/bɪˈfɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/bɪˈfɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bef/befor/before__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bef/befor/before__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You should have told me so before.
  - It had been fine the week before (= the previous week).
  - That had happened long before (= a long time earlier).
  - I think we've met before.

### [before](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/before_2) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/bɪˈfɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/bɪˈfɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bef/befor/before__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bef/befor/before__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do it before you forget.
  - Did she leave a message before she went?
  - Before I made a decision, I thought carefully about it.

### [behave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/behave) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɪˈheɪv/` · **US:** `/bɪˈheɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/beh/behav/behave__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/beh/behav/behave__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The doctor behaved very unprofessionally.
  - They behaved very badly towards their guests.
  - He behaved like a true gentleman.
  - They behave differently when you're not around.
  - He had always behaved in a friendly manner towards us.
  - I expect you to behave in a certain way.
  - She behaved with great dignity.
  - He behaved as if/though nothing had happened.

### [behaviour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/behaviour) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɪˈheɪvjə(r)/` · **US:** `/bɪˈheɪvjər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/beh/behav/behaviour__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/beh/behav/behaviour__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - good/bad behaviour
  - social/sexual/criminal behaviour
  - a plan to tackle antisocial behaviour
  - behaviour towards somebody His behaviour towards her was becoming more and more aggressive.
  - She began to exhibit violent behaviour.
  - I noticed a change in his behaviour.
  - It is hard to change old patterns of behaviour.

### [belong](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/belong) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɪˈlɒŋ/` · **US:** `/bɪˈlɔːŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bel/belon/belong__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bel/belon/belong__us_6.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Where do these plates belong (= where are they kept)?
  - Are you sure these documents belong together?
  - Several of the points you raise do not belong in this discussion.
  - A person like that does not belong in teaching.

### [belt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/belt_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/belt/` · **US:** `/belt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bel/belt_/belt__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bel/belt_/belt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to do up/fasten/tighten a belt
  - a belt buckle
  - a studded leather belt
  - Around his waist he wore a belt.

### [benefit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/benefit_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbenɪfɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈbenɪfɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/ben/benef/benefit__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/ben/benef/benefit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Freedom of information brings great benefits.
  - health/economic/tax benefits
  - the benefits of modern medicine
  - I've had the benefit of a good education.
  - It was good to see her finally reaping the benefits (= enjoying the results) of all her hard work.
  - The industry will be one of the first to enjoy the benefits of the recovery.
  - The company derived substantial benefit from the deal.
  - benefit of doing something He couldn't see the benefit of arguing any longer.
  - be of benefit to somebody/something The new regulations will be of benefit to everyone concerned.
  - to somebody's benefit It will be to your benefit to arrive early.
  - benefit for somebody/something The project will have major benefits for the local community.
  - With the benefit of hindsight (= knowing what we have learnt since), we would do things differently.
  - without the benefit of something Nobody should be sent to prison without the benefit of legal advice.
  - For maximum benefit, take the tablets before meals.

### [best](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/best_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/best/` · **US:** `/best/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bes/best_/best__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bes/best_/best__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [best](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/best_3) *(noun)*

  - We're the best of friends (= very close friends).

### [better](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/better_2) *(adverb)*

- **UK:** `/baɪˈɒlədʒi/` · **US:** `/baɪˈɑːlədʒi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bio/biolo/biology__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bio/biolo/biology__us_1.mp3)

### [birth](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/birth) *(noun)*

### [biscuit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/biscuit) *(noun)*

  - The cake has a biscuit base (= one made from crushed biscuits).
  - We were offered a cup of tea and some biscuits.

### [bit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bit_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɪt/` · **US:** `/bɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bit/bit__/bit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bit/bit__/bit__us_1.mp3)

  - I was a bit disappointed by the film.
  - It costs a bit more than I wanted to spend.

  - I felt a wee bit guilty about it.

### [blank](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/blank_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/blæŋk/` · **US:** `/blæŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bla/blank/blank__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bla/blank/blank__us_1.mp3)

### [blank](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/blank_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/blæŋk/` · **US:** `/blæŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bla/blank/blank__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bla/blank/blank__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Please fill in the blanks.
  - If you can't answer the question, leave a blank.

### [blood](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/blood_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/blʌd/` · **US:** `/blʌd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/blo/blood/blood__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/blo/blood/blood__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He lost a lot of blood in the accident.
  - Blood was pouring out of a cut on her head.
  - His hands were covered in blood.
  - to give blood (= to have blood taken from you so that it can be used in the medical treatment of other people)
  - After the attacks, people donated blood in record numbers.
  - He was a hot-headed warrior, always too quick to shed blood (= attack and injure or kill somebody).
  - to draw blood (= to wound a person so that they lose blood)
  - a drop/pool of blood
  - There was dried blood on her cheek.
  - a blood cell/sample
  - to improve blood flow to the heart
  - the blood supply to the brain

### [blow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/blow_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bləʊ/` · **US:** `/bləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/blo/blow_/blow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/blo/blow_/blow__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adv./prep. You're not blowing hard enough!
  - The policeman asked me to blow into the breathalyser.
  - blow something + adv./prep. He drew on his cigarette and blew out a stream of smoke.

### [board](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/board_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɔːd/` · **US:** `/bɔːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/boa/board/board__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/boa/board/board__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He had ripped up the carpet, leaving only the bare boards.
  - She noticed a loose board in the fence.

### [boil](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/boil_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɔɪl/` · **US:** `/bɔɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/boi/boil_/boil__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/boi/boil_/boil__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The water was bubbling and boiling away.
  - boil something Boil plenty of salted water, then add the spaghetti.

### [bone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bone_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bəʊn/` · **US:** `/bəʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bon/bone_/bone__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bon/bone_/bone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He survived the accident with no broken bones.
  - This fish has a lot of bones in it.
  - She went for a bone density scan.
  - The dog was gnawing at a bone.
  - She had a beautiful face with very good bone structure.

### [book](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/book_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bʊk/` · **US:** `/bʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/boo/book_/book__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/boo/book_/book__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Book early to avoid disappointment.
  - book something She booked a flight to Chicago.
  - He booked tickets for his trip to New York.
  - Most customers now book their holidays online.
  - The performance is booked up (= there are no more tickets available).
  - I'm sorry—we're fully booked.
  - (British English) I'd like to book a table for two for 8 o'clock tonight.

### [borrow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/borrow) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɒrəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈbɔːrəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bor/borro/borrow__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bor/borro/borrow__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - borrow something Can I borrow your umbrella?
  - borrow something from somebody/something Members can borrow up to ten books from the library at any one time.
  - borrow something off somebody (British English, informal) I borrowed the tools I needed off my brother.

### [boss](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/boss_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bɒs/` · **US:** `/bɔːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bos/boss_/boss__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bos/boss_/boss__us_8.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll ask my boss if I can have the day off.
  - I like being my own boss (= working for myself and making my own decisions).
  - Who's the boss (= who's in control) in this house?
  - He thinks he's impressing the boss by working through lunch.
  - He accused his former boss of being arrogant.

### [bottom](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bottom_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɒtəm/` · **US:** `/ˈbɑːtəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bot/botto/bottom__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bot/botto/bottom__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - your bottom lip
  - the bottom line/row/edge
  - on the bottom left/right of the page
  - in the bottom corner of the screen
  - on the bottom shelf
  - Their house is at the bottom end of Bury Road (= the end furthest from where you enter the road).

### [bottom](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bottom_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbɒtəm/` · **US:** `/ˈbɑːtəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bot/botto/bottom__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bot/botto/bottom__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the bottom of the screen/stairs/hill
  - at the bottom of something Footnotes are given at the bottom of each page.
  - at the bottom The book I want is right at the bottom (= of the pile).
  - The wind blew through gaps at the top and bottom of the door.
  - farmers who lived in the valley bottoms

### [bowl](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bowl_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/bəʊl/` · **US:** `/bəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bow/bowl_/bowl__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bow/bowl_/bowl__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a salad/fruit/sugar bowl
  - a washing-up bowl
  - I refilled the dog's water bowl.
  - Sieve the flour into a bowl.

### [brain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/brain_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/breɪn/` · **US:** `/breɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bra/brain/brain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bra/brain/brain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The human brain is a complex organ.
  - My tired brain couldn't cope with such a complex problem.
  - She was found to have sustained a brain injury.
  - brain cells/tissue
  - a brain tumour/haemorrhage/aneurysm
  - Sometimes the doctor will also do a brain scan.
  - brain surgery

### [bridge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bridge_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/brɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/brɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bri/bridg/bridge__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bri/bridg/bridge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We crossed the bridge over the River Windrush.

  - The plan incudes a pedestrian bridge connecting the arena with the convention centre.

### [bright](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/bright_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/braɪt/` · **US:** `/braɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bri/brigh/bright_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bri/brigh/bright_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - bright light/sunshine
  - a bright room
  - Her eyes were bright with tears.
  - a bright morning (= with the sun shining)
  - All of these stars are bigger and brighter than our sun.

### [brilliant](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/brilliant) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbrɪliənt/` · **US:** `/ˈbrɪliənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bri/brill/brilliant__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bri/brill/brilliant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What a brilliant idea!
  - an absolutely brilliant idea
  - a brilliant performance/invention
  - He made two brilliant saves to keep his side in the match.
  - The system is brilliant in its simplicity.

### [broken](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/broken_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbrəʊkən/` · **US:** `/ˈbrəʊkən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bro/broke/broken__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bro/broke/broken__us_1.mp3)

  - The TV's broken.
  - They opened the bag and found a broken bottle.

### [brush](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/brush_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/brʌʃ/` · **US:** `/brʌʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bru/brush/brush__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bru/brush/brush__us_1.mp3)

### [brush](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/brush_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/brʌʃ/` · **US:** `/brʌʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bru/brush/brush__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bru/brush/brush__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [burn](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/burn_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/bɜːn/` · **US:** `/bɜːrn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bur/burn_/burn__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bur/burn_/burn__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - A small candle burned brightly.

- **UK:** `/ˈbɪznəsmən/` · **US:** `/ˈbɪznəsmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/bus/busin/businessman__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/bus/busin/businessman__us_1_rr.mp3)

### [button](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/button_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈbʌtn/` · **US:** `/ˈbʌtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/b/but/butto/button__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/b/but/butto/button__us_4.mp3)

## C

### [camp](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/camp_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kæmp/` · **US:** `/kæmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cam/camp_/camp__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cam/camp_/camp_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Let's return to camp.
  - to pitch/set up camp (= put up tents)
  - to break/strike camp (= take down tents)

### [camp](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/camp_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kæmp/` · **US:** `/kæmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cam/camp_/camp__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cam/camp_/camp_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I camped overnight in a field.

### [camping](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/camping) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkæmpɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈkæmpɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cam/campi/camping__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cam/campi/camping__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you go camping?
  - a camping trip
  - We go camping every weekend.
  - No overnight camping is allowed in city parks.
  - camping equipment

### [can](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/can2_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kæn/` · **US:** `/kæn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/can/can__/can__gb_7.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/can/can__/can__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
