  - can of something a can of beans/beer/soda
  - a beer can
  - He opened the can and took a large swig.
  - an aluminium/tin can

### [care](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/care_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/keə(r)/` · **US:** `/ker/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/care_/care__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/care_/care__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Some people were badly injured and needed medical care.
  - We aim to continually improve the quality of patient care.
  - How much do men share housework and the care of the children?
  - care for somebody/something to provide care for the elderly
  - with care With proper care, the plants may last for fifty years.
  - skin/hair care products

### [care](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/care_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/keə(r)/` · **US:** `/ker/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/care_/care__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/care_/care__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I don't care (= I will not be upset) if I never see him again!
  - He threatened to fire me, as if I cared!
  - care about something She cares deeply about environmental issues.
  - I don't really care about how much it costs.
  - care what, whether, etc… I don't care what he thinks.
  - care that… She doesn't seem to care that he's been married four times before.

### [careful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/careful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkeəfl/` · **US:** `/ˈkerfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/caref/careful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/caref/careful__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Be careful!
  - careful to do something He was careful to keep out of sight.
  - He's been careful to avoid significant debt.
  - careful not to do something Be careful not to wake the baby.
  - careful when/what/how, etc. You must be careful when handling chemicals.
  - Be careful what you say.
  - careful of/with something Please be careful with my glasses (= Don't break them).
  - Be careful of the traffic.
  - careful about something As a young actor, you have to be extremely careful about the roles you accept.
  - careful about doing something I’m very careful about washing my hands before eating (= I make sure I do it).
  - careful (that)… Be careful you don't bump your head.

### [carefully](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/carefully) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkeəfəli/` · **US:** `/ˈkerfəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/caref/carefully__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/caref/carefully__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Drive carefully.
  - She put the glass down carefully.
  - Please listen carefully.
  - We need to think carefully about the possible consequences of this decision.
  - The prime minister's words were carefully chosen.
  - The candidate gave his speech before a carefully selected audience.
  - a carefully planned attack on the enemy encampment

### [carpet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/carpet_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɑːpɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːrpɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/carpe/carpet__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/carpe/carpet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He spent the morning laying the new carpet.
  - a bedroom carpet
  - (British English) We have fitted carpets (= carpets from wall to wall) in our house.
  - a roll of carpet

### [cartoon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cartoon) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɑːˈtuːn/` · **US:** `/kɑːrˈtuːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/car/carto/cartoon__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/car/carto/cartoon__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a political/satirical cartoon
  - The funniest contributions to the magazine are the cartoons.
  - He started drawing cartoons when he was still at school.

### [case](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/case_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/keɪs/` · **US:** `/keɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cas/case_/case__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cas/case_/case__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in… cases In some cases people have had to wait several weeks for an appointment.
  - In most cases the increases have been marginal.
  - In many cases it is the pressure of the job that causes managers to behave like this.
  - What should be done in such cases?
  - The substances causes nausea, sickness and, in extreme cases, death.
  - in cases of something The company only dismisses its employees in cases of gross misconduct.
  - in the case of something In the case of banks, the law can limit activities.
  - Sometimes compost isn't enough, in which case artificial products may have to be used.
  - a case of something It's a classic case (= a very typical case) of bad planning.
  - This appears to be a case of mistaken identity.

### [cash](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cash_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kæʃ/` · **US:** `/kæʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cas/cash_/cash__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cas/cash_/cash__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - How much cash do you have on you?
  - Customers are offered a 10% discount if they pay cash.
  - a cash payment
  - in cash Payments can be made by card or in cash.
  - The thieves stole £500 in cash.

### [castle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/castle) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɑːsl/` · **US:** `/ˈkæsl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cas/castl/castle__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cas/castl/castle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a medieval castle
  - Windsor Castle
  - in/at the castle They were invited to a banquet in the castle.
  - They sought safety within the castle walls.

### [catch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/catch_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kætʃ/` · **US:** `/ketʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cat/catch/catch__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cat/catch/catch__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She managed to catch the keys as they fell.
  - I caught him when he fell.
  - The dog caught the stick in its mouth.
  - I threw the bag in the air and she caught it.
  - ‘Throw me over that towel, will you?’ ‘OK. Catch!’

### [cause](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cause_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɔːz/` · **US:** `/kɔːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cau/cause/cause__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cau/cause/cause__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Unemployment is a major cause of poverty.
  - The report ignores the root cause of the problem.
  - Smoking is the leading cause of lung cancer.
  - Drinking and driving is one of the most common causes of traffic accidents.
  - It was impossible to determine the cause of death.
  - There was discussion about the fire and its likely cause.
  - He died of natural causes.
  - It is not easy to separate cause and effect in this situation.

### [cause](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cause_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kɔːz/` · **US:** `/kɔːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cau/cause/cause__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cau/cause/cause__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cause something Do they know what caused the fire?
  - Are you causing trouble again?
  - Doctors say her condition is causing some concern.
  - Recently there have been several deaths caused by dangerous driving.
  - cause something for somebody/something The drought is causing problems for many farmers.
  - cause something to something/somebody The earthquake caused widespread damage to property.
  - The revelations caused some discomfort to the president.
  - cause somebody something The project is still causing him a lot of problems.
  - cause something/somebody to do something The poor harvest caused prices to rise sharply.
  - The bad weather is likely to cause people to stay away.

### [celebrate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/celebrate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈselɪbreɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈselɪbreɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cel/celeb/celebrate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cel/celeb/celebrate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Jake's passed his exams. We're going out to celebrate.
  - We had good reason to celebrate.
  - celebrate something to celebrate a birthday
  - We celebrated our 25th wedding anniversary in Florence.
  - to celebrate a victory/a success/an achievement
  - The Film Festival is currently celebrating its 27th year.
  - How do people celebrate New Year in your country?
  - celebrate something with somebody/something He made the trip home to celebrate Christmas with his family.
  - celebrate with somebody/something She celebrated with a party for family and friends.
  - Maybe I'll celebrate with some friends.
  - celebrate doing something In this photo he celebrates winning the men's 10 000 metres final.

### [celebrity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/celebrity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈlebrəti/` · **US:** `/səˈlebrəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cel/celeb/celebrity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cel/celeb/celebrity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a celebrity chef
  - He became a celebrity in China after winning a gold medal at the Olympics.
  - Hollywood/TV celebrities
  - He achieved instant celebrity status.

### [certain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/certain_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːtn/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrtn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cer/certa/certain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cer/certa/certain__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I think it was him, but I can't be certain.
  - certain (that)… She wasn’t certain (that) he had seen her.
  - I'm certain we'll think of something.
  - certain about something Are you absolutely certain about this?
  - certain of something I am not certain of the precise date.
  - certain who/where, etc… I'm not certain who was there.

### [certainly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/certainly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːtnli/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrtnli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cer/certa/certainly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cer/certa/certainly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Without treatment, she will almost certainly die.
  - Certainly, the early years are crucial to a child's development.
  - I'm certainly never going there again.
  - I certainly hope we can do this again.
  - She was certainly attractive but you couldn’t call her beautiful.
  - It is certainly true that individual earthquakes are difficult to predict with any accuracy.
  - This will certainly make them think again.

### [chance](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chance_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃɑːns/` · **US:** `/tʃæns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chanc/chance__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chanc/chance__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - chance of doing something She only has a slim chance of passing the exam.
  - Is there any chance of getting tickets for tonight?
  - chance that… There's a good chance that he'll be back in time.
  - There is no chance that he will change his mind.
  - chance of somebody/something doing something What chance is there of anybody being found alive?
  - There is little chance of that happening.
  - chance of something The operation has a fifty-fifty chance of success.
  - Nowadays a premature baby has a very good chance of survival.
  - Not wearing a helmet could increase your chance of injury.
  - The Met Office said there was an 80 per cent chance of severe weather.
  - chance at something Now that he's met the love of his life, he finally has a chance at real happiness.
  - chance for something We believe the plan has a good chance for success.
  - with a chance They are the one team with a chance of beating us.
  - an outside chance (= a very small one)
  - The chances are a million to one against being struck by lightning.

### [character](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/character) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkærəktə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkærəktər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chara/character__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chara/character__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the main character in the film
  - a major/minor character in the book
  - Who plays the main character?
  - He loves to draw and create his own cartoon characters.
  - Clint Eastwood's character is the most sympathetic in the movie.

### [charity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/charity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtʃærəti/` · **US:** `/ˈtʃærəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chari/charity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chari/charity__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a registered charity
  - the UK's largest children's charity
  - Many charities sent money to help the victims of the famine.
  - The concert will raise money for local charities.
  - My parents always supported charities.
  - charity for somebody/something a charity for homeless people

### [chat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chat_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃæt/` · **US:** `/tʃæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chat_/chat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chat_/chat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I just called in for a chat.
  - chat with somebody I had a long chat with her.
  - chat about somebody/something He always enjoyed a chat about the old days.

### [chat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chat_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tʃæt/` · **US:** `/tʃæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cha/chat_/chat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cha/chat_/chat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm sorry I can't stop to chat.
  - The two of us got chatting (= started chatting) at the bus stop.
  - chat to/with somebody My kids spend hours chatting on the phone to their friends.
  - chat away Within minutes of being introduced they were chatting away like old friends.
  - chat away to/with somebody Everyone here chats away to you as though they've known you for decades.
  - chat about something/somebody What were you chatting about?

### [check](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/check_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃek/` · **US:** `/tʃek/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/check/check__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/check/check__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Could you give the tyres a check?
  - I went for a health check before going on the trip.
  - The drugs were found in their car during a routine check by police.
  - I'll just have a quick check to see if the letter's arrived yet.
  - check of something She had a thorough check of the room, but nothing had been taken.
  - check for something a check for spelling mistakes
  - check on something It is vital to keep a check on your speed (= look at it regularly in order to control it).
  - I did a final check on the seating arrangements.
  - check with somebody A check with the doctor confirmed his fears.
  - A spokesperson said the company made regular checks with the police and the council.

### [chef](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chef) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ʃef/` · **US:** `/ʃef/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/chef_/chef__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/chef_/chef__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a new book by celebrity chef Jamie Oliver
  - the head/executive chef at a famous London restaurant
  - She used to work as a chef in a big hotel in Johannesburg.

### [chemistry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chemistry) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkemɪstri/` · **US:** `/ˈkemɪstri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/che/chemi/chemistry__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/che/chemi/chemistry__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a degree in chemistry
  - He did physics and chemistry at university.
  - the university’s chemistry department
  - inorganic/organic chemistry

### [chip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/chip_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃɪp/` · **US:** `/tʃɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/chi/chip_/chip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/chi/chip_/chip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was eating a burger and chips.
  - a bag of chips
  - All main courses are served with chips or baked potato.

### [choice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/choice_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃɔɪs/` · **US:** `/tʃɔɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cho/choic/choice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cho/choic/choice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We are faced with a difficult choice.
  - choice between A and B women forced to make a choice between family and career
  - We aim to help students make more informed career choices.
  - I am sure you have made the right choice.
  - Resources are finite, and choices have to be made between competing priorities and needs.
  - There is a wide range of choices open to you.

### [church](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/church) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tʃɜːtʃ/` · **US:** `/tʃɜːrtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/chu/churc/church__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/chu/churc/church__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The procession moved into the church.
  - England has some beautiful parish churches.
  - a church tower/spire
  - church services

### [cigarette](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cigarette) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌsɪɡəˈret/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪɡəret/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cig/cigar/cigarette__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cig/cigar/cigarette__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to smoke/have a cigarette
  - a decrease in cigarette smoking
  - (British English) a packet of cigarettes
  - (especially North American English) a pack of cigarettes
  - to light a cigarette
  - the smell of cigarette smoke
  - There were three cigarette butts in the ashtray.
  - The man stubbed out his cigarette and lit a new one.

### [circle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/circle_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːkl/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cir/circl/circle__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cir/circl/circle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the centre/circumference/radius/diameter of a circle
  - concentric circles
  - a design of overlapping circles
  - circle of something Cut out two circles of paper.
  - Spread 1 tablespoon of the meat mixture on each circle of dough.
  - the circle of light thrown by the torch

### [circle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/circle_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːkl/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cir/circl/circle__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cir/circl/circle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - circle something Luke circled a date on the kitchen calendar.
  - Respondents were asked to circle the appropriate answers.
  - circle something in something Spelling mistakes are circled in red ink.
  - She circled her birthday in red on the calendar.
  - Investigators circled bullet holes with chalk.

### [classical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/classical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklæsɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈklæsɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cla/class/classical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cla/class/classical__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a classical composer/musician/pianist
  - a classical concert

### [clear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clear_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/klɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/klɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cle/clear/clear__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cle/clear/clear__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She gave me clear and precise directions.
  - Are these instructions clear enough?
  - Your meaning needs to be clear.
  - You'll do as you're told—is that clear?
  - clear about something She was quite clear about her reasons for leaving.
  - This behaviour must stop—do I make myself clear (= express myself clearly so there is no doubt about what I mean)?
  - I hope I made it clear to him that he was no longer welcome here.

### [clearly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clearly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklɪəli/` · **US:** `/ˈklɪrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cle/clear/clearly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cle/clear/clearly__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Please speak clearly after the tone.
  - It's difficult to see anything clearly in this mirror.
  - The church was clearly visible from my bedroom window.

### [clever](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clever) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklevə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈklevər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cle/cleve/clever__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cle/cleve/clever__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a clever child
  - Clever girl!
  - He's too clever by half, if you ask me (= it annoys me or makes me suspicious).
  - They're not clever enough to find the code.

### [climate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/climate) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈklaɪmət/` · **US:** `/ˈklaɪmət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cli/clima/climate__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cli/clima/climate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a tropical/warm/mild/temperate/cold climate
  - the harsh climate of the Arctic regions

### [close](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/close2_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kləʊs/` · **US:** `/kləʊs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/close/close__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/close/close__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I had no idea the beach was so close.
  - close to somebody/something Our new house is close to the school.
  - The children are close to each other in age.
  - She is looking for a job closer to home.
  - close together The tables were too close together.
  - Their birthdays are very close together.
  - This is the closest we can get to the beach by car.
  - We all have to work in close proximity (= near each other).
  - Victory was tantalizingly close.

### [closed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/closed) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kləʊzd/` · **US:** `/kləʊzd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/close/closed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/close/closed__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Keep the door closed.
  - He was slumped in a chair and his eyes were closed.
  - Store any leftovers in a closed container in the fridge.

### [clothing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/clothing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkləʊðɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈkləʊðɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/cloth/clothing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/cloth/clothing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Workers at the factory wear protective clothing.
  - a man wearing dark clothing
  - an item of clothing
  - warm clothing
  - the high cost of food and clothing
  - a piece/an article of clothing
  - Did you bring a change of clothing?
  - stalls selling clothing for men and women
  - The charity helped her out with clothing for work.
  - on clothing They found blood on his clothing.
  - a clothing store
  - a clothing manufacturer

### [cloud](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cloud_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/klaʊd/` · **US:** `/klaʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/clo/cloud/cloud__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/clo/cloud/cloud__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Dark clouds were gathering in the west.
  - The cloud cover is quite dense today.
  - black/grey clouds
  - thick/dense cloud
  - The sun went behind a cloud.
  - It was scorching and there wasn't a cloud in the sky.
  - in cloud The plane was flying in cloud most of the way.
  - through the clouds She could see the sun through the clouds.

### [coach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coach_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊtʃ/` · **US:** `/kəʊtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coa/coach/coach__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coa/coach/coach__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the head/assistant coach
  - a basketball/football coach
  - the team coach
  - the England coach
  - Italy’s national coach
  - Coach Bob Shapiro
  - the Giants coach Tom Coughlin
  - coach of/with/for somebody/something the coach of the New York Knicks
  - a coach with the Chicago Cubs
  - a coach for the US Olympic team
  - He is a former college football coach.

### [coast](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/coast_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊst/` · **US:** `/kəʊst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coa/coast/coast__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coa/coast/coast__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a trip to the coast
  - coast of something They plan to swim around the coast of Ireland.
  - the southern/northern/eastern/western coast of Spain
  - on the coast a town on the south/east coast of England
  - off the coast islands off the north/west coast of Scotland
  - along the coast We walked along the coast for five miles.
  - at the coast We spent the day at the coast.
  - by the coast a holiday by the coast
  - from coast to coast He cycled 2  500 miles across Australia from coast to coast.
  - the Atlantic coast
  - That's a pretty stretch of coast.
  - The coast road is closed due to bad weather.

### [code](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/code_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəʊd/` · **US:** `/kəʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cod/code_/code__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cod/code_/code__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to break/crack a code (= to understand and read the message)
  - in code It's written in code.
  - In the event of the machine not operating correctly, an error code will appear.
  - Tap your code number into the machine.
  - The code was difficult to crack.

### [colleague](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/colleague) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒliːɡ/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːliːɡ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colle/colleague__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colle/colleague__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - work/senior/male colleagues
  - We were friends and colleagues for more than 20 years.
  - some of his former colleagues on the council
  - the Prime Minister and his Cabinet colleagues
  - Scientists are now working with colleagues in Arizona to develop a working model.
  - He is described by party colleagues as a workaholic.
  - Russinova and her colleagues found that 74% of participants had held the same job for 24 months or longer.
  - I've talked to colleagues at other universities about it.
  - colleagues in the IT department
  - a colleague of mine from the office

### [collect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/collect_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈlekt/` · **US:** `/kəˈlekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colle/collect__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colle/collect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - collect something to collect data/evidence/information
  - We're collecting signatures for a petition.
  - The purpose of his trip was to collect material for a book.
  - collect something from somebody/something Samples were collected from over 200 patients.
  - an analysis of 400 specimens collected from 12 localities
  - We've been collecting data from various sources.

### [column](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/column) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒləm/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːləm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/col/colum/column__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/col/colum/column__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a dictionary with two columns per page
  - column of something a column of text
  - at the bottom of the right-hand column of this page
  - The website has an attractive two-column format.
  - in a column Click on the name of your account in the left-hand column.
  - Put a mark in the appropriate column.

### [comedy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/comedy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒmədi/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːmədi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comed/comedy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comed/comedy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a romantic comedy
  - a black comedy (= a play or film that deals with unpleasant or terrible things in a humorous way)
  - a slapstick comedy
  - He moved to Los Angeles to write comedy.
  - comedy about somebody/something a comedy about an eccentric family
  - She went on to star in a hit comedy series.
  - His TV comedy shows ran during the 70s and 80s.
  - a comedy series/show

### [comfortable](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/comfortable) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌmfətəbl/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌmfərtəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comfo/comfortable__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comfo/comfortable__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's such a comfortable bed.
  - These new shoes are not very comfortable.
  - clothes that are comfortable to wear
  - a warm comfortable house
  - Let me slip into something more comfortable (= change into more comfortable clothing).

### [comment](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/comment_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒment/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːment/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/comme/comment__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/comme/comment__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - comment on something She made helpful comments on my work.
  - thoughtful/interesting/insightful comments
  - snide/critical/derogatory comments
  - comment about something Have you any comment to make about the cause of the disaster?
  - without comment He handed me the document without comment.
  - The director was not available for comment.
  - You have to register to post a comment.
  - Please leave a comment on our website.
  - Keep up to date with all the latest news and comment.
  - You can post a question in the comments section below.
  - (especially British English) What she said was fair comment (= a reasonable criticism).

### [communicate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/communicate) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmjuːnɪkeɪt/` · **US:** `/kəˈmjuːnɪkeɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commu/communicate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commu/communicate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We only communicate by email.
  - They communicated in sign language.
  - Candidates must be able to communicate effectively.
  - communicate with somebody/something Dolphins use sound to communicate with each other.
  - Direct marketing allows a firm to communicate directly with its customers.
  - communicate something In times of uncertainty, a leader must be able to communicate a message of hope.
  - Language evolved as a means of communicating information between individuals.
  - communicate something to somebody He was eager to communicate his ideas to the group.
  - Her nervousness was communicating itself to the children.
  - communicate how/what, etc… They failed to communicate what was happening and why.

### [community](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/community) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈmjuːnəti/` · **US:** `/kəˈmjuːnəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/commu/community__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/commu/community__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The local community supported us from the start.
  - the international community (= the countries of the world as a group)
  - It is hoped that the campaign will encourage members of the community to get active.
  - the problems faced by rural communities
  - good community relations with the police
  - community groups/leaders
  - in/within the community health workers based in the community (= working with people in a local area)
  - (North American English) community parks/libraries (= paid for by the local town/city)
  - the wider/entire/whole community
  - She comes from a small island community.
  - Our aim is to serve the community.

### [compete](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/compete) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpiːt/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/compete__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/compete__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The games were an outstanding success, with almost 2 400 athletes competing.
  - compete in something He's hoping to compete in the London marathon.
  - Only 21 bodybuilders competed in the event.
  - compete against somebody They are competing against teams of full-time professional players.
  - compete for something He is expected to compete for the title of world champion next year.

### [competition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/competition) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌkɒmpəˈtɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌkɑːmpəˈtɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compe/competition__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compe/competition__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a design/an art/a talent competition
  - an international/a national competition
  - Hundreds of schools entered the competition.
  - She's won a prestigious singing competition.
  - in a competition He won fourth place in the competition.
  - a competition to do something a competition to design the new Parliament building

### [complain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/complain) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpleɪn/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpleɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/complain__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/complain__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She never complains, but she's obviously exhausted.
  - (informal) ‘How are you?’ ‘Oh, I can't complain (= I'm all right).’
  - complain to somebody Annoyed residents have complained to the police.
  - complain about something Shoppers complained about the lack of toilet facilities.
  - complain to somebody about something I'm going to complain to the manager about this.
  - complain of something The defendant complained of intimidation during the investigation.
  - complain at something She complained at the unfairness of it all.
  - complain (that)… He complained bitterly that he had been unfairly treated.
  - + speech ‘It's not fair,’ she complained.

### [completely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/completely) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/kəmˈpliːtli/` · **US:** `/kəmˈpliːtli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/com/compl/completely__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/com/compl/completely__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was completely different from how I had imagined it.
  - I've completely forgotten her name.
  - The building was completely destroyed by the explosion.
  - The technique is completely new.
  - This practice is completely unacceptable.
  - Remove the cake from the tin and leave to cool completely.
  - The waiter completely ignored my request.
  - He should recover completely in time.
  - I agree completely with what you say.

### [condition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/condition_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈdɪʃn/` · **US:** `/kənˈdɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcond/xcondition__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcond/xcondition__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in… condition to be in pristine/excellent/perfect condition
  - Many of the paintings are in poor condition.
  - the condition of something The condition of the roads is poor.

### [conference](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/conference) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒnfərəns/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːnfərəns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconference__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconf/xconference__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The hotel is used for exhibitions, conferences and social events.
  - to host/organize a conference
  - to speak at/address a conference
  - The conference will be held in Glasgow.
  - Delegates to the Labour Party's annual conference will be staying in the Grand Hotel.
  - the national conference of the American Psychiatric Association
  - at a conference We met at an international conference.
  - The resolution will be debated at the party conference in Brighton.
  - conference on something She is attending a three-day conference on AIDS education.
  - conference for somebody a conference for health professionals from around the world
  - a conference room/centre

### [connect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/connect) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈnekt/` · **US:** `/kəˈnekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconn/xconnect__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconn/xconnect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - connect A and B The towns are connected by train and bus services.
  - connect A to B The island is connected to the mainland by a bridge.
  - connect A with B The canal was built to connect Sheffield with the Humber estuary.
  - The rooms on this floor connect.
  - a connecting door (= one that connects two rooms)

### [connected](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/connected) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈnektɪd/` · **US:** `/kəˈnektɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xconn/xconnected__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xconn/xconnected__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We do not believe that the incidents are connected.
  - The two issues are closely connected.
  - market prices and other connected matters
  - connected with somebody/something The fall in retail sales is directly connected with the decline in employment.
  - jobs connected with the environment
  - connected to somebody/something Many computer files connected to the case have been erased.
  - connected by something They are connected by marriage.

### [consider](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/consider) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈsɪdə(r)/` · **US:** `/kənˈsɪdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsider__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcons/xconsider__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'd like some time to consider.
  - consider something She is considering her options.
  - Let us consider the facts.
  - We are considering various possibilities.
  - He was seriously considering an appeal.
  - a carefully considered response
  - The company is being actively considered as a potential partner (= it is thought possible that it could become one).
  - consider doing something Have you considered starting your own business?
  - consider how/what, etc… We need to consider how the law might be reformed.
  - He was considering what to do next.
  - The court must consider whether the employer was negligent.
  - consider somebody/something for something We are considering her for the job of designer.

### [contain](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/contain) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈteɪn/` · **US:** `/kənˈteɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontain__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontain__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This drink doesn't contain any alcohol.
  - The documents contain sensitive information.
  - Electronic products contain hazardous materials that cannot be deposited in landfills.
  - His account contained an element of truth.
  - to contain an amount/a number/a level of something
  - He handed over a brown envelope containing a hundred dollar bills.
  - The fish were found to contain traces of mercury.
  - The bottle contains (= can hold) two litres.
  - contained in something He said he was opposed to some of the provisions contained in the bill.
  - contained within something The stories contained within these pages are highly enjoyable.

### [context](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/context) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntekst/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntekst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontext__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontext__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Such databases are being used in a wide range of contexts.
  - to examine the wider/broader context of the war
  - the historical/cultural/social context
  - The government must consider the context of the protests.
  - in a context This kind of propaganda is used in many different contexts.
  - Institutions provide a context in which individuals can take on different roles.
  - in/within the context of something This speech needs to be set in the context of Britain in the 1960s.
  - in context His decision can only be understood in context.
  - I think it's important to put this into context.
  - context for something A ceasefire is the essential context for meaningful negotiations.

### [continent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/continent_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒntɪnənt/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːntɪnənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinent__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the continent of Africa
  - the African continent
  - The police investigation spanned three continents.
  - across a continent They trekked 1  500 miles across the Australian continent in 1931.
  - on a continent the strongest earthquake ever recorded on the North American continent

### [continue](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/continue) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtɪnjuː/` · **US:** `/kənˈtɪnjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinue__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontinue__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If the current trend continues, that number will increase 165 per cent by 2050.
  - Police have not yet made any arrests, but investigations continue.
  - + adv./prep. The exhibition continues until 25 July.
  - Fighting continued for four years.
  - The rain will continue into the evening.
  - Unions declared that the strike would continue indefinitely.
  - Life continued on as normal.
  - Work continues apace on the second phase of the development.
  - The tradition continues to this day.
  - + adj. The violence continues unabated.
  - continue to do something The economy is continuing to grow.
  - The number of car accident deaths is continuing to decline.
  - Costs continued to rise.
  - The rain continued to fall all afternoon.
  - continue doing something The rain continued falling all afternoon.

### [control](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/control_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtrəʊl/` · **US:** `/kənˈtrəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrol__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrol__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - control of somebody/something The party expects to gain control of the council in the next election.
  - Militants have taken control of the town.
  - The Democrats have lost control of Congress.
  - A military junta seized control of the country.
  - The founders eventually regained control of the company.
  - control over somebody/something He defended the tradition of civilian control over the military.
  - in the control of somebody/something The city is in the control of enemy forces.
  - under… control The area remains under international control.

### [control](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/control_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kənˈtrəʊl/` · **US:** `/kənˈtrəʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrol__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/x/xco/xcont/xcontrol__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - By the age of 21 he controlled the company.
  - The whole territory is now controlled by the army.
  - Can't you control your children?
  - a multinational company based in Britain but controlled from South Africa
  - We want to control our own destiny (= decide what happens to us).
  - attempts by corporations to control access to information

### [cook](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cook_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kʊk/` · **US:** `/kʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/cook_/cook__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/cook_/cook__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - John is a very good cook (= he cooks well).
  - Who was the cook (= who cooked the food)?
  - She was employed as a cook in a hotel.

### [cooker](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cooker) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʊkə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkʊkər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/coo/cooke/cooker__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/coo/cooke/cooker__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a gas cooker
  - an electric cooker
  - on a cooker She set the house on fire by leaving a chip pan on the cooker.

### [copy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/copy_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒpi/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːpi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cop/copy_/copy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cop/copy_/copy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The thieves replaced the original painting with a copy.
  - copy of something a copy of a letter/report/document
  - a copy of a gene
  - to have/get/obtain/receive a copy of something
  - He made careful copies of all the documents he found in the library.

### [copy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/copy_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɒpi/` · **US:** `/ˈkɑːpi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cop/copy_/copy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cop/copy_/copy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - copy something He taught himself by copying paintings in the Louvre.
  - The product has been widely copied by other manufacturers.
  - copy something from something They copied the designs from those on Greek vases.

### [corner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/corner_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɔːnə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkɔːrnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corne/corner__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corne/corner__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the four corners of a square
  - I hit my knee on the corner of the table.
  - Write your address in the top right-hand corner of the letter.
  - the left/right corner
  - the north-west/north-east/south-east/south-west corner
  - A smile lifted the corner of his mouth.
  - in the corner of something a speck of dirt in the corner of her eye
  - He scored with a shot into the bottom corner of the goal.
  - She tucked the ball into the corner of the net.

### [correctly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/correctly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/kəˈrektli/` · **US:** `/kəˈrektli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cor/corre/correctly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cor/corre/correctly__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Have you spelled it correctly?
  - Students correctly identified 16 trees species found on their study sites.
  - If I remember correctly, he was still the prime minister at that point.
  - They reasoned, correctly, that she was away for the weekend.

### [count](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/count_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kaʊnt/` · **US:** `/kaʊnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/count/count__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/count/count__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Billy can't count yet.
  - count (up) to something She can count up to 10 in Italian.
  - count from something (to/up to something) to count from 1 to 10

### [couple](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/couple_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌpl/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌpl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cou/coupl/couple__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cou/coupl/couple__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I saw a couple of men get out.

### [cover](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cover_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌvə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌvər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cov/cover/cover__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cov/cover/cover__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cover something with something Cover the chicken loosely with foil.
  - She covered her face with her hands.
  - cover something I covered my mouth to stifle a yawn.
  - (figurative) He laughed to cover (= hide) his nervousness.

### [crazy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/crazy_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkreɪzi/` · **US:** `/ˈkreɪzi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cra/crazy/crazy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cra/crazy/crazy__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Are you crazy? We could get killed doing that.
  - I'd never do anything like that. That's just crazy.
  - He drove like an idiot, passing in the craziest places.
  - What a crazy idea!
  - ‘Love makes you do crazy things,’ she said.
  - I know it sounds crazy but it just might work.
  - be crazy to do something She must be crazy to lend him money.
  - it is crazy to do something It's crazy to think he'll ever change.

### [creative](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/creative_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/kriˈeɪtɪv/` · **US:** `/kriˈeɪtɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/creat/creative__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/creat/creative__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a course on creative writing (= writing stories, plays and poems)
  - creative thinking (= thinking about problems in a new way or thinking of new ideas)
  - the company’s creative team/director
  - the creative process
  - She channels her creative energy into her art.
  - creative with something (informal) You can get creative with this recipe and add whatever fruit you like.

### [credit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/credit_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkredɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈkredɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cre/credi/credit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cre/credi/credit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to get credit
  - on credit We bought the dishwasher on credit.
  - to offer interest-free credit (= allow somebody to pay later, without any extra charge)
  - Someone with a bad credit history is less likely to be lent money.
  - a credit agreement
  - a credit facility
  - Your credit limit is now £2 000.
  - He's a bad credit risk (= he is unlikely to pay the money later).
  - The credit crisis is far from over.

### [crime](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/crime) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kraɪm/` · **US:** `/kraɪm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cri/crime/crime__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cri/crime/crime__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This month's figures show an increase in violent crime.
  - There needs to be a partnership between police and public in the fight against crime.
  - Stores spend more and more on crime prevention every year.
  - DNA data is extremely useful in fighting serious crime.
  - He began to drift into a life of petty crime (= crime that is not very serious).
  - gun/knife crime
  - a rise in street crime
  - He turned to crime when he dropped out of school.
  - The crime rate is rising.
  - crime fiction/novels (= stories about crime)
  - crime figures/statistics
  - Victims of crime may be able to obtain compensation.
  - She’s a crime writer (= she writes stories about crime).

### [criminal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/criminal_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkrɪmɪnl/` · **US:** `/ˈkrɪmɪnl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cri/crimi/criminal__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cri/crimi/criminal__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a convicted criminal
  - Police sometimes put themselves in danger when arresting violent criminals.
  - Society does not know how to deal with hardened criminals (= people who regularly commit crimes and are not sorry for what they do).
  - (especially North American English) a career criminal (= who earns their income through criminal activities)
  - He admits to being a petty criminal (= who commits crimes that are not very serious).
  - white-collar criminals
  - a gang of criminals

### [cross](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cross_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/krɒs/` · **US:** `/krɔːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cro/cross/cross__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cro/cross/cross__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've put a cross on the map to show where the hotel is.
  - Put a tick if the answer is correct and a cross if it's wrong.
  - Sign your name on the form where I've put a cross.
  - Those who could not write signed with a cross.
  - Put a cross beside the name of the candidate you wish to vote for.

### [cross](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cross_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/krɒs/` · **US:** `/krɔːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cro/cross/cross__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cro/cross/cross__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - As soon as traffic slowed down enough to safely cross, I started walking.
  - cross over I waved and she crossed over (= crossed the road towards me).
  - cross (over) from … He crossed over from the other side of the road.
  - cross (over) from… to/into… We crossed from Dover to Calais.
  - cross (over) to/into… She crossed to the other side of the room.
  - cross something to cross the road/street
  - to cross the sea/mountains
  - to cross France by train
  - The bridge crosses the River Dee.
  - He was caught trying to cross the border illegally.
  - More than 150 000 people crossed the bridge that day.
  - A look of annoyance crossed her face.
  - They crossed the finishing line together (= in a race).
  - cross over something He crossed over the road and joined me.

### [crowd](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/crowd_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kraʊd/` · **US:** `/kraʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cro/crowd/crowd__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cro/crowd/crowd__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He pushed his way through the crowd.
  - A small crowd had gathered outside the church.
  - Police had to break up the crowd.
  - The crowd was/were shouting and cheering.
  - crowd of somebody/something Crowds of people poured into the street.
  - He left the hotel surrounded by crowds of journalists.
  - Several speakers addressed the crowd at the rally.
  - a large/huge crowd
  - The match attracted a capacity crowd of 80 000 (= the maximum number of people who could attend).
  - before a crowd The President spoke before a crowd of more than 50 000 in the city's football stadium.
  - among a crowd The mayor of Paris was among the crowd that had gathered for the street celebration.
  - to attract/draw a crowd
  - I want to get there early to avoid the crowds.
  - The crowd cheered the winning hit.
  - Nearly 300 marshals will be involved in crowd control.
  - crowd trouble
  - A whole crowd of us (= a lot of us) are going to the ball.

### [crowded](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/crowded) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkraʊdɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈkraʊdɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cro/crowd/crowded__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cro/crowd/crowded__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We made our way through the crowded streets.
  - a crowded bar
  - The main beach can get really crowded in summer.
  - London was very crowded.
  - crowded with somebody In the spring the place is crowded with skiers.

### [cry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cry_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kraɪ/` · **US:** `/kraɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cry/cry__/cry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cry/cry__/cry__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's all right. Don't cry.
  - I just couldn't stop crying.
  - The little boy fell over and started to cry.
  - cry for somebody/something The baby was crying for (= because it wanted) its mother.
  - cry about/over something There's nothing to cry about.
  - cry with something He felt like crying with rage.
  - She threw her arms around his neck crying tears of joy.
  - He started to cry real tears.
  - + speech ‘Waaa!’ she cried.
  - (informal) I found him crying his eyes out (= crying very much).
  - That night she cried herself to sleep.

### [cupboard](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cupboard) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkʌbəd/` · **US:** `/ˈkʌbərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cup/cupbo/cupboard__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cup/cupbo/cupboard__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - kitchen cupboards
  - a stationery/linen cupboard
  - a cupboard door
  - There's plenty of food in the cupboards.
  - I found it hidden in the back of a cupboard.

### [curly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/curly) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkɜːli/` · **US:** `/ˈkɜːrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cur/curly/curly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cur/curly/curly__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - long curly hair
  - I wish my hair was curly.
  - a curly-haired girl
  - a curly-headed boy
  - a dog with a curly tail
  - curly cabbage leaves
  - She wished she had naturally curly hair.
  - Your hair’s gone all curly!

### [cycle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cycle_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsaɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈsaɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cyc/cycle/cycle__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cyc/cycle/cycle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was riding his cycle home when he was hit by a lorry.
  - We went for a cycle ride on Sunday.
  - on a cycle Lots of people arrived in cars or on cycles.
  - by cycle They completed their journey by cycle.

### [cycle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/cycle_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsaɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈsaɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/c/cyc/cycle/cycle__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/c/cyc/cycle/cycle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - measures to encourage more people to cycle
  - + adv./prep. I usually cycle home through the park.
  - He cycles ten miles to and from work every day.


## D

### [daily](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/daily_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdeɪli/` · **US:** `/ˈdeɪli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dai/daily/daily__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dai/daily/daily__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Many people still read a daily newspaper.
  - events affecting the daily lives of millions of people
  - The daily routine is the same for all prisoners.
  - Keep a record of your daily activities.
  - the recommended daily intake of vitamin C
  - your daily dose of vitamins/news/humour
  - Invoices are signed on a daily basis.

### [danger](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/danger) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdeɪndʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈdeɪndʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dan/dange/danger__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dan/dange/danger__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Danger! Keep Out!
  - Firefighters face danger every day.
  - in danger Children's lives are in danger every time they cross this road.
  - in grave/mortal danger
  - They found themselves in grave danger.
  - in danger of something species in imminent danger of extinction
  - out of danger Doctors said she is now out of danger (= not likely to die).

### [dark](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dark_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɑːk/` · **US:** `/dɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dar/dark_/dark__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dar/dark_/dark__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Are the children afraid of the dark?
  - in the dark All the lights went out and we were left in the dark.
  - animals that can see in the dark

### [data](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/data) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdɑːtə/` · **US:** `/ˈdætə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dat/data_/data__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dat/data_/data__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We collected publicly available data over a 10-day period.
  - to gather/obtain data
  - What methods are you using to interpret the data?
  - data on something Researchers analysed the data on 2 515 patient visits at the Children's Hospital.
  - data for something Table 2 provides data for southeast Nebraska.
  - Our model is based on experimental data.
  - published/unpublished data
  - scientific/historical/clinical data
  - It is essential to protect your personal data.
  - raw data (= that has not been analysed)
  - Multiple data collection methods were used.
  - (specialist) These data show that most cancers are detected as a result of clinical follow-up.

### [dead](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dead_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ded/` · **US:** `/ded/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dea/dead_/dead__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dea/dead_/dead__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - My mother's dead; she died in 2017.
  - a dead person/animal
  - dead leaves/wood/skin
  - Catherine's dead body lay peacefully on the bed.
  - He was shot dead by a gunman outside his home.
  - A high school shooting has left six students dead.
  - She was pronounced dead at the scene of the accident.
  - The poor child looks more dead than alive.
  - He was missing, presumed dead.
  - (informal) He dropped dead (= died suddenly) last week.
  - dead from/of something One short year later, Fred would be dead from a drug overdose.
  - (figurative) In ten years he'll be dead and buried as a politician.

### [deal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deal_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/diːl/` · **US:** `/diːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dea/deal_/deal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dea/deal_/deal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Whose turn is it to deal?
  - deal (something) (out) (to somebody) Start by dealing out ten cards to each player.
  - deal somebody something He dealt me two aces.

### [dear](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dear_2) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/dɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/dɪr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dea/dear_/dear__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dea/dear_/dear__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Oh dear! I think I've lost my purse!
  - Oh dear! What a shame.
  - Dear me! What a mess!
  - Dear oh dear! What are you going to do now?

### [death](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/death) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/deθ/` · **US:** `/deθ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dea/death/death__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dea/death/death__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the anniversary of his wife’s death
  - his sudden/untimely/premature death
  - the tragic death of a child
  - The jury returned a verdict of accidental death.
  - It is believed she died a violent death.
  - He suffered a slow and painful death.
  - death from something an increase in deaths from cancer

### [decision](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/decision) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈsɪʒn/` · **US:** `/dɪˈsɪʒn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dec/decis/decision__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dec/decis/decision__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I think I've made the right decision.
  - (British English) No decisions have yet been taken.
  - decision on something We need a decision on this by next week.
  - decision about something I will consult colleagues before making a final decision about how to proceed.
  - We must come to a decision about what to do next.
  - We finally reached a decision (= decided after some difficulty).
  - decision to do something Who made the decision to go ahead with the project?
  - Choosing a college is a big decision for students and their families.
  - a difficult/tough/hard decision
  - It is almost impossible for the average person to make an informed decision (= one based on evidence).
  - The government plans to appeal the decision in the High Court.
  - to reverse/overturn a decision on appeal
  - The editor's decision is final.

### [deep](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/deep_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/diːp/` · **US:** `/diːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dee/deep_/deep__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dee/deep_/deep__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a deep hole/well/river
  - deep water/snow
  - The water looks quite deep there.
  - Around the world, the deep oceans are heating.
  - Sunflowers have deep roots.
  - (figurative) The custom has deep roots in the community.

### [definitely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/definitely) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdefɪnətli/` · **US:** `/ˈdefɪnətli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/def/defin/definitely__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/def/defin/definitely__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I definitely remember sending the letter.
  - ‘Was it what you expected?’ ‘Yes, definitely.’
  - ‘Do you plan to have children?’ ‘Definitely not!'
  - Some old people want help; others most definitely do not.
  - The claim is definitely true.
  - If you want to know more I'd definitely recommend checking out this guide .

### [degree](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/degree) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈɡriː/` · **US:** `/dɪˈɡriː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/deg/degre/degree__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/deg/degre/degree__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at… degrees Water freezes at 32 degrees Fahrenheit (32°F) or zero/nought degrees Celsius (0°C).
  - above/below… degrees Temperatures are expected to drop below 2 degrees.
  - Last weekend temperatures reached 40 degrees.

### [dentist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/dentist) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdentɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈdentɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/den/denti/dentist__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/den/denti/dentist__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to see/visit/consult your dentist
  - the dentist's chair/drill

### [department](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/department) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈpɑːtmənt/` · **US:** `/dɪˈpɑːrtmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/depar/department__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/depar/department__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - department of something the Department of Health
  - He is a lecturer in the department of psychology at Trinity College, Dublin.
  - the Treasury Department
  - a government/university, etc. department
  - the marketing/sales, etc. department
  - The children's department (= in a large store) sells a wide range of good quality clothes.
  - the English department
  - in a department She used to work in the IT department.
  - He later served as head of the art history department.

### [depend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/depend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈpend/` · **US:** `/dɪˈpend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/dep/depen/depend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/dep/depen/depend__us_1.mp3)

### [desert](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/desert_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdezət/` · **US:** `/ˈdezərt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/deser/desert__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/deser/desert__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the Sahara Desert
  - Somalia is mostly desert.
  - They travelled many miles across burning desert sands.
  - Occasionally, we passed a desert oasis surrounded by small tracts of grass and shrub.
  - (figurative) The town has become a cultural desert (= a place without any culture).

### [designer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/designer_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈzaɪnə(r)/` · **US:** `/dɪˈzaɪnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/desig/designer__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/desig/designer__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - one of the country's top fashion designers
  - a web/game designer

### [destroy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/destroy) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈstrɔɪ/` · **US:** `/dɪˈstrɔɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/des/destr/destroy__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/des/destr/destroy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The building was completely destroyed by fire.
  - The earthquake damaged or destroyed countless homes.
  - Some of the habitat has already been totally destroyed.
  - They've destroyed all the evidence.

### [detective](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/detective) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dɪˈtektɪv/` · **US:** `/dɪˈtektɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/d/det/detec/detective__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/d/det/detec/detective__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a police/homicide/undercover detective
  - Several objects were taken away by detectives from the anti-terrorist squad.
