  - historical/romantic/crime fiction
  - to write/read fiction
  - a crime fiction writer

### [field](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/field_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fiːld/` · **US:** `/fiːld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fie/field/field__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fie/field/field__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We had to walk across a ploughed field.
  - in a field We camped in a field near the village.
  - People were working in the fields.
  - field of something We saw golden fields of wheat.
  - He planted a field of beans.
  - a rice/wheat field
  - The house overlooks the sugar cane fields.

### [fight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fight_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/faɪt/` · **US:** `/faɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fig/fight/fight__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fig/fight/fight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a street/gang fight
  - fight with somebody/something He got into a fight with a man in the bar.
  - (figurative) I had a fight with the ticket machine, which was being temperamental.
  - fight between A and B A fight broke out between rival groups of fans.

### [fight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fight_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/faɪt/` · **US:** `/faɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fig/fight/fight__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fig/fight/fight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - soldiers trained to fight
  - He fought in Vietnam.
  - fight against somebody My grandfather fought against the Fascists in Spain.
  - fight somebody/something to fight a war/battle
  - They gathered soldiers to fight the invading army.
  - fight for something They fought for control of the island.
  - fight somebody/something for something Didn't we fight a war for freedom?
  - fight over something They were fighting over disputed land.
  - fight somebody/something over something Future wars will be fought over water supplies.

### [figure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/figure_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪɡə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪɡjər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fig/figur/figure__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fig/figur/figure__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the latest sales/crime/unemployment figures
  - Official figures indicate that crime is falling.
  - Figures for April show a slight improvement on previous months.
  - By 2017, this figure had risen to 14 million.
  - Viewing figures for the series have dropped dramatically.
  - Figures released by the hospital reveal a rise in the number of admissions.
  - Experts put the real figure at closer to 75%.
  - according to… figures According to government figures, 3.6 million children are living in poverty.
  - Her argument is backed up with plenty of facts and figures.

### [film](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/film_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɪlm/` · **US:** `/fɪlm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fil/film_/film__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fil/film_/film__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They are filming in Moscow right now.
  - film something/somebody The show was filmed on location in New York.
  - It took them six weeks to film the documentary.
  - That day, we filmed the scene in the church.
  - film somebody/something doing something A young boy was filmed by the security camera stealing a pair of jeans.

### [final](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/final_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfaɪnl/` · **US:** `/ˈfaɪnl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/final/final__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/final/final__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She reached the final of the 100m hurdles.
  - The players met in last year's final.
  - The winner of each contest goes through to the grand final.
  - The victory secured them a place in the final.
  - final against somebody a nail-biting final against last year's victor

### [finally](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/finally) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfaɪnəli/` · **US:** `/ˈfaɪnəli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/final/finally__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/final/finally__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The performance finally started half an hour late.
  - The law was finally passed in May 2019.
  - I finally managed to get her attention.
  - Are you finally going to tell me why I’m here?
  - When they finally arrived it was well past midnight.
  - Two years of hard work and waiting were finally over.
  - Here, finally, we come to the heart of the problem.
  - Finally ready, they all set off.

### [finger](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/finger_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪŋɡə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪŋɡər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/finge/finger__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/finge/finger__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She ran her fingers through her hair.
  - Hold the material between finger and thumb.
  - He was about to speak but she raised a finger to her lips.
  - The old man wagged his finger at the youths.
  - Tina curled her slender fingers into a fist.

### [finish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/finish_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪnɪʃ/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪnɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fin/finis/finish__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fin/finis/finish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was a close finish as they had predicted.
  - They won in the end but it was a tight finish.
  - finish to something a dramatic finish to the race
  - The story was a lie from start to finish.
  - to the finish I want to see the job through to the finish.
  - at the finish Several runners needed medical attention at the finish.

### [first](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/first_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fɜːst/` · **US:** `/fɜːrst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fir/first/first__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fir/first/first__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was the first in my family to go to college.
  - Sheila and Jim were the first to arrive.

### [firstly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/firstly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɜːstli/` · **US:** `/ˈfɜːrstli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fir/first/firstly__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fir/first/firstly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There are two reasons for this decision: firstly…
  - Firstly let me introduce myself.
  - So where do we start? Well firstly, you need to assess your financial situation.

### [fish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fish_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɪʃ/` · **US:** `/fɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fis/fish_/fish__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fis/fish_/fish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The trawler was fishing off the coast of Iceland.
  - Several people were fishing in the river.
  - fish for something You can fish for trout in this stream.

### [fishing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fishing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪʃɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪʃɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fis/fishi/fishing__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fis/fishi/fishing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They often go fishing.
  - We enjoyed a day's fishing by the river.
  - deep-sea fishing
  - trout/salmon/tuna fishing
  - a fishing boat/vessel
  - the fishing industry
  - Do you want to go on a fishing trip this summer?
  - the rich fishing grounds off the coast of Namibia
  - the picturesque fishing village of Polperro

### [fit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fit_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/fɪt/` · **US:** `/fɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fit/fit__/fit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fit/fit__/fit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Top athletes have to be very fit.
  - She tries to keep fit by jogging every day.
  - Hiking helps keep him physically fit.
  - All the recruits seemed fit and healthy.
  - my new year's resolution to get fit
  - He is back in training after his back injury but is not yet considered match fit (= fit enough to play in a match).

### [fit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fit_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɪt/` · **US:** `/fɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fit/fit__/fit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fit/fit__/fit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I tried the dress on but it didn't fit.
  - That jacket fits well.
  - My shoes fit perfectly.
  - a close-fitting dress
  - fit somebody/something I can't find clothes to fit me.
  - The key doesn't fit the lock.

### [fix](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fix_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/fɪks/` · **US:** `/fɪks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fix/fix__/fix__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fix/fix__/fix__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The car won't start—can you fix it?
  - I've fixed the problem.
  - Businesses and government have spent billions of dollars to find and fix the bug.
  - We need to get the TV fixed.
  - If you suspect that you have a fuel leak have it fixed as soon as possible.

### [flat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flat_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/flæt/` · **US:** `/flæt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fla/flat_/flat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fla/flat_/flat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - low buildings with flat roofs
  - I need a flat surface to write on.
  - A large flat screen was mounted on the wall.
  - We found a large flat rock to sit on.
  - People used to think the earth was flat.
  - a large, deep dish with a flat bottom
  - Exercise is the only way to get a flat stomach after having a baby.

### [flu](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flu) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/fluː/` · **US:** `/fluː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/flu/flu__/flu__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/flu/flu__/flu__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The whole family has the flu.
  - (British English) She's got flu.
  - She was off work for four days because of a bout with the flu.
  - a flu outbreak/epidemic/pandemic
  - a flu vaccine/vaccination
  - (British English, informal) a flu jab
  - (especially North American English, informal) a flu shot
  - He is suffering from flu-like symptoms.

### [fly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fly_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/flaɪ/` · **US:** `/flaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fly/fly__/fly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fly/fly__/fly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A fly was buzzing against the window.
  - Flies rose in thick black swarms.
  - A fly settled on the butter.
  - I swatted the fly with a newspaper.

### [flying](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flying_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈflaɪɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈflaɪɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fly/flyin/flying__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fly/flyin/flying__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - flying insects

### [flying](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/flying_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈflaɪɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈflaɪɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fly/flyin/flying__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fly/flyin/flying__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm terrified of flying.

### [focus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/focus_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfəʊkəs/` · **US:** `/ˈfəʊkəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/foc/focus/focus__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/foc/focus/focus__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - focus of something It was the main focus of attention at the meeting.
  - The primary focus of the book is practical teaching techniques.
  - He decided to make colour the central focus of his work.
  - focus for something His comments provided a focus for debate.
  - focus on something We shall maintain our focus on the needs of the customer.
  - In today's lecture the focus will be on tax structures within the European Union.
  - The incident brought the problem of violence in schools into sharp focus.
  - The film loses focus near the end.
  - He began to shift his focus towards writing for the theatre.
  - What we need now is a change of focus (= to look at things in a different way).

### [focus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/focus_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfəʊkəs/` · **US:** `/ˈfəʊkəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/foc/focus/focus__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/foc/focus/focus__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was distracted and finding it hard to focus.
  - focus on/upon somebody/something The discussion focused on three main issues.
  - Each exercise focuses on a different grammar point.
  - focus + adv./prep. He had artistic interests but his teachers encouraged him to focus elsewhere.
  - The essay seems too narrowly focused.
  - focus something Music and crafts can help focus small children's energies.
  - focus something on/upon somebody/something The visit helped to focus world attention on the plight of the refugees.
  - (figurative) All eyes are focused on the presidential elections at the moment.
  - (figurative) The event will focus the spotlight on younger athletes.
  - focus something + adv./prep. We need to decide where to focus our efforts.

### [following](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/following_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɒləʊɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈfɑːləʊɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fol/follo/following__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fol/follo/following__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Answer the following questions.
  - The following article was written in 2017.

### [foreign](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/foreign) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɒrən/` · **US:** `/ˈfɔːrən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/forei/foreign__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/forei/foreign__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What foreign languages do you speak?
  - It can be a challenge at first living in a foreign country.
  - I went to the bank to get some foreign currency.
  - a foreign-owned company
  - foreign holidays
  - He spoke with a foreign accent.
  - You could tell she was foreign by the way she dressed.

### [formal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/formal) *(adjective)*

### [fortunately](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/fortunately) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɔːtʃənətli/` · **US:** `/ˈfɔːrtʃənətli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/for/fortu/fortunately__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/for/fortu/fortunately__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I was late, but fortunately the meeting hadn't started.
  - The documents fortunately survived the fire.

### [forward](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/forward_1) *(adverb)*

  - He stands by the fire, rocking backward and forward.

- **UK:** `/ˈfɜːnɪtʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɜːrnɪtʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fur/furni/furniture__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fur/furni/furniture__us_2.mp3)

### [further](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/further_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɜːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfɜːrðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fur/furth/further__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fur/furth/further__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - For further details call this number.

  - No further developments have been reported.
  - We have decided to take no further action.

### [future](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/future_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfjuːtʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈfjuːtʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/f/fut/futur/future__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/f/fut/futur/future__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The land will remain untouched for future generations to enjoy.
  - The reasons for the decision will be announced at a future date.
  - future developments in artificial intelligence
  - present and future trends in industry
  - the ability to anticipate possible future events
  - She told us about her future plans.
  - He met his future wife at law school.
  - I wrote down the name of the hotel for future reference (= because it might be useful in the future).

### [general](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/general_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒenrəl/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒenrəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/g/gen/gener/general__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/g/gen/gener/general__us_2_rr.mp3)

  - The bad weather has been fairly general (= has affected most areas).

  - good/bad habits
  - Most of us have some undesirable habits.
  - habit of doing something The strategy is helping children develop the habit of reading for fun.
  - It's all right to borrow money occasionally, but don't let it become a habit.
  - I'd prefer you not to make a habit of it.
  - I'm trying to break the habit of staying up too late.
  - These things have a habit of coming back to haunt you.
  - in the habit of doing something I'm not in the habit of letting strangers into my apartment.
  - I've got into the habit of turning on the TV as soon as I get home.

### [half](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/half_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/hɑːf/` · **US:** `/hæf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hal/half_/half__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hal/half_/half__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The glass was half full.
  - The hall was half empty.
  - She is half Italian.

### [hall](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hall) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hɔːl/` · **US:** `/hɔːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hal/hall_/hall__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hal/hall_/hall__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a hall Her brother was standing in the front hall.
  - She ran into the hall and up the stairs.

### [happily](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/happily) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhæpɪli/` · **US:** `/ˈhæpɪli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hap/happi/happily__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hap/happi/happily__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - children playing happily on the beach
  - to be happily married
  - I think we can manage quite happily on our own.
  - And they all lived happily ever after (= used as the end of a fairy tale).

### [have](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/have_2) *(auxiliary verb)*
- **UK:** `/hæv/` · **US:** `/hæv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hav/have_/have__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hav/have_/have__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've finished my work.
  - He's gone home, hasn't he?
  - ‘Have you seen it?’ ‘Yes, I have/No, I haven’t.’
  - She'll have had the results by now.
  - Had they left before you got there?
  - If I hadn't seen it with my own eyes I wouldn't have believed it.
  - (formal) Had I known that (= if I had known that) I would never have come.

### [headache](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/headache) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhedeɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈhedeɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/heada/headache__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/heada/headache__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He had a terrible headache.
  - Red wine gives me a headache.
  - to suffer from/get headaches
  - to cause headaches
  - I have a splitting headache (= a very bad one).
  - a migraine/tension headache
  - He developed a severe headache.

### [heart](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heart_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hɑːt/` · **US:** `/hɑːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/heart/heart__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/heart/heart__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The patient's heart stopped beating for a few seconds.
  - I could feel my heart pounding in my chest (= because of excitement, etc.).
  - to have a weak heart
  - heart disease/problems
  - to have a heart condition
  - heart patients/surgery
  - a heart transplant

### [heat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heat_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hiːt/` · **US:** `/hiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/heat_/heat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/heat_/heat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Heat rises.
  - He could feel the heat of the sun on his back.
  - Computers generate heat of their own.
  - The fire gave out a fierce heat.

### [heat](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heat_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hiːt/` · **US:** `/hiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/heat_/heat__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/heat_/heat__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - heat (something) Heat the oil and add the onions.
  - The system produced enough energy to heat several thousand homes.
  - While the oven is heating, roll out the dough.
  - heat something through Check the lasagne is heated through before serving.

### [heavy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/heavy_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhevi/` · **US:** `/ˈhevi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hea/heavy/heavy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hea/heavy/heavy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a heavy weight/load
  - She was struggling with a heavy suitcase.
  - My brother is much heavier than me.
  - He tried to push the heavy door open.
  - How heavy is it (= how much does it weigh)?
  - (especially North American English) Many young people today are too heavy (= fat).
  - (figurative) Her father carried a heavy burden of responsibility.

### [height](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/height) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/haɪt/` · **US:** `/haɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hei/heigh/height__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hei/heigh/height__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Height: 210 mm. Width: 57 mm. Length: 170 mm.
  - Please state your height and weight.
  - She is the same height as her sister.
  - to be of medium/average height
  - in height It is almost two metres in height.
  - height of somebody/something You can adjust the height of the chair.
  - The plant can reach a height of over six feet.
  - The table is available in several different heights.

### [helpful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/helpful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhelpfl/` · **US:** `/ˈhelpfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hel/helpf/helpful__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hel/helpf/helpful__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - helpful advice/information
  - helpful hints/tips/suggestions/comments
  - Sorry I can't be more helpful.
  - helpful for somebody This approach may be helpful for people with very severe symptoms.
  - helpful to do something You may find it helpful to read this before making any decisions.
  - It would be helpful to have more detailed information.
  - helpful for somebody to do something It would be helpful for me to see the damage for myself.
  - helpful in doing something Role-play is helpful in developing communication skills.
  - helpful to somebody This final section will be particularly helpful to students.
  - The booklet should be very helpful to parents of children with dyslexia.

### [hero](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hero) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɪərəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈhɪrəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/her/hero_/hero__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/her/hero_/hero__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a war hero (= somebody who was very brave during a war)
  - a hero of the First World War
  - one of the country’s national heroes
  - His charity work has made him something of a local hero.
  - The Olympic team were given a hero's welcome on their return home.
  - Scientists like her are typically unsung heroes (= people who are not praised or famous but deserve to be).
  - He was hailed as a hero after the rescue.

### [hers](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hers) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ɜːz/` · **US:** `/ɜːrz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/her/hers_/hers__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/her/hers_/hers__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His eyes met hers.
  - The choice was hers.
  - a friend of hers

### [herself](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/herself) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/həˈself/` · **US:** `/hərˈself/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/her/herse/herself__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/her/herse/herself__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She hurt herself.
  - She must be very proud of herself.

### [hide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hide_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/haɪd/` · **US:** `/haɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hid/hide_/hide__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hid/hide_/hide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hide somebody/something + adv./prep. He hid the letter in a drawer.
  - hide somebody/something I keep my private papers hidden.
  - hide somebody/something from something (+ adv./prep.) They hid me from the police in their attic.

### [high](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/high_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/haɪ/` · **US:** `/haɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hig/high_/high__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hig/high_/high__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to climb/jump/leap high
  - I can't jump any higher.
  - An eagle soared high overhead.
  - His desk was piled high with papers.
  - high above (something) The castle sits high above the wooded valley.
  - A plane flew high above.

### [hill](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hill) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hɪl/` · **US:** `/hɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hil/hill_/hill__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hil/hill_/hill__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a region of gently rolling hills
  - a hill farm/town/fort
  - on a hill The church is perched on a hill.
  - The house is built on the side of a hill overlooking the river.
  - up a hill They make their way up the hill.
  - down a hill We started to walk back down the hill.
  - in the hills I love walking in the hills (= in the area where there are hills).
  - They stopped at the top of the hill to admire the view.
  - A spring emerges at the bottom of the hill.

### [himself](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/himself) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/hɪmˈself/` · **US:** `/hɪmˈself/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/him/himse/himself__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/him/himse/himself__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He introduced himself.
  - Peter ought to be ashamed of himself.

### [his](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/his_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪz/` · **US:** `/ɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/his/his__/his__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/his/his__/his__us_3_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He took my hand in his.
  - The choice was his.
  - a friend of his

### [hit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hit_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/hɪt/` · **US:** `/hɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hit/hit__/hit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hit/hit__/hit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Give it a good hit.
  - He made the winning hit.

### [hit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hit_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hɪt/` · **US:** `/hɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hit/hit__/hit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hit/hit__/hit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hit somebody/something I was afraid he was going to hit me.
  - hit somebody/something with something She hit him with her umbrella.
  - hit somebody/something in/on something Someone hit him in the face.
  - hit somebody/something in/on something with something He hit the nail squarely on the head with the hammer.

### [hockey](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hockey) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhɒki/` · **US:** `/ˈhɑːki/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hoc/hocke/hockey__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hoc/hocke/hockey__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to play hockey
  - a hockey stick/pitch/player/team

### [hold](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hold_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/həʊld/` · **US:** `/həʊld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hol/hold_/hold__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hol/hold_/hold__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hold somebody/something She was holding a large box.
  - They were holding hands (= the right hand of one person holding the left hand of the other).
  - hold somebody/something + adv./prep. I held the mouse by its tail.
  - The girl held her father's hand tightly.
  - He was holding the baby in his arms.
  - The winning captain held the trophy in the air.
  - The lovers held each other close.

### [hole](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hole_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/həʊl/` · **US:** `/həʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hol/hole_/hole__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hol/hole_/hole__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He dug a deep hole in the garden.
  - hole in something The bomb blew a huge hole in the ground.
  - Water had collected in the holes in the road.
  - She drilled a small hole in the wall.

### [home](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/home_3) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/həʊm/` · **US:** `/həʊm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hom/home_/home__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hom/home_/home__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - home life (= with your family)
  - a person’s home address/country
  - We offer customers a free home delivery service.

### [hope](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hope_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/həʊp/` · **US:** `/həʊp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hop/hope_/hope__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hop/hope_/hope__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't lose hope—we'll find her.
  - She told me all her hopes and dreams.
  - hope for somebody/something They have high hopes for their children.
  - Hopes for the missing men are fading.
  - The Mexican president expressed hope for cooperation on trade.
  - hope of something There are hopes of a lasting peace.
  - hope of doing something They have given up hope of finding any more survivors.
  - Don't raise your hopes too high, or you may be disappointed.
  - I'll do what I can, but don't get your hopes up.
  - in the hope of doing something I called early in the hope of catching her before she went to work.
  - hope that… The situation is not good but we live in hope that it will improve.
  - It is my sincere hope that she will find happiness at last.
  - in the hope that… He asked her again in the vain hope that he could persuade her to come (= it was impossible).
  - in hopes that… (North American English) I am writing this letter in hopes that it will be forwarded to the editor.

### [huge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/huge) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/hjuːdʒ/` · **US:** `/hjuːdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hug/huge_/huge__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hug/huge_/huge__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The sums of money involved are potentially huge.
  - A huge crowd had gathered in the square.
  - Huge amounts of data were lost.
  - I wasn't a huge fan of the movie.
  - Becoming a mother made a huge difference to me.
  - Anti-social behaviour affects a huge number of people daily.
  - The party was a huge success.
  - This is going to be a huge problem for us.

### [human](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/human_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhjuːmən/` · **US:** `/ˈhjuːmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hum/human/human__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hum/human/human__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the human body/brain
  - a terrible loss of human life
  - human development/activity/behaviour
  - Human remains (= the body of a dead person) were found inside the house.
  - Contact with other people is a basic human need.
  - This food is not fit for human consumption.
  - one of the greatest leaders in human history
  - He tried to use a bystander as a human shield.
  - Firefighters formed a human chain (= a line of people) to carry the children to safety.
  - After washing her face, she felt almost human (= like her normal self) again.

### [human](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/human_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈhjuːmən/` · **US:** `/ˈhjuːmən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hum/human/human__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hum/human/human__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Dogs can hear much better than humans.
  - That is no way to treat another human being.
  - the colonization of Europe by modern humans

### [hurt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hurt_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/hɜːt/` · **US:** `/hɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hur/hurt_/hurt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hur/hurt_/hurt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - None of the passengers were badly hurt.
  - Steve didn't look seriously hurt.
  - Stop that or you'll get hurt!

### [hurt](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/hurt_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/hɜːt/` · **US:** `/hɜːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/h/hur/hurt_/hurt__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/h/hur/hurt_/hurt__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hurt something He hurt his back playing squash.
  - Strong light hurts my eyes.
  - hurt yourself Did you hurt yourself?
  - hurt (somebody) Stop it. You're hurting me.
  - No one was seriously hurt in the accident.
  - My back is really hurting me today.
  - My shoes hurt—they're too tight.


## I

### [ideal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ideal_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/aɪˈdiːəl/` · **US:** `/aɪˈdiːəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ide/ideal/ideal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ide/ideal/ideal__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an ideal location/place
  - ideal for somebody/something This beach is ideal for children.
  - She's the ideal candidate for the job.
  - The trip to Paris will be an ideal opportunity to practise my French.
  - The prime minister admitted the current situation was ‘not ideal’ (= was bad).
  - It was not the ideal solution to the problem.
  - Harvesting is being done in less than ideal conditions.

### [identify](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/identify) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/aɪˈdentɪfaɪ/` · **US:** `/aɪˈdentɪfaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ide/ident/identify__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ide/ident/identify__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - identify somebody/something She was able to identify her attacker.
  - Passengers were asked to identify their own suitcases before they were put on the plane.
  - identify yourself Many of those arrested refused to identify themselves (= would not say who they were).
  - identify somebody/something as somebody/something The bodies were identified as those of two suspected drug dealers.
  - identify somebody/something by something two species of waterbirds that can be identified by their distinctive beaks

### [ill](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ill_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪl/` · **US:** `/ɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ill/ill__/ill__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ill/ill__/ill__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her father is seriously ill in St Luke's hospital.
  - critically/gravely/severely ill
  - Uncle Harry is terminally ill with cancer (= he will die from his illness).
  - chronically ill patients (= who are ill for a long time )
  - She was taken ill suddenly.
  - We both started to feel ill shortly after the meal.
  - veterans who became ill after fighting in the Gulf War
  - Shortly after drinking the water I was violently ill.
  - (formal) He fell ill and died soon after.
  - I can't eat bananas as they make me ill.
  - ill with something He'd been ill with cancer for months.

### [illness](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/illness) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪlnəs/` · **US:** `/ˈɪlnəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ill/illne/illness__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ill/illne/illness__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The virus can cause illness in humans.
  - mental/respiratory/chronic illness
  - I missed a lot of school through illness last year.

### [image](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/image) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪmɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈɪmɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ima/image/image__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ima/image/image__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Most simple leaflets will include text and images.
  - We already have more than 22  000 digital images on file.
  - The visual image is steadily replacing the written word.
  - image of somebody/something Images of deer and hunters decorate the cave walls.
  - Consider the images of war that fill the pages of our newspapers.
  - a wooden image of the Hindu god Ganesh
  - An exhibition of images of St Nicholas is on display in the cathedral.
  - in the image of somebody/something In the Bible it states that humans were created in the image of God.

### [immediately](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/immediately_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪˈmiːdiətli/` · **US:** `/ɪˈmiːdiətli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imm/immed/immediately__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imm/immed/immediately__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She answered almost immediately.
  - They immediately began arguing.
  - The point of my question may not be immediately apparent.
  - It is immediately clear that he is out of his depth.
  - Details were not immediately available.

### [impossible](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/impossible) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪmˈpɒsəbl/` · **US:** `/ɪmˈpɑːsəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/imp/impos/impossible__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/imp/impos/impossible__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - almost/virtually/nearly impossible
  - Only a year ago and this would have seemed impossible.
  - Getting any kind of decent job was proving impossible.
  - an impossible dream/goal
  - impossible for somebody Going downstairs would be impossible for him with his sprained ankle.
  - impossible to do The outcome is impossible to predict.
  - It's impossible to prove.
  - Cheap rental accommodation was impossible to find.
  - it is impossible to do something It's impossible to say which is the best.
  - It is impossible to tell whether it will actually work.
  - I find it impossible to lie to her.
  - it is impossible for somebody to do something It's impossible for me to be there before eight.

### [included](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/included) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkluːdɪd/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkluːdɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/inclu/included__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/inclu/included__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - all of Europe (Britain included)
  - Is breakfast included?
  - We all went, me included.
  - included in/on something Also included on the disc are English subtitles.

### [including](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/including) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkluːdɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkluːdɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/inclu/including__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/inclu/including__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I've got three days' holiday including New Year's Day.
  - Six people were killed in the riot, including a policeman.
  - It's £7.50, not including tax.

### [increase](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/increase_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪŋkriːs/` · **US:** `/ˈɪŋkriːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/incre/increase__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/incre/increase__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - price/tax/wage increases
  - Profits show a steady increase.
  - increase in something Last year the business saw a 3% increase in turnover.
  - a substantial increase in the number of products on the market
  - There has been a significant increase in oil prices.
  - The tax has resulted in a dramatic increase in rents.
  - increase of something an increase of 2p in the pound on income tax
  - increase on something The figure is a ten-fold increase on the earlier amount.
  - increase of something on something This is an increase of 12% on the previous year.
  - increase over something This represents a 14% increase over last year.
  - increase from something They report a massive increase from last year.
  - on the increase Homelessness is on the increase (= increasing).
  - The rate of increase is accelerating.

### [increase](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/increase_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkriːs/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkriːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/incre/increase__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/incre/increase__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Costs have increased significantly.
  - to increase dramatically/substantially
  - The price of oil increased.
  - increase in something Oil increased in price.
  - increase by something The rate of inflation increased by 2 per cent.
  - increase from A to B The population has increased from 1.2 million to 1.8 million.
  - increase with something The effectiveness of the vaccine increases with time (= the more time passes, the more effective it is).
  - increase something Sun exposure may increase the risk of skin cancer.
  - Our aim is to increase the number of women in the sport.
  - The company plans to significantly increase product availability over the next year.
  - increase something by something They've increased the price by 50 per cent.
  - increase something from A to B Last month the reward was increased from $20 000 to $40 000.
  - An increasing number of people live alone.
  - increasing levels of carbon dioxide in the earth’s atmosphere

### [incredible](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/incredible) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈkredəbl/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈkredəbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inc/incre/incredible__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inc/incre/incredible__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an incredible story
  - it is incredible that… It seemed incredible that she had been there a week already.
  - it is incredible to somebody that… It's just incredible to me that only one person was hurt.
  - it is incredible how… It really is incredible how she managed to get away with it.
  - it is incredible to do something It's incredible to think that the affair had been going on for years.

### [independent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/independent_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪndɪˈpendənt/` · **US:** `/ˌɪndɪˈpendənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indep/independent__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indep/independent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an independent state/nation/country
  - the newly independent republics of Central Asia in the 1990s
  - Mozambique became independent in 1975.
  - In 1961 the country was declared independent.
  - independent from something The country became fully independent from France in 1960.
  - independent of something Adams urged Congress to declare the American colonies independent of Britain.

### [individual](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/individual_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪndɪˈvɪdʒuəl/` · **US:** `/ˌɪndɪˈvɪdʒuəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indiv/individual__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indiv/individual__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We interviewed each individual member of the community.
  - The minister refused to comment on individual cases.
  - Farmers each have their own individual concerns about the changes.
  - The individual members of the team must all be motivated to contribute.

### [individual](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/individual_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪndɪˈvɪdʒuəl/` · **US:** `/ˌɪndɪˈvɪdʒuəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indiv/individual__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indiv/individual__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The competition is open to both teams and individuals.
  - Treatment depends on the individual involved.
  - He has persuaded private individuals (= ordinary people rather than companies, etc.) to donate to the collection.
  - There is no single individual who is to blame.
  - Wealthy individuals currently account for about 75 per cent of hedge fund investment, and institutions make up the other 25 per cent.
  - We are concerned to protect the rights of the individual.
  - as an individual Every child is treated here as an individual.
  - among individuals Symptoms of depression vary among individuals.
  - between individuals Differences between individuals affect their susceptibility to disease.

### [industry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/industry) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪndəstri/` · **US:** `/ˈɪndəstri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ind/indus/industry__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ind/indus/industry__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the needs of British industry
  - the Department of Trade and Industry
  - in industry She got a job in industry.

### [informal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/informal) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈfɔːml/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈfɔːrml/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inf/infor/informal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inf/infor/informal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an informal atmosphere
  - an informal meeting/gathering/visit
  - an informal arrangement
  - informal discussions/talks
  - an informal chat/conversation
  - Discussions are held on an informal basis within the department.
  - The aim of the trip was to make informal contact with potential customers.
  - These meetings should remain purely informal, with no obligation on either side.

### [injury](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/injury) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪndʒəri/` · **US:** `/ˈɪndʒəri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inj/injur/injury__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inj/injur/injury__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Two people sustained minor injuries.
  - One of the girls suffered serious injuries.
  - He was lucky to escape injury.
  - The passengers escaped with only minor injuries.
  - As a result of the accident, several passengers sustained serious head injuries.
  - injury to somebody/something One youth was treated for injuries to his arm.
  - He died from multiple injuries caused by the accident.
  - There were no injuries in the crash (= no people injured).
  - No damage or injuries were reported.
  - These new guidelines will help save lives and prevent injury.
  - Taking a few simple precautions will reduce your risk of injury.
  - This could result in serious injury or even death.
  - because of injury Two players are out of the team because of injury.
  - (British English, informal) Don't do that. You'll do yourself an injury (= hurt yourself).

### [insect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/insect) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnsekt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnsekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insec/insect__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insec/insect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Most birds spend most of the day eating insects.
  - There are about 30 million species of insects in the world.
  - insect species
  - insect repellent (= a chemical that keeps insects away)
  - an insect bite
  - The pesticide is lethal to all insect life.
  - methods to ensure the plants are as free of disease and insect pests as possible

### [inside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inside_4) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the inside pages of a newspaper
  - an inside pocket
  - (British English) I was driving in the inside lane (= the part nearest the edge, not the middle of the road).

### [inside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inside_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnˈsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnˈsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She shook it to make sure there was nothing inside.
  - He went inside (= indoors) to get his car keys.
  - ‘Get inside!’ he said and closed the door.
  - We had to move inside (= indoors) when it started to rain.
  - (figurative) I pretended not to care but I was screaming inside.

### [inside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inside_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnˈsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnˈsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The inside of the box was blue.
  - the insides of the windows
  - The cake has a slight crust while the inside is soft and moist.
  - PVC is used in dishwashers for lining the inside.
  - from the inside The door was locked from the inside.
  - on the inside The shell is smooth on the inside.

### [inside](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/inside_1) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnˈsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnˈsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/insid/inside__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Go inside the house.
  - Inside the box was a gold watch.
  - She made a film about what life was like inside the refugee camp.
  - You'll feel better with a good meal inside you.
  - (figurative) Inside most of us is a small child screaming for attention.

### [instead](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/instead) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈsted/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈsted/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/inste/instead__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/inste/instead__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Lee was ill so I went instead.
  - He didn't reply. Instead, he turned on his heel and left the room.
  - She said nothing, preferring instead to save her comments till later.
  - She might have pursued a successful career in business, but she chose instead to become a nun.
  - The UN resisted any tougher sanctions, opting instead for more time to give talks a chance.
  - While the Conservatives put the emphasis on the economy, Labour focused instead on public services.

### [instruction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/instruction_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈstrʌkʃn/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈstrʌkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/instr/instruction__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/instr/instruction__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Follow the instructions on the packet carefully.
  - Always read the instructions before you start.
  - Step-by-step instructions are provided.
  - instructions on something The plant comes with full instructions on how to care for it.
  - instructions for something The tin or packet should be clearly labelled with instructions for use.
  - instructions for doing something The website has easy instructions for making dozens of costumes for children.
  - according to the instructions According to the instructions, you can get started in one hour.

### [instructor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/instructor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈstrʌktə(r)/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈstrʌktər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/instr/instructor__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/instr/instructor__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a fitness/driving/ski instructor
  - a qualified/certified instructor
  - He worked for a time as an aerobics instructor.

### [instrument](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/instrument) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɪnstrəmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈɪnstrəmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ins/instr/instrument__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ins/instr/instrument__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to play an instrument
  - Is he learning an instrument?
  - percussion/brass/string instruments
  - an instrument maker

### [intelligent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/intelligent) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈtelɪdʒənt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈtelɪdʒənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/intel/intelligent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/intel/intelligent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a highly intelligent child
  - She is clearly extremely intelligent.
  - to have an intelligent conversation/discussion/debate
  - to ask an intelligent question

### [international](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/international_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntəˈnæʃnəl/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntərˈnæʃnəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/inter/international__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/inter/international__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - international trade/law
  - national and international affairs
  - the international community
  - an international organization/institution/agency
  - In crucial areas of international relations the nation state still dominates.
  - Japan's crime rate is still low by international standards.
  - an international conference on global trade
  - a drop in prices on the international markets
  - an international treaty/agreement
  - an international airport/school/company
  - He plays international rugby.
  - a pianist with an international reputation

### [introduction](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/introduction) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪntrəˈdʌkʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɪntrəˈdʌkʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/int/intro/introduction__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/int/intro/introduction__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a book with an excellent introduction and notes
  - introduction to something Can you write a brief introduction to the text?
  - introduction by somebody The English edition has an introduction by Herbert Read.
  - in an introduction His mother is mentioned in the introduction.
  - By way of introduction, let me give you the background to the story.

### [invent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/invent) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvent/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/inven/invent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/inven/invent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Who invented the steam engine?
  - I wish mobile phones had never been invented!
  - The term ‘sociology’ was invented by Auguste Comte.

### [invention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/invention) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvenʃn/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/inven/invention__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/inven/invention__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Fax machines were a wonderful invention at the time.
  - He failed to patent his invention and never made a penny from it.

### [invitation](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/invitation) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɪnvɪˈteɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɪnvɪˈteɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/invit/invitation__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/invit/invitation__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to issue/extend an invitation
  - to accept/turn down/decline an invitation
  - invitation to something I received an invitation to the party
  - invitation for something She repeated her invitation for dinner at her place.
  - invitation from somebody/something We got a wedding invitation from Alex and Chris.
  - invitation to do something I have an open invitation (= not restricted to a particular date) to visit my friend in Japan.

### [invite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/invite_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvaɪt/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/invit/invite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/invit/invite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - invite somebody to something Have you been invited to their party?
  - She very kindly invited me to lunch.
  - invite somebody I'd have liked to have gone but I wasn't invited.
  - My mother invited a few friends that she hadn't seen for 20 years.
  - invite somebody for something Let's invite them all for dinner.
  - invite somebody to do something They have invited me to go to Paris with them.

### [involve](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/involve) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ɪnˈvɒlv/` · **US:** `/ɪnˈvɑːlv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/inv/invol/involve__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/inv/invol/involve__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - involve something Any investment involves an element of risk.
  - involve doing something The process involves using steam to sterilize the instruments.
  - The test will involve answering questions about a photograph.
  - involve somebody/something doing something The job involves me travelling all over the country.
  - (formal) The job involves my travelling all over the country.

### [item](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/item) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈaɪtəm/` · **US:** `/ˈaɪtəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/ite/item_/item__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/ite/item_/item__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's the next item on the agenda?
  - There are plenty of vegetarian items on the menu.
  - She still has five items on her to-do list.
  - Students had to select three topics from the list of items.
  - There is a maximum score of two points per test item.

### [itself](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/itself) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ɪtˈself/` · **US:** `/ɪtˈself/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/i/its/itsel/itself__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/i/its/itsel/itself__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The cat was washing itself.
  - Does the computer turn itself off?
  - The company has got itself into difficulties.
  - There's no need for the team to feel proud of itself.


## J

### [jam](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/jam_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dʒæm/` · **US:** `/dʒæm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jam/jam__/jam__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jam/jam__/jam__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - strawberry jam
  - a jar/pot of jam
  - recipes for jams and preserves
  - (British English) a jam doughnut

### [jazz](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/jazz_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dʒæz/` · **US:** `/dʒæz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jaz/jazz_/jazz__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jaz/jazz_/jazz__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - traditional/modern jazz
  - a jazz band/club
  - jazz musicians
  - the rising stars of the New York jazz scene

### [jewellery](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/jewellery) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒuːəlri/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒuːəlri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jew/jewel/jewellery__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jew/jewel/jewellery__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - silver/gold jewellery
  - She has some lovely pieces of jewellery.
  - I don't wear jewellery of any kind.
  - She inherited the family jewellery.
  - The nurse asked her to remove all her jewellery.
  - (British English) a jewellery shop
  - (US English) a jewelry store

### [joke](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/joke_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dʒəʊk/` · **US:** `/dʒəʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jok/joke_/joke__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jok/joke_/joke__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I can't tell jokes.
  - She's always cracking jokes.
  - They often make jokes at each other's expense.
  - I didn't get the joke (= understand it).
  - I wish he wouldn't tell dirty jokes (= about sex).
  - a sick joke (= about death, disease, etc.)
  - as a joke I only did it as a joke (= it was not meant seriously).
  - joke about somebody/something Have you heard the joke about the elephant and the mouse?

### [joke](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/joke_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dʒəʊk/` · **US:** `/dʒəʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jok/joke_/joke__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jok/joke_/joke__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was laughing and joking with the children.
  - I'm the sort of person who likes to joke a lot.
  - joke about something They often joked about all the things that could go wrong.
  - + speech ‘I cooked it myself, so be careful!’ he joked.

### [journalist](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/journalist) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈdʒɜːnəlɪst/` · **US:** `/ˈdʒɜːrnəlɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jou/journ/journalist__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jou/journ/journalist__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a freelance journalist
  - She's an investigative journalist with a French newspaper.
  - The minister told journalists he was not going to resign.

### [jump](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/jump_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/dʒʌmp/` · **US:** `/dʒʌmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jum/jump_/jump__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jum/jump_/jump__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to do a parachute jump
  - jump of… a jump of over six metres
  - jump from something Somehow he survived the jump from the third floor of the building.

### [jump](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/jump_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/dʒʌmp/` · **US:** `/dʒʌmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/j/jum/jump_/jump__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/j/jum/jump_/jump__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Quick, jump!’ he shouted.
  - jump + adv./prep. She jumped into the water to save them.
  - He jumped into the air and started cheering.
  - She jumped down from the chair.
  - The children were jumping up and down with excitement.
  - The pilot jumped from the burning plane (= with a parachute).
  - He killed himself by jumping off a bridge.
  - jump + noun She has jumped 2.2 metres.


## K

### [kid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kid_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɪd/` · **US:** `/kɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kid/kid__/kid__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kid/kid__/kid__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you have any kids?
  - How are the kids (= your children)?
  - A bunch of kids were hanging around outside.
  - a gang/group of kids
  - college/school kids
  - street kids who rely on their ingenuity to keep alive
  - You're acting like a little kid!
  - as a kid I remember reading with my grandmother as a young kid of 6.
  - I'm looking forward to getting home to the wife and kids.
  - Here are some fun ways to teach your kids about healthy eating.
  - The kids love playing outside in the mud.
  - The game is aimed at kids of all ages.

### [kill](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/kill_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/kɪl/` · **US:** `/kɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kil/kill_/kill__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kil/kill_/kill__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - kill (somebody/something) Cancer kills thousands of people every year.
  - Tiredness while driving can kill.
  - Dozens of civilians were killed or injured in the attack.
  - She was nearly killed by a car bomb.
  - Both members of the crew were killed instantly when the missile hit their aircraft.
  - Three soldiers were killed in action (= while fighting) yesterday.
  - She tried to kill me!
  - The terrorists threatened to kill the hostages.
  - I bought a spray to kill the weeds.
  - (informal) My mother will kill me (= be very angry with me) when she finds out.
  - kill yourself He tried to kill himself with sleeping pills.
  - kill yourself doing something (figurative) Don't kill yourself trying to get the work done by tomorrow. It can wait.

### [king](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/king) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kɪŋ/` · **US:** `/kɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kin/king_/king__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kin/king_/king__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the kings and queens of England
  - the French king
  - to be crowned king
  - King George V

### [knee](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/knee_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/niː/` · **US:** `/niː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kne/knee_/knee__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kne/knee_/knee__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a knee injury
  - I had knee surgery three years ago.
  - Bend your knees until you feel your thigh muscles working.
  - I grazed my knee when I fell.
  - He went down on one knee and asked her to marry him.
  - on your knees She was on her knees scrubbing the kitchen floor.
  - Everyone was down on (their) hands and knees (= crawling on the floor) looking for the ring.
  - He fell to his knees and begged for mercy.
  - My knees were knocking (= I was frightened).

### [knife](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/knife_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/naɪf/` · **US:** `/naɪf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kni/knife/knife__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kni/knife/knife__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - knives and forks
  - a sharp knife
  - a bread knife (= one for cutting bread)
  - The aim is to deter young people from carrying knives.
  - He had been stabbed repeatedly with a kitchen knife.
  - a plan to tackle knife crime
  - She was murdered in a frenzied knife attack.

### [knock](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/knock_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/nɒk/` · **US:** `/nɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kno/knock/knock__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kno/knock/knock__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He knocked three times and waited.
  - knock at/on something I knocked on the door and went straight in.
  - Somebody was knocking on the window.

### [knowledge](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/knowledge) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɒlɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈnɑːlɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/k/kno/knowl/knowledge__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/k/kno/knowl/knowledge__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - scientific/technical knowledge and skills
  - intimate/first-hand/basic knowledge
  - a thirst for knowledge
  - It will be an opportunity to gain knowledge and experience.
  - knowledge of something She has acquired a detailed knowledge of the subject.
  - They enjoy sharing their knowledge of the business.
  - He had no prior knowledge of the language before visiting the country.
  - He has a wide knowledge of painting and music.
  - knowledge about something There is a lack of knowledge about the tax system.


## L

### [lab](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lab_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/læb/` · **US:** `/læb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lab/lab__/lab__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lab/lab__/lab__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a computer/research lab
  - the school science labs
  - in a lab She'd been working in the lab all day.
  - a lab technician
  - a lab coat (= a white coat worn by scientists, etc. working in a laboratory)

### [lady](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lady) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪdi/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lad/lady_/lady__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lad/lady_/lady__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There's a lady waiting to see you.
  - an old/elderly lady
  - He was with an attractive young lady.
  - And who is this lovely lady?
  - the lady with the red hair
  - the lady in the designer dress
  - the ladies’ golf championship
