  - (British English, old-fashioned) a tea lady (= a woman who serves tea in an office)
  - (North American English, approving) She's a tough lady.
  - a lady doctor/golfer

### [lake](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lake) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/leɪk/` · **US:** `/leɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lak/lake_/lake__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lak/lake_/lake__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a lake We swam in the lake.
  - on a lake There were a number of boats on the lake.
  - We stayed in a hotel on the edge of Lake Ontario.
  - (figurative) a wine lake (= a large supply of wine that is not being used)

### [lamp](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lamp_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/læmp/` · **US:** `/læmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lam/lamp_/lamp__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lam/lamp_/lamp__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a desk/bedside lamp
  - a street lamp
  - an oil lamp
  - (British English) a paraffin lamp
  - (especially North American English) a kerosene lamp
  - a fluorescent/halogen lamp
  - to switch on/turn off a lamp

### [land](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/land_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lænd/` · **US:** `/lænd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lan/land_/land__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lan/land_/land__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The plane landed safely.
  - Both aircraft landed on the runway.
  - A fly landed on his nose.

### [laptop](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/laptop) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlæptɒp/` · **US:** `/ˈlæptɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lap/lapto/laptop__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lap/lapto/laptop__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I opened my laptop and started typing.
  - on a laptop She was still working on her laptop.

### [last](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/last1_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/lɑːst/` · **US:** `/læst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/las/last_/last__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/las/last_/last__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He came last in the race.
  - They arrived last of all.

### [last](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/last1_3) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɑːst/` · **US:** `/læst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/las/last_/last__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/las/last_/last__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Sorry I'm late—am I the last?
  - They were the last to arrive.

### [last](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/last1_4) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɑːst/` · **US:** `/læst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/las/last_/last__gb_6.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/las/last_/last__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Each game lasts about an hour.
  - How long does the play last?
  - Nothing lasts forever.
  - last for something The meeting only lasted for a few minutes.
  - last until something The trial is expected to last until the end of the week.

### [later](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/later_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪtə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪtər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lat/later/later__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lat/later/later__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Rembrandt's later works
  - This is discussed in more detail in a later chapter.
  - The judge will give her ruling at a later date.
  - The match has been postponed to a later date.

### [laughter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/laughter) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɑːftə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈlæftər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lau/laugh/laughter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lau/laugh/laughter__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to roar/howl with laughter
  - tears/gales/peals of laughter
  - Everyone burst into fits of laughter.
  - a house full of laughter (= with a happy atmosphere)
  - He gave a snort of derisive laughter.
  - laughter from somebody When he said this, there was hysterical laughter from the audience.

### [law](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/law) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɔː/` · **US:** `/lɔː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/law/law__/law__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/law/law__/law__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to pass a law (= officially make it part of the system of laws)
  - law against (doing) something Police don't have the resources to enforce the law against dumping waste.
  - (informal) There ought to be a law against it!
  - law on something The company's actions violated the law on equal employment.
  - The suspects were in clear violation of immigration laws.

### [lawyer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lawyer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɔɪə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈlɔɪər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/law/lawye/lawyer__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/law/lawye/lawyer__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to hire a lawyer
  - You would be wise to consult a lawyer.
  - a defence lawyer
  - (North American English) a trial lawyer
  - a human rights lawyer
  - The lawyer representing the family said he was very satisfied with the verdict.
  - lawyer for somebody Lawyers for all three defendants said their clients did not intend to kill the victim.

### [lazy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lazy) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈleɪzi/` · **US:** `/ˈleɪzi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/laz/lazy_/lazy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/laz/lazy_/lazy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was not stupid, just lazy.
  - too lazy to do something I was feeling too lazy to go out.
  - He's just plain lazy.

### [lead](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lead1_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/liːd/` · **US:** `/liːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/lead_/lead__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/lead_/lead__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If you lead, I'll follow.
  - lead somebody/something + adv./prep. He led us out into the grounds.
  - The receptionist led the way to the boardroom.
  - She led the horse back into the stable.
  - The robbers led police on a high-speed chase through the city.
  - (figurative) I tried to lead the discussion back to the main issue.

### [leader](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/leader) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈliːdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈliːdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/leade/leader__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/leade/leader__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - party/world leaders
  - a religious/political leader
  - He was elected as leader of the Democratic Party.
  - She became leader of the campaign group.
  - Union leaders agreed to begin talks with the government.
  - Tomorrow the President will meet with business leaders.
  - He was not a natural leader.
  - She's a born leader.

### [learning](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/learning) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɜːnɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈlɜːrnɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/learn/learning__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/learn/learning__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - adult learning
  - new methods to facilitate language learning
  - Field trips provide an opportunity for experiential learning.
  - teaching and learning
  - The school aims to provide a supportive learning environment.
  - Last season was a learning experience for the team.
  - The learning process is slightly different in each individual.
  - A large part of children's learning takes place in the home.

### [least](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/least_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/liːst/` · **US:** `/liːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/least/least__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/least/least__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He always turns up just when you least expect him.
  - She chose the least expensive of the hotels.
  - I never hid the truth, least of all from you.
  - We had to settle for the least worst option (= the best of several options that were all bad).

### [least](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/least_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/liːst/` · **US:** `/liːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/least/least__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/least/least__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's the best teacher, even though he has the least experience.
  - She never had the least idea what to do about it.
  - He gave (the) least of all towards the wedding present.
  - the least of something How others see me is the least of my worries (= I have more important things to worry about).
  - It's the least I can do to help (= I feel I should do more).

### [least](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/least_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/liːst/` · **US:** `/liːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lea/least/least__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lea/least/least__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He's the best teacher, even though he has the least experience.
  - She never had the least idea what to do about it.
  - He gave (the) least of all towards the wedding present.
  - the least of something How others see me is the least of my worries (= I have more important things to worry about).
  - It's the least I can do to help (= I feel I should do more).

### [lecture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lecture_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlektʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈlektʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lec/lectu/lecture__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lec/lectu/lecture__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to go to/attend a lecture
  - to give/deliver a lecture
  - I have a lecture at nine tomorrow.
  - lecture on something a series of lectures on Jane Austen
  - a course of lectures on art history
  - lecture about something an illustrated lecture about the planet Mars
  - lecture to somebody a book based on her lectures to students
  - a lecture to the Darwin Society
  - lecture by somebody a lecture by Professor Snow
  - in a lecture She spoke about the environmental problems of the future in a lecture at Georgetown University.
  - at a lecture There were very few students at his lecture that morning.

### [lecture](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lecture_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlektʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈlektʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lec/lectu/lecture__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lec/lectu/lecture__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He has taught and lectured at the University of Pretoria.
  - lecture on something He lectures on Islamic history at Birkbeck College.
  - lecture in something She lectures in Russian literature.
  - lecture to somebody a live broadcast of the professor lecturing to students

### [lemon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lemon_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlemən/` · **US:** `/ˈlemən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lem/lemon/lemon__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lem/lemon/lemon__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Add a squeeze of lemon juice.
  - Squeeze the juice of half a lemon over the fish.
  - a gin and tonic with ice and lemon
  - lemon tea
  - a lemon tree
  - Garnish the fish with wedges of lemon.

### [lend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lend/` · **US:** `/lend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/len/lend_/lend__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/len/lend_/lend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - lend something They will lend equipment free of charge.
  - lend something to somebody I've lent the car to a friend.
  - lend out something The gallery has been willing to lend out works of art in the past.
  - lend out something to somebody The charity lends out laptops to support learning at home.
  - lend somebody something Can you lend me £10?
  - Can you lend me your car this evening?
  - Has he returned that book you lent him?
  - She very kindly lent me her bicycle.

### [less](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/less_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/les/` · **US:** `/les/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/les/less_/less__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/les/less_/less__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - less expensive/likely/intelligent
  - less often/enthusiastically
  - less than… I read much less now than I used to.
  - The receptionist was less than (= not at all) helpful.
  - She wasn't any the less happy for (= she was perfectly happy) being on her own.
  - That this is a positive stereotype makes it no less a stereotype, and therefore unacceptable.

### [less](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/less_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/les/` · **US:** `/les/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/les/less_/less__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/les/less_/less__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - less something less butter/time/importance
  - He was advised to smoke fewer cigarettes and drink less beer.
  - less to do We have less to worry about now.
  - less of something They say we should exercise and eat less of some kinds of fat.
  - less than… We'll be there in less than no time (= very soon).
  - The victory was nothing less than a miracle.
  - less of something than… It is less of a problem than I'd expected.

### [less](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/less_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/les/` · **US:** `/les/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/les/less_/less__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/les/less_/less__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - less something less butter/time/importance
  - He was advised to smoke fewer cigarettes and drink less beer.
  - less to do We have less to worry about now.
  - less of something They say we should exercise and eat less of some kinds of fat.
  - less than… We'll be there in less than no time (= very soon).
  - The victory was nothing less than a miracle.
  - less of something than… It is less of a problem than I'd expected.

### [level](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/level_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlevl/` · **US:** `/ˈlevl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lev/level/level__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lev/level/level__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The cables are buried one metre below ground level.
  - The floodwater nearly reached roof level.
  - The river has fallen to its lowest level since 2012.
  - the level rose/dropped/decreased/increased
  - on a level with something On the second floor you are on a level with (= at the same height as) the treetops.
  - on a level The tables are not on a level (= the same height).

### [lifestyle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lifestyle) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlaɪfstaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈlaɪfstaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lif/lifes/lifestyle__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lif/lifes/lifestyle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Many people are trying to adopt a healthy lifestyle these days.
  - a comfortable/lavish lifestyle
  - Too many children are living a completely sedentary lifestyle.
  - It was a big change in lifestyle when we moved to the country.
  - lifestyle choices/changes
  - the lifestyle section of the newspaper (= the part that deals with clothes, furniture, hobbies, etc.)

### [lift](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lift_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɪft/` · **US:** `/lɪft/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lif/lift_/lift__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lif/lift_/lift__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's on the sixth floor—let's take the lift.
  - We got the lift to the tenth floor.
  - in a lift I went down in the lift and made my way to reception.

### [lift](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lift_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɪft/` · **US:** `/lɪft/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lif/lift_/lift__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lif/lift_/lift__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - lift somebody/something I lifted the lid of the box and peered in.
  - She lifted her head to look at him.
  - lift somebody/something up He lifted the toddler up and cuddled her.
  - lift somebody/something (up) + adv./prep. He stood there with his arms lifted above his head.
  - (figurative) John lifted his eyes (= looked up) from his book.
  - Her eyebrows lifted. ‘Apologize? Why?’

### [light](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/light_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/laɪt/` · **US:** `/laɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lig/light/light__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lig/light/light__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She lit a candle.
  - Come in, I'll light a fire and you can get warmed up.
  - to light a cigarette/cigar/pipe
  - The gas lamps were lit.
  - I put a lighted match to the letter and watched it burn.
  - The fire is thought to have started when someone dropped a lit cigarette.

### [likely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/likely_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlaɪkli/` · **US:** `/ˈlaɪkli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lik/likel/likely__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lik/likel/likely__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the most likely explanation/outcome/scenario
  - the likely cause/consequence
  - a likely nominee/contender/winner
  - A threat level of ‘Severe’ means a terrorist attack is highly likely.
  - They might ask for help or, more likely, just give up.
  - likely to do something Tickets are likely to be expensive.
  - The beetles are likely to cause damage.
  - There is likely to be heavy snowfall.
  - likely (that)… It's hardly likely (that) they'll refuse.
  - It is quite likely the discussion may never take place.

### [link](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/link_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɪŋk/` · **US:** `/lɪŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lin/link_/link__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lin/link_/link__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - link between A and B Police suspect there may be a link between the two murders.
  - There is evidence for a strong causal link between exposure to sun and skin cancer.
  - to establish/find a link
  - link with somebody/something These photographs are a direct link with the past.
  - link to somebody/something Many of them had no previous links to terrorism.

### [link](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/link_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɪŋk/` · **US:** `/lɪŋk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lin/link_/link__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lin/link_/link__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - link A to B The video cameras are linked to a powerful computer.
  - link A with B The Channel Tunnel links Britain with the rest of Europe.
  - link A and B A pedestrian bridge links the two buildings.
  - link A and B together When computers are networked, they are linked together so that information can be transferred between them.
  - be linked into something The computers are linked into a network.

### [listener](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/listener) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪsənə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪsənər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lis/liste/listener__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lis/liste/listener__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a good listener (= somebody who you can rely on to listen with attention or sympathy)
  - a sympathetic/willing listener
  - an attentive listener

### [little](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/little_3) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · **US:** `/ˈlɪtl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lit/littl/little__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lit/littl/little__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She seemed a little afraid of going inside.
  - These shoes are a little (bit) too big for me.
  - These days I'm a little more relaxed.
  - (informal) Everything has become just that little bit harder.
  - (formal) She felt tired and more than a little worried.

### [lock](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lock_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lɒk/` · **US:** `/lɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/lock_/lock__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/lock_/lock__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She turned the key in the lock.
  - It's a good idea to have locks fitted on all your windows.

### [lock](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lock_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/lɒk/` · **US:** `/lɑːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loc/lock_/lock__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loc/lock_/lock__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Did you lock the door?
  - David locked the car and slipped the key into his pocket.
  - The gates are locked at 6 o'clock.
  - a locked cabinet
  - This suitcase doesn't lock.

### [look](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/look_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lʊk/` · **US:** `/lʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/loo/look_/look__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/loo/look_/look__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - look at somebody/something Here, have a look at this.
  - Take a look at these figures!
  - Make sure you get a good look at their faces.
  - One look at his face and Jenny stopped laughing.
  - Alex and Michael exchanged looks (= they looked at each other).
  - look around It's an interesting place. Do you want to take a look around?

### [lorry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lorry) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlɒri/` · **US:** `/ˈlɔːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lor/lorry/lorry__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lor/lorry/lorry__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a lorry driver
  - a lorry load of frozen fish
  - by lorry Emergency food supplies were brought in by lorry.

### [lost](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lost_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/lɒst/` · **US:** `/lɔːst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/los/lost_/lost__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/los/lost_/lost__us_6.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We always get lost in London.
  - We're completely lost.
  - We got lost in the woods.
  - I was tired, hungry and hopelessly lost.

### [loud](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/loud_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/laʊd/` · **US:** `/laʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lou/loud_/loud__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lou/loud_/loud__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - loud laughter
  - a deafeningly loud bang
  - She spoke in a very loud voice.
  - That music's too loud—please turn it down.
  - I heard a loud noise that sounded like a cannon.
  - I was woken by a loud bang at 5.30 am.
  - Suddenly the footsteps grew louder.
  - I play loud music and dance around my house.
  - Eventually, the shouting got louder.
  - The noise was faint, but just loud enough to follow.

### [loud](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/loud_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/laʊd/` · **US:** `/laʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lou/loud_/loud__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lou/loud_/loud__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you have to play that music so loud?
  - You'll have to speak louder—I can't hear you.
  - She screamed loud enough to wake the dead.
  - If you shout loud enough, you will get what you want.

### [loudly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/loudly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlaʊdli/` · **US:** `/ˈlaʊdli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lou/loudl/loudly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lou/loudl/loudly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She screamed as loudly as she could.
  - About two hours into the flight, he began complaining loudly that his seat was too small.
  - She was loudly proclaiming her innocence.

### [lovely](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lovely_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlʌvli/` · **US:** `/ˈlʌvli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/lov/lovel/lovely__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/lov/lovel/lovely__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She looked particularly lovely that night.
  - He has a lovely voice.
  - You've got lovely eyes.
  - We travelled through some lovely countryside.
  - She's got an absolutely lovely face.

### [low](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/low_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ləʊ/` · **US:** `/ləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/low/low__/low__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/low/low__/low__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a low wall/building/table
  - The fence is too low.
  - a low range of hills
  - low clouds
  - flying at low altitude
  - The sun was low in the sky.

### [low](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/low_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ləʊ/` · **US:** `/ləʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/low/low__/low__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/low/low__/low__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to crouch/bend low
  - He bowed low and said, ‘I am your humble servant.’
  - a plane flying low over the town
  - low-flying aircraft
  - The sun sank lower towards the horizon.

### [luck](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/luck_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/lʌk/` · **US:** `/lʌk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/luc/luck_/luck__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/luc/luck_/luck__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - with (any) luck With any luck, we'll be home before dark.
  - (British English) With a bit of luck, we'll finish on time.
  - luck with doing something So far I have had no luck with finding a job.
  - luck with something I'm not having much luck with buses.
  - luck with somebody I never had much luck (= had a successful relationship) with women.
  - I could hardly believe my luck when he said yes.
  - It was a stroke of luck that we found you.
  - by luck By sheer luck nobody was hurt in the explosion.
  - The only goal of the match came more by luck than judgement.
  - We wish her luck in her new career.
  - She wears a red bracelet to bring her luck.
  - in luck You're in luck (= lucky)—there's one ticket left.
  - out of luck You're out of luck. She's not here.
  - What a piece of luck!
  - Finally my luck ran out and they caught me trying to climb the outer wall.

### [lucky](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/lucky) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈlʌki/` · **US:** `/ˈlʌki/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/l/luc/lucky/lucky__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/l/luc/lucky/lucky__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - lucky to do something His friend was killed and he knows he is lucky to be alive.
  - She was lucky enough to be chosen for the team.
  - lucky (that) You were lucky (that) you spotted the danger in time.
  - In terms of my career I feel incredibly lucky.
  - You can think yourself lucky you didn't get mugged.
  - She counted herself lucky that she still had a job.
  - Mark is one of the lucky ones—he at least has somewhere to sleep.
  - the lucky winners
  - He was among the lucky ones whose works were approved.


## M

### [mail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mail_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/meɪl/` · **US:** `/meɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mai/mail_/mail__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mai/mail_/mail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a mail service/train/van
  - the Royal Mail
  - (especially North American English) regular/postal mail (= rather than email)
  - in the mail Hard copies of the documents are in the mail to you.
  - by mail We do our business by mail.
  - through the mail Never send cash through the mail.
  - (especially North American English) via mail We did not get any message via mail or email.

### [mail](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mail_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/meɪl/` · **US:** `/meɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mai/mail_/mail__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mai/mail_/mail__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - mail something (to somebody/something) Don't forget to mail that letter to your mother.
  - mail somebody something Don't forget to mail your mother that letter.
  - mail somebody/something The company intends to mail 50 000 households in the area.
  - The brochures are mailed direct to members.
  - He headed to the post office to mail the package.
  - They keep in touch by having copies mailed to them from Barbados.
  - Postcards were mailed to residents explaining about trash.
  - Qualified applicants should email, fax or mail a résumé and salary requirements.

### [major](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/major_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeɪdʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmeɪdʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/maj/major/major__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/maj/major/major__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a major road
  - Some major international companies refused to do business with them.
  - He played a major role in setting up the system.
  - major sporting events
  - a major issue/factor/project/challenge
  - We have encountered major problems.
  - Four major cities will remove diesel cars by 2025.
  - There were calls for major changes to the welfare system.

### [male](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/male_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/meɪl/` · **US:** `/meɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mal/male_/male__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mal/male_/male__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a male friend/colleague/partner
  - a male nurse/model/dancer
  - All the participants were male, aged between 25 and 30.
  - The workforce is predominantly male.
  - There are three lead male characters in the film.
  - Female students asked more questions than their male counterparts.

### [male](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/male_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/meɪl/` · **US:** `/meɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mal/male_/male__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mal/male_/male__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The male of the species has a white tail.
  - a male-dominated profession
  - an adult/adolescent male
  - (formal) The body is that of a white male aged about 40.
  - Haemophilia is a condition that affects mostly males.

### [manage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/manage) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmænɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈmænɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/man/manag/manage__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/man/manag/manage__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to manage a business/factory/bank/hotel/soccer team
  - to manage a department/project
  - I think that Forestry Tasmania manages the forest.
  - The units in some areas are poorly maintained and badly managed.
  - We need people who are good at managing.

### [manager](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/manager) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmænɪdʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmænɪdʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/man/manag/manager__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/man/manag/manager__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a bank/hotel manager
  - the sales/marketing/human resources manager
  - a fund/portfolio/asset manager
  - The company always needs project managers.
  - I'm a senior manager in a multinational company.
  - a meeting of area managers
  - If you have any questions, speak to your manager.

### [manner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/manner) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmænə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmænər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/man/manne/manner__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/man/manne/manner__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to have good/bad manners
  - It is bad manners to talk with your mouth full.
  - He has no manners (= behaves very badly).
  - She could at least have the good manners to let me know she won’t be able to attend.
  - These children need to learn some manners.
  - Now sit down and eat and mind your manners!

### [mark](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mark_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mɑːk/` · **US:** `/mɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/mark_/mark__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/mark_/mark__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - punctuation marks
  - Any piece of silver bearing his mark is extremely valuable.
  - I put a mark in the margin to remind me to check the figure.
  - A faint pencil mark showed where the house was on the map.
  - Every image is made up of hundreds of brush marks.

### [mark](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mark_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/mɑːk/` · **US:** `/mɑːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/mark_/mark__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/mark_/mark__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - mark something The flood level is marked by a white line on the wall.
  - mark A with B Items marked with an asterisk can be omitted.
  - mark B on A Prices are marked on the goods.
  - mark somebody/something + adj. The teacher marked her absent (= made a mark by her name to show that she was absent).
  - Why have you marked this wrong?
  - Do not open any mail marked ‘Confidential’.
  - mark something as something Certain words were marked as important.

### [marry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/marry) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmæri/` · **US:** `/ˈmæri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mar/marry/marry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mar/marry/marry__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - marry (somebody) She married a German.
  - He was 36 when he married Viv.
  - I don't want to marry Robert.
  - We got married in a small village church.
  - Dali and Gala were married in a civil ceremony in Paris.
  - He never married.
  - I guess I'm not the marrying kind (= the kind of person who wants to get married).
  - + adj. They married young.

### [material](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/material_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/məˈtɪəriəl/` · **US:** `/məˈtɪriəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mat/mater/material__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mat/mater/material__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - building materials (= bricks, sand, glass, etc.)
  - Every item we sell is made out of recycled material.
  - All the furniture is crafted from natural materials.
  - synthetic/man-made materials
  - Bacteria help break down dead plant material such as fallen leaves.
  - We have 98% of the same genetic material as chimpanzees.

### [mathematics](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mathematics) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌmæθəˈmætɪks/` · **US:** `/ˌmæθəˈmætɪks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mat/mathe/mathematics__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mat/mathe/mathematics__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the school mathematics curriculum
  - applied/pure mathematics

### [maths](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/maths) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mæθs/` · **US:** `/mæθs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mat/maths/maths__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mat/maths/maths__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The core subjects are English, maths and science.
  - a maths teacher

### [matter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/matter_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmætə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmætər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mat/matte/matter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mat/matte/matter__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's the matter? Is there something wrong?
  - Is anything the matter?
  - matter with somebody/something Is something the matter with Bob? He seems very down.
  - There's something the matter with my eyes.
  - ‘We've bought a new TV.’ ‘What was the matter with the old one?’
  - What's the matter with you today (= why are you behaving like this)?
  - What on earth was the matter with her, she wondered. She’d come here to enjoy herself and here she was, stuck on her own in the hotel.

### [matter](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/matter_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmætə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmætər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mat/matte/matter__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mat/matte/matter__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘I'm afraid I forgot that book again.’ ‘It doesn't matter (= it is not important enough to worry about).’
  - ‘What did you say?’ ‘Oh, it doesn't matter’ (= it is not important enough to repeat).
  - After his death, nothing seemed to matter any more.
  - She was trying to get noticed by the people who matter.
  - matter to somebody The children matter more to her than anything else in the world.
  - Councillors are tackling the things that matter most to local people.
  - matter who, what, etc… Does it really matter who did it?
  - It doesn't matter how fast you walk (= it will not make any difference), you're going to get very wet.
  - matter to somebody who, what, etc… It doesn't matter to me what you do.
  - matter (to somebody) that… It didn't matter that the weather was bad.
  - What does it matter if I spent $100 on it—it's my money!
  - As long as you're happy, that's all that matters.
  - He's been in prison, you know—not that it matters (= that information does not affect my opinion of him).

### [may](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/may_1) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/meɪ/` · **US:** `/meɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/may/may_1/may_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/may/may__/may__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That may or may not be true.
  - He may have (= perhaps he has) missed his train.
  - They may well win.
  - There is a range of programs on the market which may be described as design aids.

### [media](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/media) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmiːdiə/` · **US:** `/ˈmiːdiə/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/med/media/media__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/med/media/media__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the national/international media
  - The news media reported extensively on the story.
  - The media was/were accused of influencing the final decision.
  - print/electronic/broadcast media
  - This is a story that the mainstream media refuses to cover.
  - in the media The trial was fully reported in the media.
  - Any event attended by the actor received widespread media coverage.
  - Rumours about her illness began in some media outlets.
  - The star has been the focus of intense media attention.

### [medical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/medical_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmedɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈmedɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/med/medic/medical__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/med/medic/medical__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - medical care/treatment
  - the medical profession
  - medical research/advances
  - her medical condition/history
  - His medical records showed that he was unfit for work.
  - a medical student/school
  - Medical staff are advising patients to avoid using the cream.
  - a medical certificate (= a statement by a doctor that gives details of your state of health)
  - Seek medical attention as quickly as possible.

### [medicine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/medicine) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmedɪsn/` · **US:** `/ˈmedɪsn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/med/medic/medicine__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/med/medic/medicine__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - advances in modern medicine
  - to study/practise medicine
  - a professor of medicine
  - conventional/orthodox medicine
  - alternative/complementary medicine
  - Acupuncture has long been a part of traditional Chinese medicine.
  - a rapidly developing field of medicine
  - There is a greater emphasis on preventive medicine.

### [memory](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/memory) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeməri/` · **US:** `/ˈmeməri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mem/memor/memory__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mem/memor/memory__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - memory for something I have a bad memory for names.
  - People have short memories (= they soon forget).
  - He had a long memory for people who had disappointed him.
  - He had a great memory for detail.
  - from memory She can recite the whole poem from memory.
  - He suffered memory loss for weeks after the accident.
  - Are you sure? Memory can play tricks on you.
  - The drugs had a severe effect on her short-term memory.
  - I quickly committed the number of the car to memory (= learned and remembered it).
  - Culture is the unit of collective memory, keeping the past alive.

### [mention](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mention_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmenʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈmenʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/men/menti/mention__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/men/menti/mention__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - mention something/somebody Sorry, I won't mention it again.
  - The newspaper failed to mention a word of Johnson's speech.
  - The doctor mentioned the possibility of going to Nairobi for special treatment.
  - The disease is rarely mentioned by Western media.
  - Now that you mention it, she did seem to be in a strange mood.
  - mention something/somebody to somebody Nobody mentioned anything to me about it.
  - mention where, why, etc… Did she mention where she was going?
  - mention that… You mentioned in your letter that you might be moving abroad.
  - He failed to mention that he was the one who started the fight.
  - mention doing something Did I mention going to see Vicky on Sunday?
  - mention something/somebody in something The cruise visits most places mentioned in the article.

### [metal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/metal) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmetl/` · **US:** `/ˈmetl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/met/metal/metal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/met/metal/metal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a piece/sheet of metal
  - a metal pipe/bar/box/plate
  - a heap of scrap metal
  - a roof of sheet metal
  - The frame is made of metal.

### [method](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/method) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmeθəd/` · **US:** `/ˈmeθəd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/met/metho/method__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/met/metho/method__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Which method is the most effective?
  - traditional/alternative methods
  - method of something a scientific method of data analysis
  - method of doing something a reliable method of measuring blood pressure
  - method for (doing) something the best method for arriving at an accurate prediction of the costs
  - method to do something Developing new methods to keep insects out of food packages is critical.
  - to use/employ/apply a method
  - to devise/propose/adopt a method
  - He's quite critical of modern teaching methods.

### [middle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/middle_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪdl/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪdl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mid/middl/middle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mid/middl/middle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Pens are kept in the middle drawer.
  - She's the middle child of three.
  - He was very successful in his middle forties.
  - a middle-sized room
  - the middle-income groups in society
  - The film's middle section was rather dull.

### [middle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/middle_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪdl/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪdl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mid/middl/middle__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mid/middl/middle__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in the middle a lake with an island in the middle
  - This chicken isn't cooked in the middle.
  - in the middle of something Her car was stuck in the middle of the road.
  - The phone rang in the middle of the night.
  - You can’t leave in the middle of the meeting!
  - His picture was right/bang (= exactly) in the middle of the front page.
  - I have a pain in the middle of my back.
  - Low temperatures in the middle of winter can kill some trees.
  - down the middle Take a sheet of paper and draw a line down the middle.
  - by the middle of something I should have finished by the middle of the week.
  - I like a story with a beginning, a middle and an end.

### [might](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/might_1) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/maɪt/` · **US:** `/maɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mig/might/might__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mig/might/might__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He might get there in time, but I can't be sure.
  - It might not be too late to save the building.
  - I know Vicky doesn't like the job, but I mightn't find it too bad.
  - The pills might have helped him, if only he'd taken them regularly.
  - He might say that now (= it is true that he does), but he can soon change his mind.
  - He might, if he chose, retire and live in luxury for the rest of his days.

### [mind](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mind_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/maɪnd/` · **US:** `/maɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/min/mind_/mind__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/min/mind_/mind__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the conscious/subconscious mind
  - There were all kinds of thoughts running through my mind.
  - There was no doubt in his mind that he'd get the job.
  - ‘Drugs’ are associated in most people's minds with drug abuse.
  - She was in a disturbed state of mind.
  - I could not have complete peace of mind before they returned.
  - The campaign to win the hearts and minds of the public continues.
  - I felt refreshed in mind and body.

### [mind](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mind_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/maɪnd/` · **US:** `/maɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/min/mind_/mind__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/min/mind_/mind__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - mind (something) I don't mind the cold—it's the rain I don't like.
  - I hope you don't mind the noise.
  - He wouldn't have minded so much if she'd told him the truth.
  - mind about something Did she mind about not getting the job?
  - mind doing something Did she mind not getting the job?
  - mind somebody/something doing something Do your parents mind you leaving home?
  - (formal) Do your parents mind your leaving home?
  - mind how, what, etc… She never minded how hot it was.
  - mind that… He minded that he hadn't been asked.

### [mine](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mine_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/maɪn/` · **US:** `/maɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/min/mine_/mine__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/min/mine_/mine__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - That's mine.
  - of mine He's a friend of mine (= one of my friends).
  - She wanted one like mine (= like I have).

### [mirror](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mirror_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪrə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪrər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mir/mirro/mirror__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mir/mirro/mirror__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in the mirror He looked at himself in the mirror.
  - Remember to look in the mirror (= in a car, when driving) before signalling.
  - the bathroom mirror

### [missing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/missing) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmɪsɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈmɪsɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mis/missi/missing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mis/missi/missing__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I never found the missing piece.
  - Cases with missing data are excluded from the report.
  - My gloves have been missing for ages.
  - Two files have gone missing.
  - (especially British English) Our cat's gone missing again.
  - The book has two pages missing.
  - The book has two missing pages.
  - missing from something He didn't notice there was anything missing from his room until later on.

### [mobile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mobile_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈməʊbaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈməʊbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mob/mobil/mobile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mob/mobil/mobile__us_7.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What's your mobile number?
  - Mobile users spent 35 per cent more time on their devices this year.
  - mobile apps aimed at children
  - mobile networks/operators (= companies that provide mobile phone services)
  - mobile platforms/operating systems
  - mobile technology/broadband
  - mobile gaming/banking
  - The mobile market has continued to grow.
  - Many companies have tried to push mobile payment services.

### [mobile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mobile_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈməʊbaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈməʊbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mob/mobil/mobile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mob/mobil/mobile__us_7.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Call me on my mobile.

### [monkey](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/monkey_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmʌŋki/` · **US:** `/ˈmʌŋki/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mon/monke/monkey__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mon/monke/monkey__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Like humans, apes and monkeys live in complex social groupings.
  - The disease affects monkeys and humans in similar ways.
  - A troop of monkeys crashed their way through the trees.
  - Scientists visiting the island encountered a troop of wild monkeys.

### [moon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/moon_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/muːn/` · **US:** `/muːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/moo/moon_/moon_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/moo/moon_/moon__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the surface of the moon
  - the first man to walk on the moon
  - a moon landing

### [mostly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/mostly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈməʊstli/` · **US:** `/ˈməʊstli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mos/mostl/mostly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mos/mostl/mostly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The sauce is mostly cream.
  - We're mostly out on Sundays.
  - Revenue grew by 18 per cent, mostly due to the opening of 33 new stores.
  - The paintings consist mostly of still lifes.

### [motorcycle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/motorcycle) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈməʊtəsaɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈməʊtərsaɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mot/motor/motorcycle__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mot/motor/motorcycle__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He never learned to ride a motorcycle or to drive a car.
  - on a motorcycle She rode round Italy on a motorcycle.
  - motorcycle racing
  - a motorcycle accident
  - The car was accompanied by a police motorcycle escort.

### [movement](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/movement) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmuːvmənt/` · **US:** `/ˈmuːvmənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mov/movem/movement__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mov/movem/movement__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - hand/eye movements
  - She observed the gentle movement of his chest as he breathed.
  - Loose clothing gives you greater freedom of movement.
  - Power comes from the forward movement of the entire body.
  - Troy watched her every movement.
  - There was a sudden movement in the undergrowth.
  - The dance combined colour, movement and music.

### [musical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/musical_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈmjuːzɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈmjuːzɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/music/musical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/music/musical__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - musical styles/tastes
  - to have exceptional musical talent
  - He was one of the greatest musical talents of all time.
  - a first-rate musical performance
  - a musical production/entertainment
  - a diverse range of musical genres
  - She had a brilliant career in musical theatre.
  - A jazz band provided the musical accompaniment to the meal.

### [musician](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/musician) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/mjuˈzɪʃn/` · **US:** `/mjuˈzɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mus/music/musician__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mus/music/musician__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a jazz/rock/classical musician
  - She has been a professional musician for over 25 years.
  - The competition recognizes talented young musicians.
  - A group of local musicians entertained the guests.
  - The pipes were played by Irish musician, John Burney.

### [myself](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/myself) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/maɪˈself/` · **US:** `/maɪˈself/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/m/mys/mysel/myself__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/m/mys/mysel/myself__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I cut myself on a knife.
  - I wrote a message to myself.
  - I found myself unable to speak.
  - I haven't been feeling myself recently (= I have not felt well).
  - I needed space to be myself (= not influenced by other people).


## N

### [narrow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/narrow_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnærəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈnærəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nar/narro/narrow__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nar/narro/narrow__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Stray dogs wander the steep narrow lanes of the old town.
  - The road soon became narrower and steeper.
  - a narrow bed/doorway/shelf
  - Tall narrow windows admit only slivers of light.
  - narrow shoulders/hips
  - There was only a narrow gap between the bed and the wall.
  - Farming is confined to a narrow strip of land between the mountains and the sea.
  - (figurative) the narrow confines of prison life

### [national](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/national_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnæʃnəl/` · **US:** `/ˈnæʃnəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/natio/national__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/natio/national__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Decide whether it would be better to advertise in a national or a local newspaper.
  - national and international news
  - national and regional politics
  - decisions made at a local rather than national level
  - a national election
  - a coach for the national basketball team
  - The protection of public health is a matter of national security.
  - It is in the national interest to have a public enquiry.
  - The national average is just over two children per family.
  - They are afraid of losing their national identity.
  - These buildings are part of our national heritage.

### [nature](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nature_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈneɪtʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈneɪtʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nat/natur/nature__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nat/natur/nature__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Take time to appreciate the beauties of nature.
  - man-made substances not found in nature
  - A lot of nature conservation is about protecting habitats.

### [nearly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nearly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɪəli/` · **US:** `/ˈnɪrli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nea/nearl/nearly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nea/nearl/nearly__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The bottle's nearly empty.
  - I've worked here for nearly two years.
  - It's nearly time to leave.
  - The audience was nearly all men.
  - He's nearly as tall as you are.
  - They're nearly always late.
  - She very nearly died.
  - It's nearly impossible to get tickets for her gigs.
  - Sales have nearly doubled this year.

### [necessary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/necessary) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnesəsəri/` · **US:** `/ˈnesəseri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nec/neces/necessary__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nec/neces/necessary__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - necessary to do something It may be necessary to buy a new one.
  - necessary for somebody to do something It doesn't seem necessary for us to meet.
  - necessary for something Food is necessary for survival.
  - I'll make the necessary arrangements.
  - Radical change was a necessary step for reform.
  - if necessary If necessary, you can contact me at home.
  - when necessary Only use your car when absolutely necessary.
  - where necessary Changes can easily be made where necessary.
  - He has the expertise necessary to make it work.
  - I was determined not to stay in hospital for any longer than was strictly necessary.

### [neck](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/neck_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nek/` · **US:** `/nek/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nec/neck_/neck__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nec/neck_/neck__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He tied a scarf around his neck.
  - I woke up with a stiff neck.
  - Giraffes have very long necks.
  - She craned (= stretched) her neck to get a better view.
  - He broke his neck in the fall.
  - Somebody's going to break their neck (= injure themselves) on these steps.
  - The hairs on the nape of my neck stood on end.

### [need](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/need_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/niːd/` · **US:** `/niːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nee/need_/need__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nee/need_/need__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to satisfy/meet/fulfil a need
  - need for something There is an urgent need for qualified teachers.
  - a desperate/pressing need for something
  - They identified a need for further training.
  - need for somebody/something to do something There is no need for you to get up early tomorrow.
  - need to do something There's no need to cry (= stop crying).
  - I had no need to open the letter—I knew what it would say.
  - in need of something The house is in need of a thorough clean.
  - We will contact you again if the need arises.

### [neither](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/neither_1) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/ˈniːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnaɪðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nei/neith/neither__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nei/neith/neither__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Neither answer is correct.
  - Neither of them has/have a car.
  - They produced two reports, neither of which contained any useful suggestions.
  - ‘Which do you like?’ ‘Neither. I think they're both ugly.’

### [neither](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/neither_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈniːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnaɪðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nei/neith/neither__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nei/neith/neither__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Neither answer is correct.
  - Neither of them has/have a car.
  - They produced two reports, neither of which contained any useful suggestions.
  - ‘Which do you like?’ ‘Neither. I think they're both ugly.’

### [nervous](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nervous) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɜːvəs/` · **US:** `/ˈnɜːrvəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/ner/nervo/nervous__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/ner/nervo/nervous__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I felt really nervous before the interview.
  - I get so nervous before exams.
  - I was too nervous to say anything.
  - nervous about something Consumers are very nervous about the future.
  - nervous of somebody/something The horse may be nervous of cars.
  - nervous about/of doing something He had been nervous about inviting us.
  - a nervous laugh/glance/smile (= one that shows that you feel anxious)
  - By the time the police arrived, I was a nervous wreck.

### [network](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/network_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnetwɜːk/` · **US:** `/ˈnetwɜːrk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/net/netwo/network__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/net/netwo/network__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a rail/road/canal network
  - The new rail services will form a network connecting the capital and major cities.
  - Europe's mobile phone networks are controlled by five dominant players.
  - network of something a network of veins
  - an extensive network of underground tunnels

### [noise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/noise) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nɔɪz/` · **US:** `/nɔɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/noi/noise/noise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/noi/noise/noise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There was a rattling noise coming from the back of the car.
  - What's that noise?
  - I heard a loud noise and turned around.
  - Don't make a noise.
  - They were making too much noise.
  - I was woken by the noise of a car starting up.
  - There was constant background noise from the motorway.
  - Measures are needed to reduce noise levels.
  - above the noise We had to shout above the noise of the traffic.

### [noisy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/noisy) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɔɪzi/` · **US:** `/ˈnɔɪzi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/noi/noisy/noisy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/noi/noisy/noisy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - noisy children/traffic/crowds
  - He was kept awake by noisy neighbours.
  - a noisy protest (= when people shout)
  - The kids were even noisier than the dogs.
  - The engine is very noisy at high speed.
  - They are a small but noisy pressure group (= they attract attention to their ideas by frequent discussion and argument in public and in the media).

### [none](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/none_1) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/nʌn/` · **US:** `/nʌn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/non/none_/none__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/non/none_/none__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - None of these pens works/work.
  - We have three sons but none of them lives/live nearby.
  - We saw several houses but none we really liked.
  - Tickets for Friday? Sorry we've got none left.
  - He told me all the news but none of it was very exciting.
  - ‘Is there any more milk?’ ‘No, none at all.’
  - (formal) Everybody liked him but none (= nobody) more than I.

### [normal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/normal_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɔːml/` · **US:** `/ˈnɔːrml/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/norma/normal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/norma/normal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - quite/perfectly (= completely) normal
  - Her temperature is normal.
  - They are just quiet, normal people.
  - it is normal (for somebody) to do something It's normal to feel tired after such a long trip.
  - It is now regarded as normal for women to work outside the home.
  - He should be able to lead a perfectly normal life.
  - Divorce is complicated enough in normal circumstances, but this situation is even worse.
  - Under normal circumstances, I would say ‘yes’.
  - In the normal course of events I wouldn't go to that part of town.
  - Statistically, in a normal distribution, 68 per cent of the scores fall fairly close.
  - The unemployment rate was above normal levels.
  - Temperatures are expected to be in the normal range for May.
  - We are open during normal office hours.
  - It is normal practice to inform somebody if you want to leave early.
  - My day began in the normal way, and then I received a very strange phone call.
  - During normal conditions the road is clear of stones and mud.

### [normally](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/normally) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɔːməli/` · **US:** `/ˈnɔːrməli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nor/norma/normally__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nor/norma/normally__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I would never normally discuss this.
  - He didn't eat as much as he normally does.
  - It normally takes 20 minutes to get there.
  - The track is normally used by farmers and their goats.
  - Normally, there was a background hum of noise.
  - They played at venues not normally associated with classical music.
  - to function/operate/develop normally
  - The data were not normally distributed.

### [notice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/notice_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊtɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊtɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/notic/notice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/notic/notice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There was a notice on the board saying the class had been cancelled.
  - A public notice about the planned development was pinned to the wall.

### [notice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/notice_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊtɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊtɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/not/notic/notice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/not/notic/notice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - People were making fun of him but he didn't seem to notice.
  - notice somebody/something The first thing I noticed about the room was the smell.
  - I began to notice something strange.
  - You can't fail to notice the poverty of the region.
  - to barely/hardly/scarcely notice something
  - Most people never notice these details.
  - notice (that)… I couldn’t help noticing (that) she was wearing a wig.
  - He won't even notice I'm gone.
  - notice how, what, etc… Did you notice how Rachel kept looking at her watch?
  - notice somebody/something do something I noticed them come in.
  - notice somebody/something doing something I didn't notice him leaving.

### [novel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/novel_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnɒvl/` · **US:** `/ˈnɑːvl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nov/novel/novel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nov/novel/novel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to write/publish/read a novel
  - detective/historical/romantic novels
  - the novels of Jane Austen
  - Her latest novel is set in Cornwall.
  - a best-selling novel
  - Highsmith's debut novel was a bestseller in 1949.

### [nowhere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nowhere) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnəʊweə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnəʊwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/now/nowhe/nowhere__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/now/nowhe/nowhere__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I had nowhere to live, so I was sleeping on my sister's couch.
  - There was nowhere for me to sit.
  - ‘Where are you going this weekend?’ ‘Nowhere special.’
  - She doesn't want to stay there but she has nowhere else to go.
  - Nowhere do plants flourish with such vigour as they do in tropical rainforests.
  - This animal is found in Australia, and nowhere else.
  - Nowhere is the effect of government policy more apparent than in agriculture.

### [number](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/number_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈnʌmbə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈnʌmbər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/num/numbe/number__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/num/numbe/number__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - number something All the seats in the stadium are numbered.
  - I couldn't work out the numbering system for the hotel rooms.
  - Every paper was carefully numbered and indexed.
  - The coins are individually numbered.
  - Each pigeonhole is clearly numbered by floor and by room.
  - Park your car on the even-numbered side of the street.
  - number something from… to… Number the car's features from 1 to 10 according to importance.
  - number something + noun The doors were numbered 2, 4, 6 and 8.

### [nut](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/nut_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/nʌt/` · **US:** `/nʌt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/n/nut/nut__/nut__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/n/nut/nut__/nut__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to crack a nut (= open it)
  - a cashew nut
  - a hazelnut
  - nuts and raisins
  - chopped nuts
  - She is allergic to nuts.
  - She has a severe nut allergy.
  - a nut roast/cutlet
