### [ocean](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ocean) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/oce/ocean/ocean__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oce/ocean/ocean__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The ship was dredged from the depths of the ocean.
  - People were swimming in the ocean despite the hurricane warning.
  - The plane hit the ocean several miles offshore.
  - Our beach house is just a couple of miles from the ocean.
  - ships large enough to sail the open ocean
  - an ocean liner
  - a species of fish that lives on the ocean floor
  - El Nino is a warm ocean current that appears off the coast of Peru during the winter.
  - Ocean levels are rising.

### [offer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/offer_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒfə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːfər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offer/offer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offer/offer__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to receive a job offer
  - to accept/reject/decline an offer
  - offer of something Thank you for your kind offer of help.
  - I took him up on his offer of a loan.
  - You can't just turn down offers of work like that.
  - an offer of marriage
  - offer to do something I accepted her offer to pay.

### [offer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/offer_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒfə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːfər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offer/offer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offer/offer__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - offer (something) Josie had offered her services as a guide.
  - He offered some useful advice.
  - to offer support/protection/help
  - I don't think they need help, but I think I should offer anyway.
  - offer something for something He offered $4 000 for the car.
  - They are offering a reward for the return of their cat.
  - offer something to somebody They decided to offer the job to Jo.
  - offer somebody something They decided to offer Jo the job.
  - I gratefully took the cup of coffee she offered me.
  - offer somebody something to do something Taylor offered him 500 dollars to do the work.

### [officer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/officer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒfɪsə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːfɪsər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/off/offic/officer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/off/offic/officer__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - army/military/naval officers
  - senior officers in the Royal Air Force
  - The matter was passed on to me, as your commanding officer.
  - He's a retired army intelligence officer.

### [oil](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/oil_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ɔɪl/` · **US:** `/ɔɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/oil/oil__/oil__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/oil/oil__/oil__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Several companies are drilling for oil in the region.
  - The price of crude oil has more than doubled.
  - rising world oil prices
  - an oil spill
  - The country is rich in oil reserves.
  - oil and gas drilling/exploration
  - oil refineries/pipelines
  - oil companies
  - the oil industry
  - The well produced oil at a rate of 2 000 barrels of oil a day.

### [onto](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/onto) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒntu/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːntu/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ont/onto_/onto__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ont/onto_/onto__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Move the books onto the second shelf.
  - She stepped down from the train onto the platform.

### [opportunity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/opportunity_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɒpəˈtjuːnəti/` · **US:** `/ˌɑːpərˈtuːnəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opp/oppor/opportunity__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opp/oppor/opportunity__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't miss this opportunity!
  - opportunity to do something You'll have the opportunity to ask any questions at the end.
  - I'd like to take this opportunity to thank my colleagues for their support.
  - opportunity for somebody to do something The conference offers a unique opportunity for professionals to meet each other.
  - The internship provides an opportunity for students to learn about the challenges involved.
  - opportunity for something There was no opportunity for further discussion.
  - opportunity for doing something There’ll be plenty of opportunity for relaxing once the work is done.
  - opportunity of doing something At least give him the opportunity of explaining what happened.
  - career/employment/job opportunities
  - a business/an investment opportunity
  - He is rude to me at every opportunity (= whenever possible).
  - They intend to close the school at the earliest opportunity (= as soon as possible).
  - I would definitely go if the opportunity arose.
  - a window of opportunity (= a period of time when the circumstances are right for doing something)

### [option](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/option_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɒpʃn/` · **US:** `/ˈɑːpʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/opt/optio/option__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/opt/optio/option__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - As I see it, we have two options…
  - There are various options open to you.
  - to explore/consider/look at all your options
  - We are currently studying all the options available.
  - Going to college was not an option for me.
  - Selling the house was our only option.
  - Waiting a year may be your best option.
  - We felt this was the most viable option.
  - I had no option but to (= I had to) ask him to leave.
  - option of doing something Students have the option of studying abroad in their second year.
  - option of something He was given one month’s imprisonment without the option of a fine.
  - option to do something A savings plan that gives you the option to vary your monthly payments.
  - This particular model comes with a wide range of options (= things you can choose to have when buying something but which you will have to pay extra for).
  - In most cases, I would go for the cheaper option.
  - Farming is not an attractive career option for many young people.
  - The poor villagers are left with no option but to risk their lives.
  - Your doctor will discuss treatment options with you.
  - Failure is not an option.

### [ordinary](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/ordinary) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːdnri/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrdneri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ord/ordin/ordinary__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ord/ordin/ordinary__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an ordinary sort of day
  - in the ordinary course of events
  - ordinary people like you and me

  - She was a perfectly ordinary little girl.
  - They are just ordinary working folk.

### [organization](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/organization) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌɔːɡənaɪˈzeɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌɔːrɡənəˈzeɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/org/organ/organization__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/org/organ/organization__us_1.mp3)

### [organize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/organize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈɔːɡənaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈɔːrɡənaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/org/organ/organize__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/org/organ/organize__us_2.mp3)

  - to organize a protest/demonstration/rally

  - I'll invite people if you can organize food and drinks.
  - She's on the organizing committee for the tournament.

### [original](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/original_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/əˈrɪdʒənl/` · **US:** `/əˈrɪdʒənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ori/origi/original__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ori/origi/original__us_3.mp3)

- **Examples:**

- **UK:** `/ˈaʊtsaɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈaʊtsaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/out/outsi/outside__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/out/outsi/outside__us_2.mp3)

- **UK:** `/ˈʌvn/` · **US:** `/ˈʌvn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/ove/oven_/oven__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/ove/oven_/oven__us_1.mp3)

### [own](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/own_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/əʊn/` · **US:** `/əʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/own/own__/own__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/own/own__/own__us_1.mp3)

### [owner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/owner) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈəʊnə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈəʊnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/o/own/owner/owner__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/o/own/owner/owner__us_1.mp3)

### [particular](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/particular_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈtɪkjələ(r)/` · **US:** `/pərˈtɪkjələr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/par/parti/particular__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/par/parti/particular__us_1.mp3)

  - In this particular case, the owners were not local but Indian.
  - specialized advice tailored to your particular circumstances

### [pass](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pass_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːs/` · **US:** `/pæs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/pass_/pass__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/pass_/pass__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'm not really expecting to pass first time.
  - She passed with flying colours (= very easily).
  - pass something She hasn't passed her driving test yet.
  - pass an exam/examination

### [passenger](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/passenger) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpæsɪndʒə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpæsɪndʒər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/passe/passenger__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/passe/passenger__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - airline/cruise/rail/bus passengers
  - I soon got talking to a fellow passenger on the flight.
  - The boat was carrying more than 100 passengers.
  - a passenger train (= carrying passengers, not goods)

### [past](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/past_4) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/pɑːst/` · **US:** `/pæst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pas/past_/past__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pas/past_/past__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She smiled at me as she walked past.
  - I called out to him as he ran past.

### [patient](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/patient_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpeɪʃnt/` · **US:** `/ˈpeɪʃnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pat/patie/patient__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pat/patie/patient__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cancer/AIDS/heart patients
  - critically ill/elderly patients
  - Hospitals are treating more patients than ever before.
  - patients receiving/undergoing treatment
  - patient with something patients with heart disease
  - patient care/safety

### [pattern](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pattern_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpætn/` · **US:** `/ˈpætərn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pat/patte/pattern__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pat/patte/pattern__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - changing patterns of urban life
  - We have no way of predicting next year's weather patterns.
  - behaviour/sleep/growth patterns
  - stress/intonation/speech patterns
  - Wages in both sectors have followed a similar pattern.
  - The studies show different patterns of distribution.
  - The music contains repeated rhythmic patterns.

### [pay](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pay_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/peɪ/` · **US:** `/peɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pay/pay__/pay__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pay/pay__/pay__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Her job is hard work, but the pay is good.
  - workers on low pay
  - overtime/holiday pay
  - Her monthly take-home pay after taxes is $2 600.
  - the principle of equal pay for equal work
  - Two members of staff have been suspended on full pay.
  - a pay increase/cut
  - (British English) a pay rise
  - (North American English) a pay raise
  - a 3 per cent pay offer
  - The company has imposed a two-year pay freeze.
  - the pay gap between men and women
  - to make a pay claim (= to officially ask for an increase in pay)
  - They demanded improved pay and conditions.

### [peace](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/peace) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/piːs/` · **US:** `/piːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pea/peace/peace__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pea/peace/peace__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - world peace
  - in peace The two communities live together in peace.
  - at peace The countries have been at peace for more than a century.
  - peace talks/negotiations
  - the peace process
  - a peace treaty/accord/agreement/deal
  - peace between A and B The negotiators are trying to make peace between the warring factions.
  - The fragile peace between North and South is threatened.
  - He is critical of US efforts to restore peace to the country.
  - to bring/promote/achieve/ensure peace
  - A UN force has been sent to keep the peace (= to prevent people from fighting).
  - After years of war, the people long for a lasting peace.
  - the peace movement (= that tries to prevent war by protesting, persuading politicians, etc.)
  - the Peace of Utrecht, 1713 (= the agreement ending the war)

### [penny](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/penny_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpeni/` · **US:** `/ˈpeni/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pen/penny/penny__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pen/penny/penny__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He had a few pennies in his pocket.
  - That will be 45 pence, please.
  - They cost 20p each.

### [per](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/per) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/pɜː(r)/` · **US:** `/pɜːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/per__/per__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/per__/per__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Rooms cost £50 per person, per night.
  - 60 miles per hour
  - France consumes around 200 000 tonnes of cocoa per year.
  - This country has a higher crime rate per 100 000 of the population than most other European countries.

### [per cent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/per-cent_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pə ˈsent/` · **US:** `/pər ˈsent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/per_c/per_cent_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/per_c/per_cent_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a 15 per cent rise in price
  - The company announced a 21 per cent increase in profits.
  - House prices dropped 8 per cent last year.
  - Dairy product prices jumped (= increased by)10.4 per cent in April.
  - Shares in the company are up 1.9 percent.

### [per cent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/per-cent_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/pə ˈsent/` · **US:** `/pər ˈsent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/per_c/per_cent_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/per_c/per_cent_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a 15 per cent rise in price
  - The company announced a 21 per cent increase in profits.
  - House prices dropped 8 per cent last year.
  - Dairy product prices jumped (= increased by)10.4 per cent in April.
  - Shares in the company are up 1.9 percent.

### [per cent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/per-cent_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pə ˈsent/` · **US:** `/pər ˈsent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/per_c/per_cent_1_gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/per_c/per_cent_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - per cent of something/somebody Poor families spend about 80 to 90 per cent of their income on food.
  - It is often stated that we use only 10 per cent of our brain.
  - What per cent of the population is/are overweight?
  - Sales increased by 5 percent last year.

### [perform](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/perform) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈfɔːm/` · **US:** `/pərˈfɔːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perfo/perform__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perfo/perform__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to perform a song/dance/play
  - The play was first performed in 2007.
  - Visiting artists will perform their music and poetry.
  - I'm looking forward to seeing you perform.
  - I'd love to see them perform live (= in a live show, not a recording).
  - What do you like about performing on stage?

### [perhaps](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/perhaps) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/præps/` · **US:** `/pərˈhæps/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perha/perhaps__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perha/perhaps__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Are you going to come?’ ‘Perhaps. I'll see how I feel.’
  - Perhaps he's forgotten.

### [permission](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/permission) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈmɪʃn/` · **US:** `/pərˈmɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/permi/permission__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/permi/permission__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - permission for something You must ask permission for all major expenditure.
  - permission to do something After much persuasion, permission was granted to speak to the refugees at the camp.
  - The school has been refused permission to expand.
  - All minors must seek parental permission to marry.
  - permission for somebody/something to do something No official permission has been given for the event to take place.
  - permission from somebody/something You shouldn't rent out your property without seeking permission from your mortgage lender.
  - permission from somebody/something to do something He had to get permission from his parents to audition for the show.
  - without permission She took the car without permission.
  - with somebody's permission (formal) With your permission, I'd like to say a few words.
  - by permission of somebody poems reprinted by kind permission of the author

### [personality](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/personality) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌpɜːsəˈnæləti/` · **US:** `/ˌpɜːrsəˈnæləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/per/perso/personality__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/per/perso/personality__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - His wife has a strong personality.
  - My outgoing personality has definitely helped me succeed.
  - He maintained order by sheer force of personality.
  - normal human personality traits (= qualities), such as shyness
  - Participants identified their own personality type and explored the implications.
  - The characters' names reflect their personalities.
  - There are likely to be tensions and personality clashes in any social group.
  - People’s clothes are often an expression of their personality.

### [pet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pet_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pet/` · **US:** `/pet/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pet/pet__/pet__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pet/pet__/pet__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Do you have any pets?
  - a pet dog/hamster
  - a family pet
  - exotic pets
  - pet food
  - Being a responsible pet owner means caring for your pet.
  - a pet shop (= where animals are sold as pets)
  - Keeping pigs as pets has become extremely popular.

### [petrol](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/petrol) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpetrəl/` · **US:** `/ˈpetrəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pet/petro/petrol__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pet/petro/petrol__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to fill a car up with petrol
  - to run out of petrol
  - The car has a 2-litre petrol engine.
  - the petrol tank of a car
  - An increase in petrol prices will lead to severe difficulties for the company.
  - leaded/unleaded petrol

### [photograph](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/photograph_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfəʊtəɡrɑːf/` · **US:** `/ˈfəʊtəɡræf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pho/photo/photograph__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pho/photo/photograph__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - photograph somebody/something a workshop on photographing wildlife
  - a beautifully photographed book (= with very good photographs in it)
  - photograph somebody/something doing something They were photographed playing with their children.
  - photograph somebody/something + adj. The actor was photographed maskless at the height of the pandemic.

### [physical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/physical_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪzɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪzɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/phy/physi/physical__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/phy/physi/physical__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - physical activity/fitness
  - All subjects undergo a physical examination.
  - physical appearance
  - physical disabilities/injuries
  - The ordeal has affected both her mental and physical health.
  - He tends to avoid all physical contact.
  - The centre offers activities for everyone, whatever your age or physical condition.

### [physics](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/physics) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈfɪzɪks/` · **US:** `/ˈfɪzɪks/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/phy/physi/physics__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/phy/physi/physics__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - quantum/theoretical physics
  - the laws of physics
  - the physics laboratory/department
  - a physics professor/teacher
  - physics of something to study the physics of the electron

### [pick](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pick_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pɪk/` · **US:** `/pɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pic/pick_/pick__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pic/pick_/pick__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - pick somebody/something Pick a number from one to twenty.
  - They are picking the final team this weekend.
  - It's time to pick a side (= decide who you support).
  - He picked his words carefully.
  - At the beach we picked a spot to sit down.
  - pick somebody/something to do something He has been picked to play in this week's game.
  - pick somebody/something for something Have you been picked for the team?
  - pick somebody/something as something It was picked as the best film in the competition.

### [pilot](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pilot_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpaɪlət/` · **US:** `/ˈpaɪlət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pil/pilot/pilot__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pil/pilot/pilot__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an airline pilot
  - a fighter/helicopter pilot
  - The accident was caused by pilot error.
  - The pilot bailed out as the aircraft crashed into the ocean.
  - He has his own plane and a pilot's licence.

### [planet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/planet) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈplænɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈplænɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plane/planet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plane/planet__us_1.mp3)

- **UK:** `/plɑːnt/` · **US:** `/plænt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plant/plant__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plant/plant__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to plant a tree/seed/crop
  - to plant and harvest rice
  - Plant the bulbs in pots for a spring display.

### [plastic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plastic_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈplæstɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈplæstɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plast/plastic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plast/plastic__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**

### [plastic](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plastic_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈplæstɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈplæstɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plast/plastic__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plast/plastic__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The pipes should be made of plastic.
  - a sheet of clear plastic
  - the plastic industry
  - recycled plastic
  - chairs made from plastic
  - dashboards moulded in plastic

### [plate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/plate_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pleɪt/` · **US:** `/pleɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/plate/plate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/plate/plate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - sandwiches on a plate
  - There was an enormous pile of dirty plates in the kitchen sink.
  - The set includes four dinner plates, four side plates and four soup bowls.
  - They filled their plates with spaghetti.

### [platform](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/platform) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈplætfɔːm/` · **US:** `/ˈplætfɔːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pla/platf/platform__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pla/platf/platform__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (British English) What platform does it go from?
  - (British English) The train now standing at platform 1 is for Leeds.
  - The train drew into the platform.
  - The couple met on a train platform.

### [please](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/please_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pliːz/` · **US:** `/pliːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/ple/pleas/please__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/ple/pleas/please__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - please somebody You can't please everybody.
  - Children are usually easy to please.
  - He's a difficult man to please.
  - I was pleased by the results.
  - There's just no pleasing some people (= some people are impossible to please).
  - I did it to please my parents.
  - You must be pleased by their confidence in you.
  - it pleases somebody to do something It pleased him to see the children playing with their gifts.
  - She's always very eager to please.
  - This film is sure to please horror fans.
  - These guys aim to please and they never fail.

### [pleased](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pleased) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pliːzd/` · **US:** `/pliːzd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/ple/pleas/pleased__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/ple/pleas/pleased__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You're coming? I'm so pleased.
  - pleased with somebody/something She was very pleased with her exam results.
  - Mary and I were most pleased with the present we bought in Edinburgh.
  - The boss should be pleased with you.
  - pleased at something She seemed pleased at our success.
  - pleased about something You're looking very pleased about something.
  - pleased for somebody I'm very pleased for you both.
  - pleased (that)… I'm really pleased (that) you're feeling better.

### [pocket](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pocket_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɒkɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈpɑːkɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/poc/pocke/pocket__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/poc/pocke/pocket__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a jacket/coat/shirt/trouser pocket
  - a back/a hip/an inside pocket
  - in/into a pocket I put the note in my pocket.
  - The three of us stood there, hands in pockets, and waited.
  - She reached into her pocket and pulled out her phone.
  - out of/from a pocket Take your hands out of your pockets!
  - Turn out your pockets (= empty your pockets).
  - a pocket dictionary (= one that is small enough to fit in your pocket)

### [polite](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/polite) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈlaɪt/` · **US:** `/pəˈlaɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pol/polit/polite__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pol/polit/polite__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - polite to somebody Please be polite to our guests.
  - Our waiter was very polite and helpful.
  - We were all too polite to object.

### [pollution](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pollution) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈluːʃn/` · **US:** `/pəˈluːʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pol/pollu/pollution__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pol/pollu/pollution__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - air/water pollution
  - to reduce pollution levels
  - new pollution control technologies

### [pop](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pop_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/pɒp/` · **US:** `/pɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pop/pop__/pop__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pop/pop__/pop__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pop song
  - a pop star/singer
  - a pop band/group
  - a pop concert
  - the pop charts

### [pop](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pop_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pɒp/` · **US:** `/pɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pop/pop__/pop__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pop/pop__/pop__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - rock, pop and soul
  - The album covers a range of genres—from mainstream pop to jazz.

### [population](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/population) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌpɒpjuˈleɪʃn/` · **US:** `/ˌpɑːpjuˈleɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pop/popul/population__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pop/popul/population__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - One third of the world’s population consumes/consume two thirds of the world’s resources.
  - The country has a total population of 65 million.
  - The entire population of the town was at the meeting.
  - countries with ageing populations
  - Muslims make up 55 per cent of the population.
  - an increase in population
  - areas of dense/sparse population (= where many/not many people live)
  - The population is increasing at about 6 per cent per year.
  - population growth/density

### [position](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/position_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈzɪʃn/` · **US:** `/pəˈzɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pos/posit/position__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pos/posit/position__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Where would be the best position for the lights?
  - from a/your position From his position on the cliff top, he had a good view of the harbour.
  - She looked down over the scene from a raised position.
  - in a position These plants will grow well in a sheltered position.
  - The hotel stands in an elevated position looking out over the lake.

### [possession](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/possession) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pəˈzeʃn/` · **US:** `/pəˈzeʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pos/posse/possession__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pos/posse/possession__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Prisoners were allowed no personal possessions.
  - One of Jane's most prized possessions was her photo album.
  - The ring is one of her most treasured possessions.
  - Please make sure you have all your possessions with you when leaving the plane.

### [possibility](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/possibility) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌpɒsəˈbɪləti/` · **US:** `/ˌpɑːsəˈbɪləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pos/possi/possibility__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pos/possi/possibility__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Bankruptcy is a real possibility if sales don't improve.
  - What had seemed impossible now seemed a distinct possibility.
  - Reaching the final seems a remote possibility.
  - possibility that… There's a strong possibility that it will rain.
  - There is now no possibility that she will make a full recovery.
  - We cannot exclude the possibility that another person may have been involved.
  - I have considered the possibility that she is dead.
  - possibility of something He refused to rule out the possibility of a tax increase.
  - There is a real possibility of war.
  - The company has raised the possibility of legal action.
  - possibility of doing something I am excited about the possibility of going to the Olympics.
  - with the possibility of something This is a one-year appointment, with the possibility of renewal for an additional year.

### [poster](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/poster) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpəʊstə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpəʊstər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pos/poste/poster__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pos/poste/poster__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - campaign/election posters
  - a poster campaign (= an attempt to educate people about something by using posters)
  - a wanted poster (= with a picture of a person that the police are looking for)
  - They put up a poster advertising the concert.

### [power](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/power_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpaʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈpaʊər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pow/power/power__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pow/power/power__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - power to do something He has the power to make things very unpleasant for us.
  - Religion is losing its power to shape our behaviour.
  - power over somebody/something The aim is to give people more power over their own lives.
  - The government wields enormous power over the economy.
  - in your power She had him completely in her power (= was able to do what she liked with him).
  - In those days the king exercised real political power.

### [predict](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/predict) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈdɪkt/` · **US:** `/prɪˈdɪkt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/predi/predict__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/predi/predict__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - predict something a reliable method of predicting earthquakes
  - Nobody could predict the outcome.
  - to predict the future
  - He predicts a bright future for the business in the next financial year.
  - The party is predicting a majority of 20 seats.
  - predict what, whether, etc… It is impossible to predict what will happen.
  - predict (that)… She predicted (that) the election result would be close.
  - He predicted (that) it would be a smash hit.
  - it is predicted that… It was predicted that inflation would continue to fall.
  - somebody/something is predicted to do something The trial is predicted to last for months.
  - This figure is predicted to rise to one billion by the year 2015.

### [present](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/present_3) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈzent/` · **US:** `/prɪˈzent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/prese/present__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/prese/present__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - present something Are you presenting a paper at the conference?
  - She presented some data on the 2016 presidential election.
  - The paper presents no evidence of a link.
  - The results presented here confirm our prediction.
  - Findings are presented in Table 9.
  - He presents a convincing case.
  - present something to somebody The committee will present its final report to Parliament in June.
  - present something for something Eight options were presented for consideration.

### [president](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/president) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprezɪdənt/` · **US:** `/ˈprezɪdənt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/presi/president__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/presi/president__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Several presidents attended the funeral.
  - the President of the United States
  - President Biden is due to visit the country next month.
  - Do you have any comment, Mr President?
  - France elected a new president in 2017.
  - Ford became president following Nixon's resignation.
  - She should run for president.
  - former South African president, Nelson Mandela
  - as president He was sworn in as President on August 31.

### [prevent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prevent) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪˈvent/` · **US:** `/prɪˈvent/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pre/preve/prevent__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pre/preve/prevent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - prevent something/somebody The accident could have been prevented.
  - Maddie would have joined the army if an injury had not prevented her.
  - The government took steps to prevent a scandal.
  - prevent somebody/something from doing something He is prevented by law from holding a licence.
  - Nothing would prevent him from speaking out against injustice.
  - prevent somebody/something doing something (British English) Nothing would prevent him speaking out against injustice.

### [print](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/print_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prɪnt/` · **US:** `/prɪnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pri/print/print__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pri/print/print__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - print (something) I'm printing a copy of the document for you.
  - Do you want your address printed at the top of the letter?
  - (computing) Click on the icon when you want to print.
  - be printed with something Each card is printed with a different message.

### [printer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/printer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɪntə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈprɪntər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pri/print/printer__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pri/print/printer__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a colour printer
  - a printer cartridge

### [prison](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prison) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɪzn/` · **US:** `/ˈprɪzn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pri/priso/prison__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pri/priso/prison__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She went to prison for tax evasion.
  - He was sent to prison for five years.
  - in prison She is in prison, awaiting trial.
  - in prison for something Her son is in prison for murder.
  - in prison for doing something He served four years in prison for killing a man.
  - out of prison He's just out of prison, after ten years inside.
  - When did she get out of prison?
  - to be released from prison
  - On his release from prison he tried hard to find a decent job.
  - a maximum-security prison
  - (US English) a federal/state prison
  - a prison sentence/term
  - a prison cell
  - the prison population (= the total number of prisoners in a country)
  - (British English) a prison officer
  - (North American English) a prison guard
  - (British English) Ten prison officers and three inmates needed hospital treatment following the riot.

### [prize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/prize_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/praɪz/` · **US:** `/praɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pri/prize/prize__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pri/prize/prize__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize.
  - He won first prize in the woodwind section.
  - The prize went to the grey long-haired cat.
  - prize for something She has received numerous prizes for her work.
  - prize for doing something There are no prizes for guessing (= it is very easy to guess) who she was with.
  - a cash prize of $5 000
  - The grand prize is a family trip to Hawaii.
  - His film took the top prize at Cannes last year.
  - I won £500 in prize money.
  - a prize fund of £20 000
  - Win a car in our prize draw!

### [process](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/process1_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊses/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːses/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/proce/process__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/proce/process__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the consultation/planning process
  - Each time we have to go through the whole decision-making process again.
  - Repeat this process until the entire area is tiled.
  - I'm afraid getting things changed will be a slow process.
  - Coming off the drug was a long and painful (= difficult) process for him.
  - mental/cognitive/thought processes
  - process of doing something They began the difficult process of reforming the education system.
  - process for doing something The company has introduced a new process for handling complaints.
  - Find which food you are allergic to by a process of elimination.
  - in the process of doing something We're in the process of selling our house.
  - in the process I was moving some furniture and I twisted my ankle in the process (= while I was doing it).

### [produce](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/produce_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈdjuːs/` · **US:** `/prəˈduːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/produ/produce__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/produ/produce__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our company mainly produces goods for export.
  - We pride ourselves on producing high-quality products.
  - The local factory produces electronic components.

### [professional](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/professional_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈfeʃənl/` · **US:** `/prəˈfeʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/profe/professional__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/profe/professional__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - professional athletes/players
  - a professional golfer
  - She began her professional career as a dancer 20 years ago.
  - After he won the amateur championship he turned professional.
  - I enjoyed boxing but I was never good enough to go professional.

### [professor](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/professor) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈfesə(r)/` · **US:** `/prəˈfesər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/profe/professor__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/profe/professor__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Professor (Ann) Williams
  - professor of something to be appointed professor of economics at Cambridge
  - He was made (a) professor at the age of 40.

### [profile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/profile_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊfaɪl/` · **US:** `/ˈprəʊfaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/profi/profile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/profi/profile__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We first build up a detailed profile of our customers and their requirements.
  - You can update your Facebook profile (= your description of yourself on a social media website).
  - He fits the profile of the killer.
  - The magazine published a short profile of the new mayor.
  - a job/employee profile
  - His psychological profile is revealing.

### [program](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/program_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊɡræm/` · **US:** `/ˈprəʊɡræm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/progr/programme__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/progr/program__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You'll need to download and install the program on your computer.
  - To run the program, simply click the icon.
  - She uses a computer program to produce these maps.

### [progress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/progress_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprəʊɡres/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːɡrəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/progr/progress__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/progr/progress__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I think we're making progress.
  - We will continue to monitor progress over the next few months.
  - economic/scientific/technological progress
  - rapid/good progress
  - We hope to see some real progress by March.
  - progress in something James is making steady progress in his recovery.
  - progress in doing something Police are making significant progress in fighting computer crime.
  - The two sides have made very slow progress in resolving the dispute.
  - progress on something There's been no sign of progress on this issue.
  - progress towards something The company is making progress towards this target.
  - They asked for a progress report on the building work.

### [promise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/promise_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɒmɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːmɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/promi/promise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/promi/promise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to make/keep/fulfil/break a promise
  - You haven't gone back on your promise, have you?
  - He simply broke every single promise he ever made me.
  - The minister was under attack for a series of broken promises.
  - promise to do something She kept her promise to visit her aunt regularly.
  - He fulfilled a campaign promise to end the war.
  - promise to somebody He had now to deliver on his promises to the Canadian electorate.
  - promise from/by somebody These are empty promises from a party not in a position to honour them.
  - It is unlikely that these laws will be changed, despite promises by the Prime Minister.
  - promise of something The government failed to keep its promise of lower taxes.
  - They were lured by government promises of either money or other benefits.
  - promise (that…) Do I have your promise that you won't tell anyone about this?

### [promise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/promise_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈprɒmɪs/` · **US:** `/ˈprɑːmɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/promi/promise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/promi/promise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘We haven't got time to go to the park.’ ‘But you promised!’
  - promise to do something The college principal promised to look into the matter.
  - ‘Promise not to tell anyone!’ ‘I promise.’
  - They arrived at 7.30 as they had promised.
  - promise something The government has promised a full investigation into the disaster.
  - I'll see what I can do but I can't promise anything.
  - promise (that)… The brochure promised (that) the local food would be superb.
  - promise somebody (that)… You promised me (that) you’d be home early tonight.
  - promise something to somebody He promised the money to his grandchildren.
  - promise somebody something He promised his grandchildren the money.
  - promise yourself something I've promised myself some fun when the exams are over.
  - promise (somebody) + speech ‘I'll be back soon,’ she promised.

### [pronounce](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pronounce) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈnaʊns/` · **US:** `/prəˈnaʊns/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prono/pronounce__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prono/pronounce__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to pronounce a word/syllable/vowel/consonant
  - Very few people can pronounce my name correctly.
  - The ‘b’ in lamb is not pronounced.
  - I found it difficult to pronounce the name of the port.

### [protect](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/protect) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈtekt/` · **US:** `/prəˈtekt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/prote/protect__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/prote/protect__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - protect somebody/something/yourself to protect children/citizens/civilians/consumers/workers
  - Our aim is to protect the jobs of our members.
  - the section of the treaty that protects the rights of employees
  - Each company is fighting to protect its own commercial interests.
  - A majority of Democrats believe that such regulations protect the public.
  - protect somebody/something/yourself against something Troops have been sent to protect aid workers against attack.
  - protect somebody/something/yourself from something They huddled together to protect themselves from the wind.
  - protect against something a paint that helps protect against rust
  - protect from something Vitamin B12 may have a role in protecting from heart disease.

### [provide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/provide) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/prəˈvaɪd/` · **US:** `/prəˈvaɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pro/provi/provide__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pro/provi/provide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - provide something Please provide the following information.
  - The exhibition provides an opportunity for local artists to show their work.
  - She did not provide any evidence to substantiate the claims.
  - The hospital has a commitment to provide the best possible medical care.
  - Please answer questions in the space provided.
  - Refreshments will be provided.
  - provide something for somebody We are here to provide a service for the public.
  - provide somebody with something We are here to provide the public with a service.
  - He provided us with a lot of useful information.
  - provide something to somebody We provide financial support to low-income families.

### [pub](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pub) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/pʌb/` · **US:** `/pʌb/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pub/pub__/pub__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pub/pub__/pub__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We're all going to the pub after work.
  - (informal) They've gone down the pub for a drink.
  - in a pub They spent the whole evening in the pub.
  - at a pub He is one of the regulars at the pub.
  - I got home from the pub just after 11.
  - a pub lunch
  - the landlord of the local pub
  - The Elm Tree will remain a traditional village pub.
  - We are holding a pub quiz in the bar on Tuesday night.

### [public](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/public_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpʌblɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈpʌblɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pub/publi/public__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pub/publi/public__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The campaign is designed to increase public awareness of the issues.
  - Levels of waste from the factory may be a danger to public health.
  - Public safety must come first.
  - Why would the closure of hospitals be in the public interest (= useful to ordinary people)?
  - The government had to bow to public pressure.

### [public](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/public_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpʌblɪk/` · **US:** `/ˈpʌblɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pub/publi/public__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pub/publi/public__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The palace is now open to the public.
  - There have been many complaints from members of the public.
  - The public has/have a right to know what is contained in the report.
  - The campaign aims to educate the public about the dangers of solvent abuse.

### [publish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/publish) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpʌblɪʃ/` · **US:** `/ˈpʌblɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pub/publi/publish__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pub/publi/publish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to publish a book/novel/magazine/paper
  - The first edition was published in 2007.
  - He works for a company that publishes reference books.
  - Most of our titles are also published on CD-ROM.
  - The book was first published in 1960.
  - This essay was originally published in German.
  - Many plays were published anonymously.

### [pull](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/pull_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pʊl/` · **US:** `/pʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pul/pull_/pull__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pul/pull_/pull__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You push and I'll pull.
  - Don't pull so hard or the handle will come off.
  - He got hold of the end and pulled.
  - pull on something I pulled on the rope to see if it was secure.
  - pull at something He pulled at her coat sleeve.
  - pull something Stop pulling her hair!
  - She pulled the cord and the parachute opened.
  - pull somebody/something + adv./prep. She pulled him gently towards her.
  - He smiled and pulled her closer.
  - pull something + adj. Pull the door shut.
  - She pulled the straps tight.

### [purpose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/purpose) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈpɜːpəs/` · **US:** `/ˈpɜːrpəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pur/purpo/purpose__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pur/purpo/purpose__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our campaign's main purpose is to raise money.
  - The plan achieved its primary purpose, if nothing else.
  - The purpose of the book is to provide a complete guide to the university.
  - Giving too much advance notice would defeat the purpose of the inspection.
  - for the purpose of doing something A meeting was called for the purpose of appointing a new treasurer.
  - I moved to London for the sole purpose of working with her.
  - for the purpose of something I have included this data for the purpose of comparison.
  - with the purpose of doing something This project was launched with the purpose of increasing the number of visitors to the region.
  - purpose for something I could find no practical purpose for this app.
  - The space station serves several purposes.
  - The experiments serve no useful purpose (= are not useful).

### [push](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/push_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/pʊʃ/` · **US:** `/pʊʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/p/pus/push_/push__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/p/pus/push_/push__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We pushed and pushed but the piano wouldn't move.
  - Push hard when I tell you to.
  - You push and I'll pull.
  - push at something She pushed at the door but it wouldn't budge.
  - push somebody/something He walked slowly up the hill pushing his bike.
  - Somebody pushed me and I fell over.
  - When you push the gate, it doesn't open.
  - push somebody/something + adv./prep. She pushed the cup towards me.
  - He pushed his chair back and stood up.
  - I tried to hug him but he pushed me away.
  - She pushed her face towards him.
  - push something + adj. I pushed the door open.


## Q

### [quality](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/quality_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkwɒləti/` · **US:** `/ˈkwɑːləti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/q/qua/quali/quality__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/q/qua/quali/quality__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Improving the quality of care for nursing home residents is a priority.
  - When costs are cut product quality suffers.
  - of… quality The soil here is of poor quality.
  - The food is fresh and of good quality.
  - His goal was of top quality.
  - goods of a high quality
  - high-quality goods
  - in quality a decline in water quality
  - We have seen a marked improvement in the quality of service provided.

### [quantity](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/quantity) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkwɒntəti/` · **US:** `/ˈkwɑːntəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/q/qua/quant/quantity__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/q/qua/quant/quantity__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - quantity of something a large/small quantity of something
  - vast/huge quantities of food
  - enormous/massive/considerable/substantial quantities of something
  - in… quantities a product that is cheap to produce in large quantities
  - Lamb is consumed in large quantities in rural areas.
  - in… quantity Is it available in sufficient quantity?

### [queen](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/queen_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/kwiːn/` · **US:** `/kwiːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/q/que/queen/queen_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/q/que/queen/queen__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was crowned queen at the age of fifteen.
  - kings and queens
  - queen of… the Queen of Norway
  - Queen Victoria

### [question](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/question_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkwestʃən/` · **US:** `/ˈkwestʃən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/q/que/quest/question__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/q/que/quest/question__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - question somebody Police are keen to question any witnesses.
  - Two men are being questioned by police.
  - Over half of those questioned said they rarely took any exercise.
  - question somebody about something She was arrested and questioned about the fire.
  - question somebody on something The students were questioned on the books they had been studying.
  - + speech ‘What's wrong?’ she questioned.

### [quietly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/quietly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈkwaɪətli/` · **US:** `/ˈkwaɪətli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/q/qui/quiet/quietly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/q/qui/quiet/quietly__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to ask/speak/talk quietly
  - to move/stand quietly
  - a quietly spoken woman
  - ‘I'm sorry,’ she said quietly.


## R

### [race](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/race_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/reɪs/` · **US:** `/reɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rac/race_/race__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rac/race_/race__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a boat/horse race
  - a five-kilometre road race
  - Who won the race?
  - Shall we have a race to the end of the beach?
  - She ran a good race to finish second.
  - race between A and B a race between the two best runners of the club
  - race against somebody He's already in training for the big race against Bailey.
  - in a race Their horse came third in the race last year.

### [race](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/race_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/reɪs/` · **US:** `/reɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rac/race_/race__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rac/race_/race__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They raced to a thrilling victory in the relay.
  - She'll be racing for the senior team next year.
  - race against somebody/something Who will he be racing against in the next round?
  - race somebody/something We raced each other back to the car.
  - race to do something Television companies are racing to be the first to screen his life story.

### [railway](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/railway) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈreɪlweɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈreɪlweɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rai/railw/railway__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rai/railw/railway__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The railway is still under construction.
  - a disused railway
  - They are doing maintenance work on the railway.
  - Follow the path west along the old railway track.
  - a railway bridge/tunnel

### [raise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/raise_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/reɪz/` · **US:** `/reɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rai/raise/raise__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rai/raise/raise__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She raised the gun and fired.
  - He raised a hand in greeting.
  - She raised her eyes from her work.
  - raise something up He slowly raised his head up.

### [rate](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rate_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/reɪt/` · **US:** `/reɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rat/rate_/rate__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rat/rate_/rate__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - rate (of something) Figures published today show another fall in the rate of inflation.
  - at a/the rate of something Most people walk at an average rate of five kilometres an hour.
  - at a… rate The number of reported crimes is increasing at an alarming rate.
  - rate (that)… At the rate you work, you'll never finish!

### [rather](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/rather_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrɑːðə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈræðər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rat/rathe/rather__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rat/rathe/rather__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - rather odd/strange/unusual
  - a rather large sum of money
  - A rather small number of people turned up.
  - They took a rather different approach.
  - I thought it was a rather good idea.
  - The instructions were rather complicated.
  - She fell and hurt her leg rather badly.
  - I didn't fail the exam; in fact I did rather well!
  - It was a rather difficult question.
  - It was rather a difficult question.
  - He looks rather like his father.
  - The patient has responded to the treatment rather better than expected.
  - He was conscious that he was talking rather too much.

### [reach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reach_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/riːtʃ/` · **US:** `/riːtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rea/reach/reach__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rea/reach/reach__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They didn't reach the border until after dark.
  - The beach can only be reached by boat.
  - I hope this letter reaches you.
  - We finally reached our destination.

### [react](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/react) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/riˈækt/` · **US:** `/riˈækt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rea/react/react__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rea/react/react__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I nudged her but she didn't react.
  - You never know how he is going to react.
  - He would have reacted in the same way.
  - react to something Local residents have reacted angrily to the news.
  - react by doing something The market reacted by falling a further two points.
  - react to something by doing something The army has reacted to the situation by increasing investment in recruitment.
  - react with something Her family reacted with horror when she told them.
  - Residents have reacted with fury to the news.

### [realize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/realize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrɪəlaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈriːəlaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rea/reali/realize__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rea/reali/realize__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - realize (that)… I didn’t realize (that) you were so unhappy.
  - The moment I saw her, I realized something was wrong.
  - I finally came to realize that he would never change.
  - It is important to realize that there are still potential problems.
  - realize how, what, etc… I don't think you realize how important this is to her.
  - They hadn't realized just how much time it would take.
  - realize something Many families fail to realize the importance of a well-balanced diet
  - Only later did she realize her mistake.
  - I hope you realize the seriousness of this crime.
  - The situation was more complicated than they had at first realized.
  - They managed to leave without any of us realizing.
  - it is realized that… There was a cheer when it was realized that everyone was safely back.

### [receive](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/receive) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈsiːv/` · **US:** `/rɪˈsiːv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recei/receive__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recei/receive__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - receive something
  - I was delighted to receive your letter.
  - She received many messages of support.
  - Police had received information about a planned post office robbery.
  - to receive payment/thanks
  - receive something from somebody/something He received an award for bravery from the police.
  - I received a phone call from her a few weeks later.
  - I received a lovely email from Tina.
  - The programme's website received many complaints from listeners.
  - The service receives no money from the local council.

### [recent](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/recent) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈriːsnt/` · **US:** `/ˈriːsnt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recen/recent__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recen/recent__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a recent study/report/survey
  - a recent event/development/discovery
  - The Prime Minister discussed the issue during his most recent visit to Poland.
  - There have been many changes in recent years.
  - in recent months/weeks/days
  - a relatively recent phenomenon
  - It is only in the recent past that these problems have arisen.

### [recently](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/recently) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈriːsntli/` · **US:** `/ˈriːsntli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recen/recently__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recen/recently__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a recently published book
  - a recently released report
  - The company recently announced plans to lay off one-fifth of its workforce.
  - She recently completed a follow-up study of participants.
  - We received a letter from him recently.
  - Until recently they were living in York.
  - I haven't seen them recently (= it is some time since I saw them).
  - Have you used it recently (= in the recent past)?
  - It's only recently that it's become a problem.
  - Her father died recently at age 79.
  - Public meetings were held recently to discuss the proposals.
  - The company has recently signed a deal with a major bank.

### [reception](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/reception) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/rɪˈsepʃn/` · **US:** `/rɪˈsepʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recep/reception__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recep/reception__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Please report to reception on arrival.
  - You can leave a message with reception.
  - the reception area
  - in reception We arranged to meet in reception at 6.30.
  - at reception I've left the keys at reception.
  - on reception I've been on reception (= working there) the whole morning.
  - (North American English, British English) the reception desk

### [recipe](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/recipe) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈresəpi/` · **US:** `/ˈresəpi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recip/recipe__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recip/recipe__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - recipe for something a recipe for chicken soup
  - vegetarian recipes
  - a recipe book
  - You need to follow the recipe carefully.
  - She eventually convinced her mother to share the recipe.
  - This recipe calls for 120g of butter.
  - a collection of 100 delicious recipes from top chefs
  - I tried some recipes from this book.
  - The dish is made to a (= according to) traditional Italian recipe.
  - The jam is based on a 14th century recipe.

### [recognize](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/recognize) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrekəɡnaɪz/` · **US:** `/ˈrekəɡnaɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recog/recognize__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recog/recognize__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - recognize somebody/something I recognized him as soon as he came in the room.
  - Do you recognize this tune?
  - You might not recognize the name but you'll know her face.
  - I recognized the voice immediately.
  - recognize somebody/something by something I recognized her by her red hair.
  - recognize somebody/something from something I recognized him from the photo in the paper.
  - recognize somebody/something as something He recognized the man as one of the police officers.

### [recommend](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/recommend) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌrekəˈmend/` · **US:** `/ˌrekəˈmend/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recom/recommend__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recom/recommend__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - recommend somebody/something The hotel's new restaurant comes highly recommended (= a lot of people have praised it).
  - Can you recommend a good hotel?
  - recommend somebody/something to somebody I recommend the book to all my students.
  - recommend somebody/something for something/somebody She was recommended for the post by a colleague.
  - This game is recommended for children aged 12 and above.
  - recommend somebody/something as something The guidelines recommend low-fat dairy products as excellent sources of calcium

### [record](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/record_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈrekɔːd/` · **US:** `/ˈrekərd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/r/rec/recor/record__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/r/rec/recor/record__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You should keep a record of your expenses.
  - medical/dental records
  - It was the worst flood since records began.
  - according to… records According to official records, there were 21 murders in the city that year.
  - on record Last summer was the wettest on record.
  - people who leave no trace in the historical record (= written documents that provide evidence about the past)

### [record](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/record_2) *(verb)*
