  - They were both soaked to the skin and shivering from cold.
  - He could feel the sun on his skin.
  - the wrinkled skin of his face
  - dead skin cells
  - to have a skin test

### [sky](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sky_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/skaɪ/` · **US:** `/skaɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sky/sky__/sky__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sky/sky__/sky__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The sky suddenly went dark and it started to rain.
  - in the sky What's that in the sky?
  - The sun was shining brightly and there was not a single cloud in the sky.
  - across the sky White clouds moved swiftly across the sky.
  - the night sky
  - a cloudless sky
  - cloudless skies
  - a land of blue skies and sunshine
  - Clear skies could well mean temperatures plummeting tonight.
  - under a sky A crowd had gathered in the square under an overcast sky.
  - meteors falling from the sky
  - sky over/above something The skies above London were ablaze with a spectacular firework display.

### [sleep](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sleep_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sliːp/` · **US:** `/sliːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sle/sleep/sleep__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sle/sleep/sleep__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I need to get some sleep.
  - I didn't get much sleep last night.
  - Can you give me something to help me get to sleep (= start sleeping)?
  - Go to sleep—it's late.
  - His talk nearly sent me to sleep (= it was boring).
  - Try to go back to sleep.
  - in your sleep He cried out in his sleep.
  - Anxiety can be caused by lack of sleep.
  - I only got about five hours' sleep.
  - She was clearly suffering from sleep deprivation.
  - How long have you ever gone without sleep?

### [slowly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/slowly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsləʊli/` · **US:** `/ˈsləʊli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/slo/slowl/slowly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/slo/slowl/slowly__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to move/walk/turn slowly
  - Please could you speak more slowly?
  - The boat chugged slowly along.
  - Don't rush into a decision. Take it slowly.
  - ‘I don't really know,’ Anna said slowly.
  - Slowly things began to improve.
  - The film starts slowly, introducing each of the characters and establishing their relationships.
  - He found that life moved slowly in the countryside.

### [smartphone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smartphone) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsmɑːtfəʊn/` · **US:** `/ˈsmɑːrtfəʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sma/smart/smartphone__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sma/smart/smartphone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**

  - The smartphone supports Wi-Fi networking.

### [smell](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smell_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/smel/` · **US:** `/smel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sme/smell/smell__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sme/smell/smell__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sweet/pleasant smell
  - a foul/pungent/musty smell
  - the air fresheners that we use to cover up unpleasant smells
  - smell of something a faint/strong smell of garlic
  - an acrid smell of smoke
  - There was a smell of burning in the air.
  - I love the smell of freshly baked bread.
  - The air is heavy with the smell of rain and moist vegetation.
  - smell from something The smells from the kitchen filled the room.

### [smell](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smell_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/smel/` · **US:** `/smel/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sme/smell/smell__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sme/smell/smell__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adj. The room smelt damp.
  - Dinner smells good.
  - ‘You smell nice,’ Aidan said with a smile.
  - a bunch of sweet-smelling flowers
  - smell of something His breath smelt of garlic.
  - When he did come home, he smelled of alcohol.
  - The house smelt of cedar wood and fresh polish.
  - smell like something What does the perfume smell like?
  - Hydrogen sulphide smells like rotten eggs.
  - The crisp air smells like clean hay.

### [smile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smile_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/smaɪl/` · **US:** `/smaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/smi/smile/smile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/smi/smile/smile__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a broad/warm/bright/slight smile
  - with a smile ‘Oh, hello,’ he said, with a smile.
  - ‘Well, that's life,’ she said with a wry smile.
  - He greeted me with a big smile.
  - He gave a weak smile as she approached.
  - He had a huge smile on his face.
  - The thought brought a smile to her face.
  - Here's a story that is guaranteed to put a smile on your face.
  - I'm going to wipe that smile off your face (= make you stop thinking this is funny).
  - Her face broke into a smile.
  - Her welcoming smile at the sight of him was a good sign.
  - He allowed himself a small smile of satisfaction.

### [smile](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smile_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/smaɪl/` · **US:** `/smaɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/smi/smile/smile__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/smi/smile/smile__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to smile slightly/broadly
  - to smile sweetly/warmly/brightly
  - to smile weakly/faintly/softly/sadly
  - He never seems to smile.
  - ‘That's easy,’ Elizabeth said, smiling.
  - Looking up from my book, I saw Monica's smiling face.
  - He smiled to think how naive he used to be.
  - smile at somebody/something She smiled at him and he smiled back.
  - She smiled at the thought of finally being able to see him again.
  - I had to smile at (= was amused by) his optimism.
  - Her eyes smiled up at him.
  - smile with/in something He smiled with relief.
  - She smiled in satisfaction.

### [smoke](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smoke_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sməʊk/` · **US:** `/sməʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/smo/smoke/smoke__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/smo/smoke/smoke__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cigarette/tobacco smoke
  - Plumes of black smoke could be seen rising from the area.
  - The explosion sent a huge cloud of smoke into the sky.
  - Clouds of thick black smoke billowed from the car's exhaust.
  - smoke from something His eyes were smarting from the smoke from the fire.
  - The smoke from their cigarettes curled upwards.
  - The majority of people who die in fires die of smoke inhalation.
  - Check your smoke detectors for dead batteries.
  - The witch disappeared in a puff of smoke.
  - I can definitely smell smoke.

### [smoke](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smoke_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sməʊk/` · **US:** `/sməʊk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/smo/smoke/smoke__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/smo/smoke/smoke__us_1.mp3)

  - You see kids openly smoking in the streets.
  - smoke something He was smoking a large cigar.
  - How many cigarettes do you smoke a day?
  - He was still smoking 20 cigarettes a day.
  - An old man smoking a pipe sat at a corner table.
  - to smoke marijuana/cannabis/pot

### [smoking](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/smoking) *(noun)*

  - soldiers in uniform
  - soldiers on duty

### [solution](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/solution) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈluːʃn/` · **US:** `/səˈluːʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/solut/solution__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/solut/solution__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Attempts to find a solution have failed.
  - to propose/offer/provide/develop a solution
  - to look for/seek a solution
  - It was decided that the only solution was for him to leave the company.
  - an easy/innovative solution
  - a possible/practical/long-term solution
  - Do you have a better solution?
  - solution to something There's no simple solution to this problem.
  - the solution to a crisis/an issue
  - Will this lead to a peaceful solution of the conflict?
  - Technology must become part of the solution, rather than part of the problem.

### [solve](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/solve) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/sɒlv/` · **US:** `/sɑːlv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sol/solve/solve__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sol/solve/solve__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Attempts are being made to solve the problem of waste disposal.
  - to solve an issue/a crisis
  - You can't solve anything by just running away.
  - I hope that he can find a good way to solve these problems.

### [somewhere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/somewhere_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌmweə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌmwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/som/somew/somewhere__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/som/somew/somewhere__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I read somewhere that she refuses to do interviews.
  - Can we go somewhere warm?
  - I've already looked there—it must be somewhere else.
  - He went to school in York or somewhere (= I'm not sure where).
  - They live somewhere or other in France.
  - I've seen him somewhere before.
  - I'm sure the information is out there somewhere.

### [somewhere](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/somewhere_2) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌmweə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌmwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/som/somew/somewhere__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/som/somew/somewhere__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We need to find somewhere to live.
  - I know somewhere we can go.

### [sort](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sort_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sɔːt/` · **US:** `/sɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sou/sough/sought__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sor/sort_/sort__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - sort of somebody/something ‘What sort of music do you like?’ ‘Oh, all sorts.’
  - This sort of problem is quite common./These sorts of problems are quite common.
  - He's the sort of person who only cares about money.
  - The sort/sorts of people who are having large families in the US and Ireland are very similar.
  - For dessert there's a fruit pie of some sort (= you are not sure what kind).
  - Most people went on training courses of one sort or another (= of various types) last year.
  - (informal) There were snacks—peanuts, olives, that sort of thing.
  - (informal) There are all sorts of activities (= many different ones) for kids at the campsite.
  - (informal) What sort of price did you want to pay? (= approximately how much)
  - (informal) What sort of time do you call this? (= I'm very angry that you have come so late.)
  - Statements like these are not the sort of stuff you'd expect from one of Australia's most powerful women.
  - (informal) Tracey has been causing her mother all sorts of problems (= many different ones).

### [source](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/source_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/sɔːs/` · **US:** `/sɔːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sou/sourc/source__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sou/sourc/source__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - renewable energy sources
  - These fish are widely used as a food source.
  - source of something Your local library will be a useful source of information.
  - What is their main source of income?
  - Tourism is a major source of revenue for the area.
  - The tiny window was the only source of light.
  - from a source Funding came from a wide variety of sources.

### [speaker](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/speaker) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈspiːkə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈspiːkər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/speak/speaker__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/speak/speaker__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was a guest speaker at the conference.
  - a keynote speaker at the Republican convention
  - She was a brilliant public speaker.
  - The Society has an interesting talk by a visiting speaker every month.
  - Previous speakers include two former US Secretaries of State.
  - He was much in demand as a visiting speaker.
  - The conference featured a number of distinguished speakers from the world of business and industry.
  - speaker on something She is a frequent speaker on human and civil rights.

### [specific](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/specific) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/spəˈsɪfɪk/` · **US:** `/spəˈsɪfɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/speci/specific__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/speci/specific__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - specific needs/requirements
  - patients who suffer from a specific type of cancer
  - The money was collected for a specific purpose.
  - specific problems/issues
  - a specific location/task/case
  - children with specific learning difficulties (= in one area only)

### [speech](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/speech) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/spiːtʃ/` · **US:** `/spiːtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/speec/speech__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/speec/speech__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Several people made speeches at the wedding.
  - She gave a rousing speech to the crowd.
  - speech on something to deliver a speech on human rights
  - speech about something He inspired everyone with a moving speech about tolerance and respect.
  - in a speech In his acceptance speech, the actor thanked his family.

### [speed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/speed_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/spiːd/` · **US:** `/spiːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spe/speed/speed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spe/speed/speed__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at … speed at high/low/full/top speed
  - The five-door version has a maximum speed of 130 mph.
  - a fast/slow speed
  - London traffic moves at an average speed of 11 mph.
  - speed of something travelling at the speed of light/sound
  - a speed of 50 mph/80 kph
  - The missile reaches speeds of 5  800 kilometres per hour.
  - The train began to pick up speed (= go faster).
  - at breakneck speed (= fast in a way that is dangerous)
  - an attempt to break the land speed record
  - It is best not to spray when wind speeds are greater than 5 miles per hour.
  - The car was gathering speed.
  - He reduced speed and turned sharp left.
  - There are speed restrictions on this road.
  - Increasing your walking speed will help to exercise your heart.

### [spider](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/spider) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈspaɪdə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈspaɪdər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spi/spide/spider__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spi/spide/spider__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a poisonous spider
  - She stared in horror at the hairy black spider.
  - In the film, giant spiders attack a town in Arizona.
  - I was bitten by a spider.

### [spoon](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/spoon_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/spuːn/` · **US:** `/spuːn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/spo/spoon/spoon__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/spo/spoon/spoon__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a plastic/metal spoon
  - a soup spoon
  - Wait until the chocolate melts completely, stirring with a spoon.
  - Press the top down gently with the back of a spoon.
  - Thai food is eaten with a spoon.

### [square](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/square_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/skweə(r)/` · **US:** `/skwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/squ/squar/square__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/squ/squar/square__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a square room

### [square](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/square_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/skweə(r)/` · **US:** `/skwer/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/squ/squar/square__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/squ/squar/square__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - First break the chocolate into squares.
  - The floor was tiled in squares of grey and white marble.

### [stage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stage_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/steɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/steɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/stage/stage__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/stage/stage__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at… stage I can't make a decision at this stage.
  - At one stage it looked as though they would win.
  - The product is at the design stage.
  - in… stage This technology is still in its early stages.
  - A new version is in the final stages of testing.
  - stage in something She's reached a crucial stage in her career.
  - stage of something The children are at various stages of development.
  - stage of doing something All babies go through a stage of not wanting to leave their mother.

### [stair](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stair_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/steə(r)/` · **US:** `/ster/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/stair/stair__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/stair/stair__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We had to carry the piano up three flights of stairs.
  - He climbed the stairs to his bedroom.
  - up/down the stairs The children ran up the stairs.
  - on the stairs He remembered passing her on the stairs.
  - at the bottom/top of the stairs

### [stamp](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stamp_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stæmp/` · **US:** `/stæmp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/stamp/stamp__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/stamp/stamp__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a 67p stamp
  - Could I have three first-class stamps, please?
  - He has been collecting stamps since he was eight.
  - a stamp album
  - The Post Office has issued a commemorative stamp to mark the event.

### [star](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/star_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/stɑː(r)/` · **US:** `/stɑːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/star_/star__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/star_/star__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - star in something He starred in that movie about the crazy professor.
  - star with/opposite somebody (in something) She starred opposite Cary Grant in ‘Bringing up Baby’.
  - No one has yet been chosen for the starring role (= the main part).

### [start](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/start_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stɑːt/` · **US:** `/stɑːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/start/start__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/start/start__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - If we don't hurry, we'll miss the start.
  - They haven't won since the start of the season.
  - at the start (of something) Things didn't look too hopeful at the start of the year.
  - from the start We've had problems right from the start.
  - We've had problems from the very start.
  - The trip was a disaster from start to finish.
  - a… start to something a perfect start to the day
  - After a slow start to his career, things improved.
  - The campaign is off to a promising start.
  - a shaky/rocky start
  - (informal) This could be the start of something big.

### [state](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/state_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/steɪt/` · **US:** `/steɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/state/state__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/state/state__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the Baltic States
  - European Union member states

### [stay](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stay_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/steɪ/` · **US:** `/steɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sta/stay_/stay__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sta/stay_/stay__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I enjoyed my stay in Prague.
  - an overnight stay
  - In recent years the average hospital stay for elderly patients has decreased.

### [steal](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/steal_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/stiːl/` · **US:** `/stiːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/ste/steal/steal__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/ste/steal/steal__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I'll report you to the police if I catch you stealing again.
  - steal from somebody/something We found out he'd been stealing from us for years.
  - steal something My wallet was stolen.
  - I had my wallet stolen.
  - Thieves stole jewellery worth over £10 000.
  - steal something from somebody/something He stole a car from the parking lot of a mall.
  - It's a crime to handle stolen goods.
  - He was charged with possession of stolen property.
  - (figurative) to steal somebody’s ideas
  - (figurative) They accused the president of stealing the election (= winning it by cheating).

### [step](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/step_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/step/` · **US:** `/step/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/ste/step_/step__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/ste/step_/step__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We are taking steps to prevent pollution.
  - It's a big step giving up your job and moving halfway across the world.
  - a necessary/an important/a positive step
  - step toward(s) something This was a first step towards a united Europe.
  - This won't solve the problem but it's a step in the right direction.
  - The new drug represents a major step forward in the treatment of the disease.
  - Closing the factory would be a retrograde step.

### [stomach](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stomach_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstʌmək/` · **US:** `/ˈstʌmək/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sto/stoma/stomach__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sto/stoma/stomach__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - stomach pains/cramps
  - an upset stomach
  - (British English also) a stomach upset
  - It's not a good idea to drink (= alcohol) on an empty stomach (= without having eaten anything).
  - You shouldn't exercise on a full stomach.

### [stone](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stone_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stəʊn/` · **US:** `/stəʊn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sto/stone/stone__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sto/stone/stone__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Most of the houses are built of stone.
  - stone walls
  - a stone floor/bridge/carving/pillar/slab
  - a brick and stone building
  - a flight of stone steps
  - a huge block of stone

### [store](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/store_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/stɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sto/store/store__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sto/store/store__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's available at London's three biggest stores.

### [storm](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/storm_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stɔːm/` · **US:** `/stɔːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sto/storm/storm__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sto/storm/storm__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - fierce/heavy/severe/violent storms
  - Winter storms swept the coasts.
  - Her home was hit by two tropical storms.
  - A few minutes later the storm broke (= began).
  - I think we're in for a storm (= going to have one).
  - Off on the horizon, dark grey storm clouds gathered.
  - Insurance companies face hefty payouts for storm damage.

### [straight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/straight_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/streɪt/` · **US:** `/streɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strai/straight__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strai/straight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a straight road
  - long, straight hair (= not curly)
  - a boat sailing in a straight line
  - straight-backed chairs
  - Keep the car straight when you're backing out.

### [straight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/straight_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/streɪt/` · **US:** `/streɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strai/straight__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strai/straight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Keep straight on for two miles.
  - Can you stretch your arms out straighter?
  - He was too tired to walk straight.
  - I can't shoot straight (= accurately).
  - She looked me straight in the eye.

### [strange](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strange) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/streɪndʒ/` · **US:** `/streɪndʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/stran/strange__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/stran/strange__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A strange thing happened this morning.
  - He had a strange feeling that he had seen her somewhere before.
  - This may seem strange but it's the truth.
  - strange (that)… It’s strange (that) we haven’t heard from him.
  - strange how… It's strange how childhood impressions linger.
  - That's strange—the front door's open.
  - There was something strange about her eyes.
  - She was looking at me in a very strange way.
  - I'm looking forward to the exam, strange as it may seem.
  - Strange to say, I don't really enjoy television.

### [strategy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/strategy) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstrætədʒi/` · **US:** `/ˈstrætədʒi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/strat/strategy__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/strat/strategy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the government’s economic strategy
  - We need to devise an effective long-term strategy.
  - Often, individuals employ multiple coping strategies because one particular strategy is not sufficient
  - to adopt/implement/pursue a strategy
  - strategy for doing something to develop a strategy for dealing with unemployment
  - strategy to do something It's all part of an overall strategy to gain promotion.

### [stress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stress_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/stres/` · **US:** `/stres/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/stres/stress__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/stres/stress__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - emotional/mental stress
  - to suffer from stress
  - to relieve/reduce/alleviate stress
  - under stress Things can easily go wrong when people are under stress.
  - The incident has caused enormous stress and anxiety to my family.
  - We all sometimes struggle with the stresses and strains of daily life.
  - How can we help young people cope with the stress of exams?
  - stress-related illnesses
  - stress management (= dealing with stress)
  - The release of the stress hormone cortisol is part of the human stress response.
  - Stress is often a factor in the development of long-term sickness.
  - Few of us can understand the stresses of a job where an everyday mistake can cost lives.

### [stress](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stress_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/stres/` · **US:** `/stres/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/stres/stress__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/stres/stress__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - You stress the first syllable in ‘happiness’.
  - They might (and I stress the word 'might') do the right thing the next time.

### [structure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/structure_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstrʌktʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈstrʌktʃər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/str/struc/structure__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/str/struc/structure__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the structure of the building
  - changes in the economic structure of society
  - Bees have a complex social structure.
  - the grammatical structures of a language
  - the structure of proteins/DNA

### [stupid](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/stupid_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈstjuːpɪd/` · **US:** `/ˈstuːpɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/stu/stupi/stupid__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/stu/stupi/stupid__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a stupid mistake/question/idea
  - It was a pretty stupid thing to do.
  - You've been warned, so don't do anything stupid.
  - I was stupid enough to believe him.
  - It was stupid of you to get involved.
  - That was just plain stupid!
  - We made some incredibly stupid business mistakes.

### [succeed](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/succeed) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səkˈsiːd/` · **US:** `/səkˈsiːd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/succe/succeed__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/succe/succeed__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Our plan succeeded.
  - Whether we succeed or fail depends on ingenuity and luck.
  - succeed in doing something He succeeded in getting a place at art school.
  - I tried to discuss it with her but only succeeded in making her angry (= I failed and did the opposite of what I intended).

### [successful](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/successful) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səkˈsesfl/` · **US:** `/səkˈsesfl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/succe/successful__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/succe/successful__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The experiment was entirely successful.
  - We congratulated them on the successful completion of the project.
  - successful in something He's always been highly successful in his work.
  - successful in something They were successful in winning the contract.
  - successful at something No one can be successful at everything.
  - successful at doing something I wasn't very successful at keeping the news secret.

### [such](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/such) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/sʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/sʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/such_/such__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/such_/such__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This issue was of such importance that we could not afford to ignore it.
  - such a/an… Why are you in such a hurry?
  - It's such a beautiful day!
  - such is something that… (formal) Such is the elegance of this typeface that it is still a favourite of designers.

### [such](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/such) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/sʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/sʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/suc/such_/such__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/suc/such_/such__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This issue was of such importance that we could not afford to ignore it.
  - such a/an… Why are you in such a hurry?
  - It's such a beautiful day!
  - such is something that… (formal) Such is the elegance of this typeface that it is still a favourite of designers.

### [suddenly](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suddenly) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsʌdənli/` · **US:** `/ˈsʌdənli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sud/sudde/suddenly__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sud/sudde/suddenly__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I suddenly realized what I had to do.
  - I suddenly became aware of just how late it was.
  - She took ill and died suddenly at her home.
  - You might suddenly find yourself in dire need of cash.
  - A pedestrian suddenly appeared in front of the taxi.
  - I tried to sit up but I suddenly felt dizzy.

### [suggest](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suggest) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈdʒest/` · **US:** `/səɡˈdʒest/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sug/sugge/suggest__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sug/sugge/suggest__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - suggest something I'd like to suggest a different explanation for the company's decline.
  - suggest something to somebody The designer is known for suggesting innovative ideas to her clients.
  - suggest itself He thought long and hard, but no answer suggested itself.
  - suggest itself to somebody A solution immediately suggested itself to me (= I immediately thought of a solution).
  - suggest (that)… I strongly suggest (that) you don't get involved.
  - suggest doing something I suggested going in my car.
  - it is suggested that… It has been suggested that bright children take their exams early.
  - (British English also) It has been suggested that bright children should take their exams early.
  - + speech ‘We could go for a drive,’ Nate suggested hopefully.

### [suggestion](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suggestion) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈdʒestʃən/` · **US:** `/səɡˈdʒestʃən/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sug/sugge/suggestion__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sug/sugge/suggestion__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Can I make a suggestion?
  - Do you have any suggestions?
  - I would like to offer a suggestion.
  - He rejected my suggestion as impractical.
  - suggestion for something The report offers suggestions for improvement to policy.
  - I'd like to hear your suggestions for ways of raising money.
  - suggestion on something We welcome any comments and suggestions on these proposals.
  - suggestion about something Are there any suggestions about how best to tackle the problem?
  - suggestion that… He agreed with my suggestion that we should change the date.
  - We are open to suggestions (= willing to listen to ideas from other people).
  - We need to get it there by four. Any suggestions?

### [suit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suit_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/suːt/` · **US:** `/suːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sui/suit_/suit__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sui/suit_/suit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She was wearing a grey business suit.
  - He is dressed in a formal black suit.
  - They won't let men into the restaurant without a suit and tie.
  - a two-/three-piece suit (= of two/three pieces of clothing)
  - a pinstripe suit

### [support](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/support_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpɔːt/` · **US:** `/səˈpɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppo/support__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppo/support__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - support for something There is strong public support for the policy.
  - There is a continued lack of support for the war.
  - His ideas are gaining popular support (= the support of many people).
  - to provide/offer support
  - I intend to lend my full support to the campaign.
  - to win/gain somebody's support
  - Can I rely on your support (= will you vote for me) in the election?
  - support from somebody/something The policy has broad support from industry.
  - in support of something Only a few people spoke in support of the proposal.
  - organizations that provide advice and support to small businesses

### [support](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/support_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpɔːt/` · **US:** `/səˈpɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppo/support__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppo/support__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - support somebody/something to support an idea/a view
  - The party fully supports new welfare reform bill.
  - Efforts to reduce waste are strongly supported by environmental groups.
  - The majority of ordinary citizens did not support the war.
  - If you raise it at the meeting, I'll support you.
  - support somebody/something in something The government supported the unions in their demand for a minimum wage.

### [suppose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/suppose) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpəʊz/` · **US:** `/səˈpəʊz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sup/suppo/suppose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sup/suppo/suppose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Getting a visa isn't as simple as you might suppose.
  - Prices will go up, I suppose.
  - suppose (that)… I don't suppose for a minute that he'll agree (= I'm sure that he won't).
  - It's reasonable to suppose that he left the note after he tried to call her.
  - Why do you suppose he resigned?
  - I suppose all the tickets have been sold now, have they?
  - There is no reason to suppose she's lying.
  - I suppose you think it's funny, do you (= showing anger)? ‘That’ is nearly always left out, especially in speech.

### [sure](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sure_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ʃɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/ʃʊr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/sure_/sure__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/sure_/sure__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - ‘Will you get me my coat?’ ‘Sure, where is it?’
  - Did it hurt? Sure it hurt.
  - Sure, no problem.

### [surprise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surprise_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpraɪz/` · **US:** `/sərˈpraɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprise__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - What a nice surprise!
  - I have a surprise for you!
  - a surprise attack
  - There are few surprises in this year's budget.
  - It comes as no surprise to learn that they broke their promises.
  - Her letter came as a complete surprise.
  - ‘The appointment came as a pleasant surprise to me,’ she says.
  - The announcements came as something of a surprise to them.
  - There are lots of surprises in store for visitors to the gallery.
  - He was there on a surprise visit to the troops.
  - Life’s full of surprises.
  - She likes springing surprises on people.
  - Visitors to the gallery are in for a few surprises.

### [surprise](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surprise_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpraɪz/` · **US:** `/sərˈpraɪz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprise__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprise__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - surprise somebody It wouldn't surprise me if they got married soon.
  - surprise somebody how, what It's always surprised me how popular he is.
  - it surprises somebody that… It surprises me that you've never sung professionally.
  - It will surprise no one to hear that the aubergine is my favourite vegetable.
  - it surprises somebody to do something Would it surprise you to know that I'm thinking of leaving?

### [surprised](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surprised) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpraɪzd/` · **US:** `/sərˈpraɪzd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprised__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprised__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a surprised look
  - She looked surprised when I told her.
  - surprised at/by somebody/something I was surprised at how quickly she agreed.
  - I'm surprised at you, behaving like that in front of the kids.
  - surprised to see, hear, learn, discover, find They were surprised to find that he'd already left.
  - surprised (that)… You shouldn’t be surprised (that) he didn’t come.
  - Don't be surprised if I pretend not to recognize you.
  - ‘Will she cancel the party?’ ‘I wouldn't be surprised.’
  - You’d be surprised how many people voted for him.
  - He was pleasantly surprised to discover that he was no longer afraid.
  - to be a little/a bit/quite surprised
  - She seemed somewhat surprised at his reaction.

### [surprising](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/surprising) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/səˈpraɪzɪŋ/` · **US:** `/sərˈpraɪzɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprising__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surpr/surprising__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It’s not surprising (that) they lost.
  - The frequency of such bad weather is hardly surprising given how far north we are.
  - A surprising number of people came.
  - We had a surprising amount in common.
  - It's surprising what people will do for money.
  - It’s surprising how many people applied for the job.

### [survey](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/survey_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɜːveɪ/` · **US:** `/ˈsɜːrveɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sur/surve/survey__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sur/surve/survey__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A recent survey showed 75 per cent of those questioned were in favour of the plan.
  - The survey revealed/found that…
  - to conduct/carry out/do/complete a survey
  - According to the survey, many young adults have experimented with drugs of some kind.
  - Another national survey conducted last year found the same result.
  - Roughly half of survey respondents express strong interest in additional education.
  - The results of the survey come as no surprise to us.

### [sweet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sweet_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/swiːt/` · **US:** `/swiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/swe/sweet/sweet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/swe/sweet/sweet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a cup of hot sweet tea
  - sweet food
  - I had a craving for something sweet.
  - roast duck with a sweet and sour sauce
  - sweet sherry
  - This wine is too sweet for me.
  - I found the dessert a bit sweet for my taste.

### [sweet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/sweet_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/swiːt/` · **US:** `/swiːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/swe/sweet/sweet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/swe/sweet/sweet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a packet of sweets
  - a sweet shop

### [symbol](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/symbol) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪmbl/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪmbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sym/symbo/symbol__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sym/symbo/symbol__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The vase is decorated with religious symbols.
  - symbol of something The new school stands as a symbol of hope for a better future.
  - Mandela became a symbol of the anti-apartheid struggle.

### [system](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/system) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈsɪstəm/` · **US:** `/ˈsɪstəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/s/sys/syste/system__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/s/sys/syste/system__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - reform of the country's education system
  - the criminal justice system
  - the healthcare system
  - the legal/financial/political system
  - system for doing something Systems are in place for dealing with complaints.
  - system of something a system of government


## T

### [tablet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tablet) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtæblət/` · **US:** `/ˈtæblət/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tab/table/tablet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tab/table/tablet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The company has launched its latest 10-inch tablet.
  - Consumers show a growing preference for tablets and smartphones over PCs and laptops.

### [talk](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/talk_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɔːk/` · **US:** `/tɔːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tal/talk_/talk__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tal/talk_/talk__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I went to several interesting talks at the conference.
  - talk on something She gave a talk on her visit to China.
  - talk about something I heard a talk about the history of printing.

### [target](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/target_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɑːɡɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈtɑːrɡɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tar/targe/target__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tar/targe/target__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to meet/hit a target
  - The firm is on track to achieve its growth target for the year.
  - The university will reach its target of 5 000 students next September.
  - The department has missed its sales target for the third month running.
  - Set yourself targets that you can reasonably hope to achieve.
  - on target The new sports complex is on target to open in June.
  - off target These figures are way off target.
  - a target date of April 2022
  - a target audience/market (= the particular audience, area etc. that a product, programme, etc. is aimed at)
  - The film's target demographic is women aged 18–49 years.
  - We're using social media to communicate with our target customers.

### [task](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/task_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɑːsk/` · **US:** `/tæsk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tas/task_/task__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tas/task_/task__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to accomplish/perform/undertake/complete a task
  - a difficult/a daunting/an impossible task
  - Getting hold of this information was no easy task (= was difficult).
  - a thankless task (= an unpleasant one that nobody wants to do and nobody thanks you for doing)
  - The first task for the new leader is to focus on the economy.
  - The new role involves a variety of specific tasks.
  - task of doing something Detectives are now faced with the task of identifying the body.
  - task of something The government now has to take on the task of reconstruction of the country.
  - We should stop chatting and get back to the task at hand.
  - You need to concentrate on the task in hand.
  - It was a challenge to adapt this novel for the screen, but the writer proved himself equal to the task.

### [taste](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/taste_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/teɪst/` · **US:** `/teɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tas/taste/taste__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tas/taste/taste__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a sweet/salty/bitter/sour taste
  - I don't like the taste of olives.
  - This dish has an unusual combination of tastes and textures.
  - The soup has very little taste.

### [taste](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/taste_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/teɪst/` · **US:** `/teɪst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tas/taste/taste__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tas/taste/taste__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - + adj. to taste good/delicious/sweet
  - taste like something This drink tastes like sherry.
  - taste of something The ice tasted of mint.

### [teaching](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/teaching) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtiːtʃɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈtiːtʃɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tea/teach/teaching__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tea/teach/teaching__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She wants to go into teaching (= make it a career).
  - He has now retired from full-time teaching
  - The course combines classroom teaching and distance learning.
  - How much teaching do you actually do?
  - the teaching profession
  - teaching staff
  - traditional/innovative teaching methods
  - Applicants must hold a recognized teaching qualification.

### [technology](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/technology) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tekˈnɒlədʒi/` · **US:** `/tekˈnɑːlədʒi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tec/techn/technology__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tec/techn/technology__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - science and technology
  - advances in communications technology
  - advanced/modern technology
  - to develop and use new technologies
  - small businesses that are involved with emerging technologies
  - the use of the latest digital technology to create a virtual world
  - This technology enables computers to read handwriting.

### [teenage](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/teenage) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtiːneɪdʒ/` · **US:** `/ˈtiːneɪdʒ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tee/teena/teenage__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tee/teena/teenage__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a teenage girl/boy
  - teenage angst
  - teenage pregnancy
  - teen magazines

### [temperature](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/temperature) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtemprətʃə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtemprətʃʊr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tem/tempe/temperature__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tem/tempe/temperature__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - high/low temperatures
  - cold/warm temperatures
  - a drop/rise in temperature
  - The temperature has risen (by) five degrees.
  - Some places have had temperatures in the 40s (= over 40° centigrade).
  - at a temperature Chemical reactions take place more slowly at low temperatures.
  - the water/air/surface temperature
  - Heat the oven to a temperature of 200°C (= degrees centigrade).
  - The climate is generally mild, and freezing temperatures are almost unknown.

### [term](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/term_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɜːm/` · **US:** `/tɜːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/ter/term_/term__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/ter/term_/term__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a technical/legal/generic term
  - a glossary of terms
  - a term of abuse
  - What is the meaning of the term ‘globalization’?
  - William Gibson coined the term (= invented the term) ‘cyberspace’ in 1984.
  - term for something ‘Old man’ is a slang term for ‘father’.
  - The term ‘Generation X’ refers to the people born in the 1950s and 1960s.
  - ‘Register’ is the term commonly used to describe different levels of formality in language.
  - I would describe myself, for want of a better term, as a nerd.

### [text](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/text_2#text_sng_9) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tekst/` · **US:** `/tekst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tex/text_/text__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tex/text_/text__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - text (somebody) Text me when you're on your way.
  - Kids seem to be texting non-stop these days.
  - text somebody something I'll text you the final score.

### [themselves](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/themselves) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/ðəmˈselvz/` · **US:** `/ðəmˈselvz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/the/thems/themselves__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/the/thems/themselves__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They seemed to be enjoying themselves.
  - The children were arguing amongst themselves.
  - They've bought themselves a new car.

### [thick](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thick_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/θɪk/` · **US:** `/θɪk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thi/thick/thick__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thi/thick/thick__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a thick slice of bread
  - a thick book (= one that has a lot of pages)
  - a thick coat (= one made of heavy cloth)
  - thick fingers
  - Everything was covered with a thick layer of dust.
  - Use fairly thick wads of newspaper.
  - He wore thick glasses.
  - a thick carpet of snow

### [thief](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thief) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/θiːf/` · **US:** `/θiːf/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thi/thief/thief__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thi/thief/thief__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a car/jewel thief
  - a gang of thieves
  - Thieves stole £70 000 worth of jewellery from his home.
  - a petty thief (= who steals items of quite low value)
  - It is the fourth time the shop has been targeted by thieves.

### [thin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thin_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/θɪn/` · **US:** `/θɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thi/thin_/thin__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thi/thin_/thin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Cut the vegetables into thin strips.
  - A number of thin cracks appeared in the wall.
  - The body was hidden beneath a thin layer of soil.
  - Palm trees cast long, thin shadows on the lawn.
  - He knew that the ice was too thin to risk crossing the river.
  - a thin blouse (= of light cloth)

### [thinking](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thinking_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈθɪŋkɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈθɪŋkɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thi/think/thinking__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thi/think/thinking__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I had to do some quick thinking.
  - She believes in encouraging creative thinking in the classroom.
  - He had some serious thinking to do.
  - With good, clear thinking one can arrive at an answer.

### [third](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/third_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/θɜːd/` · **US:** `/θɜːrd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thi/third/third__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thi/third/third__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He divided the money into thirds.
  - third of something Over a third of sales were made over the internet.
  - Two thirds of the population live in the poorer south.

### [thought](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/thought) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/θɔːt/` · **US:** `/θɔːt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tho/thoug/thought__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tho/thoug/thought__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - thought of doing something I couldn't bear the thought of waiting any longer.
  - thought of something/somebody The very thought of it makes me feel sick.
  - thought of somebody/something doing something I don't like the thought of you walking home alone.
  - thought (that)… She was struck by the sudden thought that he might already have left.
  - thought on something Thank you for sharing your thoughts on this topic.
  - I'd like to hear your thoughts on the subject.
  - thought about something He shared his thoughts about the future of technology.
  - at the thought of something He shuddered at the thought of going to the dentist.
  - He often expresses his thoughts on Twitter.
  - I've just had a thought (= an idea).
  - Would Mark be able to help? It's just a thought.
  - ‘Why don't you try the other key?’ ‘That's a thought!’
  - Just then a worrying thought came to me.
  - Suddenly a thought struck him—he didn't even know her name.
  - I must admit, the thought crossed my mind too.
  - Negative thoughts can be a symptom of depression.

### [throw](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/throw_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/θrəʊ/` · **US:** `/θrəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/thr/throw/throw__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/thr/throw/throw__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Practise throwing and catching.
  - They had a competition to see who could throw the furthest.
  - throw something Who threw that rock?
  - throw something + adv./prep. Stop throwing stones at the window!
  - She threw the ball up and caught it again.
  - He threw the ball into the bushes and the dog ran to fetch it.
  - Three grenades were thrown into the corridor.
  - Two petrol bombs had been thrown through the front window.
  - throw something to somebody Don't throw it to him; give it to him!
  - throw somebody something Can you throw me that towel?

### [tidy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tidy_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaɪdi/` · **US:** `/ˈtaɪdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tid/tidy_/tidy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tid/tidy_/tidy__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a tidy desk
  - She keeps her flat very tidy.
  - I like everything to be neat and tidy.
  - The room was clean and tidy.
  - The place has never looked so tidy.

### [tidy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tidy_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaɪdi/` · **US:** `/ˈtaɪdi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tid/tidy_/tidy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tid/tidy_/tidy__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I spent all morning cleaning and tidying.
  - tidy something to tidy a room
  - I've tidied the bedroom and the bathroom.

### [tie](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tie_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/taɪ/` · **US:** `/taɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tie/tie__/tie__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tie/tie__/tie__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He was dressed impeccably in a suit and tie.
  - They prefer their employees to wear a shirt and tie.
  - a striped silk tie

### [tie](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tie_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/taɪ/` · **US:** `/taɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tie/tie__/tie__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tie/tie__/tie__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tie something + adv./prep. She tied the newspapers in a bundle.
  - He had to tie her hands together.
  - They tied him to a chair with cable.
  - I tie back my hair when I'm cooking.
  - The box was tied with plastic string.
  - His hands were tied behind his back.
  - tie something Shall I tie the package or tape it?

### [tip](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tip_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɪp/` · **US:** `/tɪp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tip/tip__/tip__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tip/tip__/tip__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - tip on/for doing something handy tips for buying a computer
  - tip on/for something useful tips on how to save money
  - (British English, informal) Here are my top tips for interview success.
  - The blog offered various beauty tips.
  - His money tips and tricks could save a typical family up to £6 000 a year.

### [tool](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tool_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tuːl/` · **US:** `/tuːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/too/tool_/tool__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/too/tool_/tool__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - garden tools
  - a cutting tool
  - Always select the right tool for the job.
  - craftsmen using traditional tools
  - a set of tools

### [top](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/top_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/tɒp/` · **US:** `/tɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/top/top__/top__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/top/top__/top__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He lives on the top floor.
  - She kept her passport in the top drawer.
  - The score is in the top left-hand corner of the screen.

### [top](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/top_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɒp/` · **US:** `/tɑːp/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/top/top__/top__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/top/top__/top__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - at the top of something She was standing at the top of the stairs.
  - The title is right at the top of the page.
  - at the top Write your name at the top.
  - to the top He filled my glass to the top.
  - to the top of something We climbed to the very top of the hill.
  - on the top Snow was falling on the mountain tops.
  - on the top of something He was completely bald on the top of his head.
  - I was out of breath when I reached the top.
  - (British English) the top of the milk (= the cream that rises to the top of a bottle of milk)
  - The wind was blowing in the tops of the trees.

### [touch](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/touch_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/tʌtʃ/` · **US:** `/tʌtʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tou/touch/touch__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tou/touch/touch__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't touch that plate—it's hot!
  - Can you touch your toes? (= bend and reach them with your hands)
  - touch somebody on something I touched him lightly on the arm.
  - touch somebody/something with something Do not touch anything with your bare hands.
  - He has hardly touched the ball all game.
  - (figurative) I must do some more work on that article—I haven't touched it all week.

### [tour](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tour_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/tʊr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tou/tour_/tour__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tou/tour_/tour__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a walking/sightseeing/bus tour
  - tour of/round/around something a coach tour of northern France
  - They embarked on a tour around the country.
  - a tour operator (= a person or company that organizes tours)

### [tourism](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tourism) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɔːrɪzəm/` · **US:** `/ˈtʊrɪzəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tou/touri/tourism__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tou/touri/tourism__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The area is heavily dependent on tourism.
  - the tourism industry
  - The tourism sector is expected to grow by 9.7 percent per annum.

### [towards](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/towards) *(preposition)*
- **UK:** `/təˈwɔːdz/` · **US:** `/tɔːrdz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tow/towar/towards__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tow/towar/towards__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - They were heading towards the German border.
  - She had her back towards me.

### [towel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/towel_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaʊəl/` · **US:** `/ˈtaʊəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tow/towel/towel__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tow/towel/towel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Help yourself to a clean towel.
  - a hand/bath towel (= a small/large towel)
  - a beach towel (= a large towel used for lying on in the sun)
  - I got out of the pool and wrapped my towel around me.
  - Pat the area dry with a clean towel.

### [tower](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tower_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtaʊə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtaʊər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tow/tower/tower__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tow/tower/tower__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a bell tower
  - the Eiffel Tower
  - a 19-storey office tower
  - The castle is rectangular in shape, with a tower at each corner.

### [toy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/toy_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/tɔɪ/` · **US:** `/tɔɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/toy/toy__/toy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/toy/toy__/toy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a toy car
  - toy soldiers

### [toy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/toy_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/tɔɪ/` · **US:** `/tɔɪ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/toy/toy__/toy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/toy/toy__/toy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - cuddly/stuffed toys
  - The children were playing happily with their toys.
  - a toy shop/store
  - She's an entrepreneur and a designer of children's toys.

### [track](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/track_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/træk/` · **US:** `/træk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/track/track__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/track/track__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - railway/railroad tracks
  - We crossed the rail/train track.
  - India has thousands of miles of track.
  - Many branch lines were closed, and the tracks lifted.

### [tradition](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/tradition) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/trəˈdɪʃn/` · **US:** `/trəˈdɪʃn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/tradi/tradition__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/tradi/tradition__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - religious/cultural/ancient traditions
  - This region is steeped in tradition.
  - a tradition of something The company has a long tradition of fine design.
  - The company has continued its tradition of innovation.
  - The British are said to love tradition (= to want to do things in the way they have always been done).
  - They broke with tradition (= did things differently) and got married quietly.
  - by tradition By tradition, children play tricks on 1 April.
  - according to tradition According to tradition, a tree grew on this spot.
  - There's a tradition in our family that we have a party on New Year's Eve.
  - in the tradition of somebody He's a politician in the tradition of (= similar in style to) Kennedy.
  - a native culture expressed almost wholly through an oral tradition (= that is only spoken and not written down)
  - He is motivated by a family tradition of charity and good works.
  - his country's rich folk traditions
  - His work has earned him a central place in the African American literary tradition.

### [traditional](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/traditional) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/trəˈdɪʃənl/` · **US:** `/trəˈdɪʃənl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/tradi/traditional__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/tradi/traditional__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - traditional dress/music/art/culture/dance
  - It's traditional in America to eat turkey on Thanksgiving Day.
  - traditional values/practices/beliefs
  - Acupuncture has long been a part of traditional Chinese medicine.
  - Janet makes cheese by traditional methods.
  - Cinema poses a threat to traditional art forms.

### [train](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/train_2) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/treɪn/` · **US:** `/treɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/train/train__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/train/train__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - train somebody/something highly trained professionals
  - badly trained staff
  - Specially trained coaches will provide support at no extra charge.
  - He is well trained to complete any mission that he is given.
  - train somebody/something to do something They train dogs to sniff out drugs.
  - The crew are well trained to deal with emergencies.
  - We trained our sales force to be sensitive to the needs of customers.
  - train somebody as/in/for something The students are being trained for careers in big business.
  - train as/in/for something He trained as a teacher before becoming an actor.
  - All members of the team have trained in first aid.
  - train to do/be something Sue is training to be a doctor.

### [trainer](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/trainer) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtreɪnə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtreɪnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/train/trainer__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/train/trainer__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a pair of trainers

### [training](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/training) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtreɪnɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈtreɪnɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/train/training__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/train/training__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - staff training
  - training in something Few candidates had received any training in management.
  - training in doing something She has some training in dealing with children with emotional problems.
  - training for somebody The company failed to provide adequate training for staff.
  - Volunteers will undergo intensive training.
  - a training course/session/programme

### [transport](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/transport_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtrænspɔːt/` · **US:** `/ˈtrænspɔːrt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/trans/transport__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/trans/transport__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - air/freight/road transport
  - the government’s transport policy
  - The region has good transport links.
  - We need a reliable transport network.
  - Modern transport systems have been a severe threat to the environment.
  - Good public transport infrastructure is urgently required.

### [traveller](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/traveller) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtrævələ(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈtrævələr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tra/trave/traveller__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tra/trave/traveller__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She is a frequent traveller to Belgium.
  - leisure/business travellers
  - These hotels are becoming popular with the discerning traveller.

### [trouble](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/trouble_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtrʌbl/` · **US:** `/ˈtrʌbl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tro/troub/trouble__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tro/troub/trouble__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We have trouble getting staff.
  - to make/cause/spell trouble for somebody
  - He could make trouble for me if he wanted to.
  - Sorry, I didn't mean to cause any trouble.
  - Falling shark numbers could spell (= cause) big trouble for ocean ecosystems.
  - trouble with somebody/something The trouble with you is you don't really want to work.
  - We've never had much trouble with vandals around here.
  - Her trouble is she's incapable of making a decision.
