  - The trouble is (= what is difficult is) there aren't any trains at that time.
  - The only trouble is we won't be here then.
  - No, I don't know his number—I have quite enough trouble remembering my own.
  - financial troubles
  - She was on the phone for an hour telling me her troubles.
  - Our troubles aren't over yet.
  - Finance was the least of his troubles.
  - There was trouble brewing (= a problem was developing) among the workforce.

### [truck](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/truck_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/trʌk/` · **US:** `/trʌk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/tru/truck/truck__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/tru/truck/truck__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a truck driver
  - a 10-ton truck
  - Scott got a full time job driving a truck.

### [twin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/twin_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/twɪn/` · **US:** `/twɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/twi/twin_/twin__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/twi/twin_/twin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a twin brother/sister
  - twin daughters/sons
  - twin boys/girls

### [twin](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/twin_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/twɪn/` · **US:** `/twɪn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/twi/twin_/twin__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/twi/twin_/twin__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She's expecting twins.
  - If you're carrying (= expecting) twins or triplets, that can put you at risk.
  - I'm 5 months pregnant with twins.

### [typical](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/typical) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈtɪpɪkl/` · **US:** `/ˈtɪpɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/t/typ/typic/typical__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/t/typ/typic/typical__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a typical Italian cafe
  - This is a typical example of Roman pottery.
  - Judy is a typical American teenager.
  - typical of somebody/something This meal is typical of local cookery.
  - typical for somebody/something The weather at the moment is not typical for July.


## U

### [underground](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/underground_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌndəˈɡraʊnd/` · **US:** `/ˌʌndərˈɡraʊnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/under/underground__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/under/underground__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an underground bunker/tunnel
  - underground parking
  - an underground car park
  - underground passages/caves/streams
  - underground cables
  - They carried out a series of underground nuclear tests.

### [underground](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/underground_2) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌndəˈɡraʊnd/` · **US:** `/ˌʌndərˈɡraʊnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/under/underground__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/under/underground__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Rescuers found victims trapped several feet underground.
  - animals that live underground
  - toxic waste buried deep underground
  - a network of pipelines running underground

### [understanding](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/understanding_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌndəˈstændɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˌʌndərˈstændɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/und/under/understanding__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/und/under/understanding__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Students will gain a broad understanding of the workings of Parliament.
  - a deep/clear understanding of something
  - Unions said her comments showed a complete lack of understanding of what the civil service does.
  - She possesses a thorough understanding of the nature of the threat we are faced with.
  - The book aims to inform readers seeking a greater understanding of the issues involved.
  - The committee has little or no understanding of the problem.
  - to develop/improve/increase understanding of something
  - to contribute to/lead to an understanding of something
  - Most of the students have a sound understanding of English grammar.
  - The existence of God is beyond human understanding (= humans cannot know whether God exists or not).

### [unfortunately](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unfortunately) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈfɔːtʃənətli/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈfɔːrtʃənətli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unf/unfor/unfortunately__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unf/unfor/unfortunately__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Unfortunately, I won't be able to attend the meeting.
  - I can't make it, unfortunately.
  - unfortunately for somebody/something Unfortunately for him, the police had been informed and were waiting outside.
  - It won't be finished for a few weeks. Unfortunately!
  - We wanted to have only films from Spain at this year's festival. Unfortunately, however, there weren't enough good ones.

### [unhappy](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unhappy) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈhæpi/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈhæpi/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unh/unhap/unhappy__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unh/unhap/unhappy__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to feel/look/seem/sound/become unhappy
  - an unhappy marriage/childhood
  - I didn't realize but he was deeply unhappy at that time.
  - This story has an unhappy ending.
  - unhappy to do something It makes me very unhappy to see you so miserable.

### [uniform](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/uniform_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːnɪfɔːm/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːnɪfɔːrm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uni/unifo/uniform__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uni/unifo/uniform__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The hat is part of the school uniform.
  - a military uniform
  - Do you have to wear uniform?
  - in (a) uniform He was dressed in the uniform of a Royal Navy officer.
  - soldiers in uniform
  - a police/nurse's uniform
  - an army uniform
  - a soldier out of uniform
  - the uniform of the Parachute Regiment
  - He quickly put on his uniform.

### [unit](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unit) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːnɪt/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːnɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uni/unit_/unit__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uni/unit_/unit__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - After 1946 the British Government treated the four territorial divisions as a single unit.
  - The basic unit of society is the family.
  - The cell is the unit of which all living organisms are composed.
  - the role of the family unit in the community

### [united](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/united) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/juˈnaɪtɪd/` · **US:** `/juˈnaɪtɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/uni/unite/united__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/uni/unite/united__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the United States of America
  - efforts to build a united Europe
  - a politically united federation

### [unusual](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/unusual) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ʌnˈjuːʒəl/` · **US:** `/ʌnˈjuːʒəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/unu/unusu/unusual__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/unu/unusu/unusual__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The case is highly unusual.
  - Police then took the unusual step of publishing the names and pictures of the two suspects.
  - it is unusual for somebody/something to do something It's not unusual for young doctors to work a 70-hour week (= it happens often).
  - It's unusual for the trees to flower so early.
  - it is unusual to do something It was a bit unusual to see her up and about before nine in the morning.
  - She has a very unusual name.
  - She hadn't noticed anything unusual about Susan's behaviour.

### [upstairs](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/upstairs_2) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˌʌpˈsteəz/` · **US:** `/ˌʌpˈsterz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/ups/upsta/upstairs__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/ups/upsta/upstairs__us_4.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - an upstairs room/window/bathroom
  - Bob looked up and saw the light in the upstairs bedroom.
  - She rents a small upstairs apartment in a family's house.
  - The restaurant has a great upstairs bar with very comfortable seating.

### [use](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/use_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/juːs/` · **US:** `/juːs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/use/use__/use__gb_4.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/use/use__/use__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - A ban was imposed on the use of chemical weapons.
  - You can't justify the use of military force.
  - I'm not sure that this is the most valuable use of my time.
  - for… use These videos are intended for classroom use.
  - for use in something The software is designed for use in schools.
  - in use The chapel was built in the 12th century and is still in use today.
  - for the use of somebody (formal) The bar is for the use of members only.

### [used to](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/used-to) *(modal verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːst tu/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːst tu/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/use/used_/used_to_1_gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/use/used_/used_to_1_us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I used to live in London.
  - That's what my father always used to say.
  - I didn't use to like him much when we were at school.
  - Didn't you use to have long hair?
  - I used to think that it would be a great idea to live there.
  - We used to go sailing on the lake in summer.
  - She used to work for a large insurance company.
  - I used to play a lot of football.
  - You used to see a lot of her, didn't you?

### [user](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/user) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːzə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːzər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/use/user_/user__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/use/user_/user__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - road users
  - The service allows users to download and read a wide collection of books for no charge.
  - computer/internet/mobile phone users
  - wheelchair users
  - Service users can now get help 365 days a year.
  - Our advice to companies and home users is to waste no time in implementing this security patch.
  - a site with more than 2 million registered users
  - Many of the centre's regular users were mothers with young children.
  - The average user spends 40 minutes a day on unnecessary email.
  - We're always working to improve the user experience.
  - We're expecting the user base to expand significantly.
  - Although the sites don't charge user fees, some make money by selling ads.
  - The site enables users to access census data.
  - The service is designed to let users share large files.
  - user of something The main users of this service are government departments.

### [usual](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/usual) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈjuːʒəl/` · **US:** `/ˈjuːʒəl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/u/usu/usual/usual__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/u/usu/usual/usual__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - This is the usual way of doing it.
  - He came home later than usual.
  - He didn't sound like his usual happy self.
  - She sat in her usual seat at the back.
  - They asked me why I wanted the job and why I thought I was suitable—you know, the usual thing.
  - You need to enter all the usual stuff—name, address, credit card details.
  - Our usual practice is to ask for references from previous employers.
  - She made all the usual excuses.
  - usual for somebody/something This weather is not usual for this time of year.
  - it is usual to do something It is usual to start a speech by thanking everybody for coming.
  - it is usual for somebody/something to do something It is not usual for a head of government to attend a meeting like this.
  - usual in somebody/something This kind of behaviour is far from usual in children of this age.


## V

### [valley](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/valley) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvæli/` · **US:** `/ˈvæli/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/val/valle/valley__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/val/valle/valley__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in a valley In the valley below cows were grazing peacefully.
  - the fertile soils of river valleys
  - A stream runs the length of the valley floor.
  - the Shenandoah Valley
  - the valley of the Nile
  - a small town set in a valley
  - The cottage was in a wooded valley.
  - The road climbs up the side of the valley.

### [van](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/van) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/væn/` · **US:** `/væn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/van/van__/van__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/van/van__/van__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the driver of a white van
  - a police van (= for carrying police officers or prisoners)
  - A delivery van was almost blocking the narrow street.
  - an ice-cream van
  - a van driver
  - in a van A passenger in the van was fatally injured.
  - He usually sleeps in the back of his van.

### [variety](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/variety_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/vəˈraɪəti/` · **US:** `/vəˈraɪəti/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/var/varie/variety__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/var/varie/variety__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - There is a wide variety of patterns to choose from.
  - He resigned for a variety of reasons.
  - This tool can be used in a variety of ways.
  - The text consists of extracts from a variety of sources.
  - articles and essays on a variety of topics
  - I don't eat meat, only fish, but there's a good variety available here.
  - The day-long excursion is one of a variety on offer for tourists.
  - I was impressed by the variety of dishes on offer.

### [vehicle](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/vehicle) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈviːəkl/` · **US:** `/ˈviːhɪkl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/veh/vehic/vehicle__gb_5.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/veh/vehic/vehicle__us_5.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - motor vehicles (= cars, buses, lorries, etc.)
  - Are you the driver of this vehicle?
  - The police think that he was driving a stolen vehicle.
  - rows of parked vehicles

### [view](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/view_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/vjuː/` · **US:** `/vjuː/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vie/view_/view__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vie/view_/view__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - view of something There were magnificent views of the surrounding countryside.
  - Most rooms enjoy panoramic views of the ocean.
  - view from something The view from the top of the tower was spectacular.
  - a sea/mountain view
  - I'd like a room with a view.

### [virus](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/virus) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈvaɪrəs/` · **US:** `/ˈvaɪrəs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/vir/virus/virus__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/vir/virus/virus__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the flu/influenza virus
  - patients known to have been infected with the virus
  - the human immunodeficiency virus or HIV
  - the hepatitis C virus
  - a highly infectious disease caused by a virus
  - a virus infection
  - The vaccination offers good protection against the virus.
  - It is not clear how she contracted the virus.

### [voice](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/voice_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/vɔɪs/` · **US:** `/vɔɪs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/v/voi/voice/voice__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/v/voi/voice/voice__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I could hear voices in the next room.
  - He recognized Sarah's voice.
  - in a… voice to speak in a deep/soft/loud voice
  - ‘I promise,’ she said in a small voice (= a quiet, shy voice).
  - in somebody's voice There was a note of concern in his voice.
  - He never raised his voice (= spoke loudly in an angry way) to the children.
  - to lower your voice (= to speak more quietly)
  - Keep your voice down (= speak quietly).
  - Don't take that tone of voice with me!
  - The men turned around at the sound of my voice.
  - Her voice shook with emotion.
  - ‘There you are,’ said a voice behind me.
  - When did his voice break (= become deep like a man's)?
  - Her voice sounded familiar.
  - He was suffering from flu and had lost his voice (= could not speak).
  - She has a good singing voice.
  - She was in good voice (= singing well) at the concert tonight.


## W

### [wait](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wait_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/weɪt/` · **US:** `/weɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wai/wait_/wait__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wai/wait_/wait__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The wait seemed interminable.
  - It took six months for the house to be finished, but it was worth the wait.
  - wait for somebody/something We had a long wait for the bus.
  - He now faces an agonizing two-month wait for the test results.
  - We had a half-hour wait to see the doctor.
  - wait of… There could be a wait of four months or more.

### [war](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/war) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɔː(r)/` · **US:** `/wɔːr/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/war/war__/war__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/war/war__/war__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the Second World War
  - the threat of (a) nuclear war
  - to win/lose a/the war
  - war between A and B the war between England and Scotland
  - war with somebody England's war with Scotland
  - war against somebody The war against Spain lasted 18 years.
  - war on somebody It was the year Britain declared war on Germany.
  - (formal) In the Middle Ages England waged war on France.
  - He returned home to the US at the end of the war.
  - Social and political problems led to the outbreak (= the beginning) of war.
  - Where were you living when war broke out?
  - The government does not want to go to war (= start a war) unless all other alternatives have failed.
  - The two countries fought a short but bloody war.
  - My grandfather fought in the war.
  - Her husband was killed during the war.
  - at war How long have they been at war?
  - a war hero/veteran
  - More troops are being despatched to the war zone.
  - (formal) the theatre of war (= the area in which fighting takes place)

### [wash](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wash_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɒʃ/` · **US:** `/wɑːʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/was/wash_/wash__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/was/wash_/wash__us_1_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - These towels are ready for a wash.
  - I'll just have a quick wash before dinner.
  - The curtains could do with a good (= thorough) wash.
  - I'm doing a dark wash (= washing all the dark clothes together).
  - Your shirt's in the wash (= being washed or waiting to be washed).
  - My sweater shrank in the wash.
  - That blouse shouldn't look like that after only two washes.

### [washing](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/washing) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɒʃɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈwɑːʃɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/was/washi/washing__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/was/washi/washing__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a gentle shampoo for frequent washing
  - I do the washing (= wash the clothes) in our house.

### [wave](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wave_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/weɪv/` · **US:** `/weɪv/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wav/wave_/wave__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wav/wave_/wave__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Huge waves were breaking on the shore.
  - Surfers flocked to the beach to ride the waves.
  - the gentle sound of waves lapping
  - The wind made little waves on the pond.
  - in the waves Children were playing in the waves.
  - on the waves Seagulls bobbed on the waves.

### [weak](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weak) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/wiːk/` · **US:** `/wiːk/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wea/weak_/weak__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wea/weak_/weak__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - She is still weak after her illness.
  - His legs felt weak.
  - She suffered from a weak heart.
  - weak with/from something I was exhausted and weak with hunger.

### [web](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/web) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/web/` · **US:** `/web/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/web/web__/web__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/web/web__/web__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to surf/browse/search the web
  - The service is available to anyone with access to the web.
  - on the Web I found the information on the Web.
  - I did a Web search to find the best price.
  - a web browser/server

### [wedding](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wedding) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwedɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈwedɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wed/weddi/wedding__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wed/weddi/wedding__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - I dreamed of having a big wedding, with all my family and friends.
  - I'm going to a wedding this weekend.
  - Have you been invited to their wedding?
  - television coverage of the royal wedding
  - at a wedding I met her at my brother's wedding.
  - wedding to somebody Over 300 guests attended his wedding to his childhood sweetheart.
  - a wedding ceremony/reception
  - She looked beautiful on her wedding day.
  - a wedding present/gift
  - All her friends could hear wedding bells (= they thought she would soon get married).

### [weight](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/weight_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/weɪt/` · **US:** `/weɪt/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wei/weigh/weight__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wei/weigh/weight__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - in weight It is about 76 kilos in weight.
  - by weight Bananas are sold by weight.
  - weight of… In the wild, this fish can reach a weight of 5lbs.
  - She is trying to lose weight (= become less heavy and less fat).
  - He’s put on/gained weight (= become heavier and fatter) since he gave up smoking.
  - No more for me. I have to watch my weight.
  - He has now shed the extra weight.
  - Body fat increases rapidly as body weight increases.
  - The point is to achieve permanent weight loss.
  - Stress can cause weight gain.
  - Sam has a weight problem (= is too fat).

### [welcome](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/welcome_4) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · **US:** `/ˈwelkəm/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wel/welco/welcome__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - to get/receive a welcome
  - Thank you for your warm welcome.
  - The winners were given an enthusiastic welcome when they arrived home.
  - He returned to a hero’s welcome.
  - a speech/smile of welcome
  - She was asked to say a few words of welcome.
  - I wish to extend a very warm welcome to the team.
  - welcome to something It was a nice welcome to a strange new city.

### [wet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wet_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/wet/` · **US:** `/wet/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wet/wet__/wet__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wet/wet__/wet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - wet clothes/hair/grass
  - You'll get wet (= in the rain) if you go out now.
  - Try not to get your shoes wet.
  - His face was wet with tears.
  - We were all soaking wet (= extremely wet).
  - Her hair was still dripping wet.
  - My shirt was wet through (= completely wet).

### [wheel](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wheel_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wiːl/` · **US:** `/wiːl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whe/wheel/wheel__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whe/wheel/wheel__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He braked suddenly, causing the front wheels to skid.
  - the rear wheels of the car
  - She was killed when she was crushed under the wheels of a bus.
  - I had lightweight wheels on my bike.
  - tractors with powered front wheels
  - on wheels One of the boys was pushing the other along in a little box on wheels.

### [while](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/while_1) *(conjunction)*
- **UK:** `/waɪl/` · **US:** `/waɪl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/whi/while/while__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/whi/while/while__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - We must have been burgled while we were asleep.
  - Her parents died while she was still at school.
  - While I was waiting at the bus stop, three buses went by in the opposite direction.
  - You can go swimming while I'm having lunch.
  - shoes mended while you wait

### [whole](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whole_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/həʊl/` · **US:** `/həʊl/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/who/whole/whole__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/who/whole/whole__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Let's forget the whole thing.
  - Jenna was my best friend in the whole world.
  - It seems I've spent my whole life travelling.
  - The whole family will be there.
  - But that's not the whole story, is it?
  - He spent the whole day writing.
  - We drank a whole bottle each.
  - I went through the whole process with them.
  - My whole body ached.
  - The whole country (= all the people in it) mourned her death.
  - The school is keen to involve the whole community in this project.
  - She wasn't telling the whole truth.

### [whose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whose) *(determiner)*
- **UK:** `/huːz/` · **US:** `/huːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/who/whose/whose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/who/whose/whose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Whose house is that?
  - I wonder whose this is.

### [whose](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/whose) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/huːz/` · **US:** `/huːz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/who/whose/whose__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/who/whose/whose__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Whose house is that?
  - I wonder whose this is.

### [wide](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wide_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/waɪd/` · **US:** `/waɪd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wid/wide_/wide__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wid/wide_/wide__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It's a wide, fast-flowing river.
  - The river gets quite wide here.
  - He wore a jacket with wide lapels.
  - Her face broke into a wide grin.

### [wild](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wild_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/waɪld/` · **US:** `/waɪld/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wil/wild_/wild__gb_2.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wil/wild_/wild__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - wild animals/birds/flowers
  - wild salmon
  - wild mushrooms/rice
  - The plants grow wild along the banks of rivers.
  - Their aim is to document wild populations of fast-disappearing species.
  - a wild boar

### [wind](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wind1_1#wind1_sng_1) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪnd/` · **US:** `/wɪnd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/wind_/wind__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/wind_/wind__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - strong/high winds
  - gale-force winds
  - a light wind
  - a north/south/east/west wind
  - westerly/easterly winds
  - a chill/cold/biting wind from the north
  - gusty/howling winds
  - The wind is blowing from the south.
  - in the wind The trees were swaying in the wind.
  - A gust of wind blew my hat off.
  - The weather was hot, without a breath of wind.
  - The wall gives some protection from the prevailing wind (= the direction the wind blows from most often).
  - The wind is getting up (= starting to blow strongly).
  - The wind has dropped (= stopped blowing strongly).
  - wind speed/direction
  - renewable energies like solar and wind power

### [winner](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/winner) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɪnə(r)/` · **US:** `/ˈwɪnər/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/win/winne/winner__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/win/winne/winner__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The lucky winner gets an all-expenses-paid trip to Sydney.
  - an award winner
  - a medal/prize winner
  - a raffle/jackpot winner
  - the winner of a prize/an award
  - The winners of the competition will be announced next month.
  - There are no winners in a divorce (= everyone suffers).
  - They were decisively beaten by the eventual winners, Spain.
  - A panel of celebrity judges will pick the winners.
  - Several factors make the difference between winners and losers.
  - the 2018 World Cup winners
  - Johnson rode his 48th winner of the year.
  - Labour was the clear winner in Scotland.

### [wish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wish_2) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wɪʃ/` · **US:** `/wɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wis/wish_/wish__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wis/wish_/wish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Give my good wishes to the family.
  - wish for something We all send our best wishes for the future.
  - With best wishes (= for example, at the end of a letter)

### [wish](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wish_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/wɪʃ/` · **US:** `/wɪʃ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wis/wish_/wish__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wis/wish_/wish__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - wish (that)… I wish I were taller.
  - (British English also) I wish I was taller.
  - I wish I hadn't eaten so much.
  - ‘Where is he now?’ ‘I only wish I knew!’
  - I wish you wouldn't leave your clothes all over the floor.
  - She really wished she’d stayed on at college.
  - He sat by the phone, wishing it would ring. ‘That’ is nearly always left out, especially in speech.
  - wish somebody/something/yourself + adj. He's dead and it's no use wishing him alive again.
  - wish somebody/something/yourself + adv./prep. She wished herself a million miles away.

### [wood](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wood) *(noun)*
- **UK:** `/wʊd/` · **US:** `/wʊd/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/woo/wood_/wood__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/woo/wood_/wood__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - He chopped some wood for the fire.
  - a piece of wood
  - a plank /block of wood
  - All the furniture was made of wood.
  - The house had dark wood floors.
  - furniture made of a variety of different woods
  - In the spring, cut out the old wood and shorten the young stems (= of bushes, etc.).

### [wooden](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wooden) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʊdn/` · **US:** `/ˈwʊdn/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/woo/woode/wooden__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/woo/woode/wooden__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - a wooden box/door/floor
  - This is a large double bedroom with polished wooden floorboards.
  - Behind the house was a small wooden hut.

### [working](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/working_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwɜːkɪŋ/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːrkɪŋ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worki/working__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worki/working__us_3.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - the working population
  - working people/mothers/families

### [worried](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worried) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʌrid/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːrid/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worri/worried__gb_3.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worri/worried__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't look so worried!
  - worried about somebody/something I'm not worried about her—she can take care of herself.
  - Doctors are worried about the possible spread of the disease.
  - worried about doing something People are really worried about losing their jobs.
  - worried for somebody/something He is extremely worried for her safety.
  - I was worried for my family.
  - worried by something We're not too worried by these results.
  - worried (that)… The police are worried that the man may be armed.
  - I was worried you wouldn't come.
  - Where have you been? I've been worried sick (= extremely worried).
  - Try not to get worried.
  - His worried parents were waiting for him when he got home.
  - She gave me a worried look.

### [worry](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worry_1) *(verb)*
- **UK:** `/ˈwʌri/` · **US:** `/ˈwɜːri/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worry/worry__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worry/worry__us_2.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Don't worry. We have plenty of time.
  - Stop worrying, Dad—it'll be fine.
  - worry about somebody/something Don't worry about me. I'll be all right.
  - He's always worrying about his weight.
  - Never mind—it's nothing to worry about.
  - That's not something you need to worry about.
  - worry about doing something You can stay here tonight, so you don't have to worry about walking home in the dark.
  - worry over somebody/something There's no point in worrying over things you can't change.
  - worry for somebody/something She's my sister, and I worry for her safety.
  - She began to worry for her friend.
  - worry (that)… I worry that I won't get into college.

### [worse](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worse_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːs/` · **US:** `/wɜːrs/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worse/worse__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worse/worse__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - The rooms were awful and the food was worse.
  - The weather got worse during the day.
  - I've been to far worse places.
  - worse than something The film was no worse than many others he made in the 1930s.
  - The interview was much worse than he had expected.
  - The area seemed almost worse than the city he had left.
  - worse than doing something There's nothing worse than going out in the cold with wet hair.
  - worse for somebody/something It's much worse for the parents than it is for the child.

### [worst](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/worst_1) *(adjective)*
- **UK:** `/wɜːst/` · **US:** `/wɜːrst/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wor/worst/worst__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wor/worst/worst__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - It was by far the worst speech he had ever made.
  - It's not the worst idea I've ever heard.
  - What's the worst thing that could happen?
  - What she said confirmed my worst fears (= proved they were right).
  - This is every parent's worst nightmare.

### [wow](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/wow_1) *(exclamation)*
- **UK:** `/waʊ/` · **US:** `/waʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/w/wow/wow__/wow__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/w/wow/wow__/wow__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Wow! You look terrific!


## Y

### [yet](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yet_1) *(adverb)*
- **UK:** `/jet/` · **US:** `/jet/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/yet/yet__/yet__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/yet/yet__/yet__us_1.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - (British English) I haven't received a letter from him yet.
  - (North American English) I didn't receive a letter from him yet.
  - not yet ‘Are you ready?’ ‘No, not yet.’
  - We don't yet know what really happened.
  - have yet to do something We have yet to decide what action to take (= We have not decided what action to take).

### [yours](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/yours) *(pronoun)*
- **UK:** `/jɔːz/` · **US:** `/jʊrz/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/y/you/yours/yours__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/y/you/yours/yours__us_2_rr.mp3)
- **Examples:**
  - Is that book yours?
  - Is she a friend of yours?
  - My hair is very fine. Yours is much thicker.


## Z

### [zero](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/definition/english/zero_1) *(number)*
- **UK:** `/ˈzɪərəʊ/` · **US:** `/ˈzɪrəʊ/` · [🔊 UK Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/uk_pron/z/zer/zero_/zero__gb_1.mp3) | [🔊 US Audio](https://www.oxfordlearnersdictionaries.com/media/english/us_pron/z/zer/zero_/zero__us_1_rr.mp3)
